- **T09-F04 — Medium [code observation]: UX exposes permanent deletion to users who can only receive a denial.** The component offers the hard-delete request (`SkillsManager.jsx:121`) without a super-admin visibility check, while the API correctly returns 403 (`admin.js:740`). Category-fetch failures are also swallowed (`SkillsManager.jsx:54`), making outage look like no suggestions. Show permitted soft-hide actions, protected deletion, and explicit category-load errors.

### Expansion scenario

Curators in France and Haiti add differently worded labels for the same plumbing capability, while a North American team deprecates an outdated skill used by thousands of helpers. A six-million-user matching system needs to retain referenced IDs and map aliases consistently. Two curators editing category/visibility must not lose one another's work. A regional skill may require local qualifications, but engineering should link to the existing license model and jurisdiction-reviewed eligibility policy rather than make unverified legal claims from a category name.

### Exact recommended controls and UX

Create a concept-detail screen around the existing skill ID: localized labels, aliases, references, owner, revision, and deprecation destination. Show affected profile/job/business counts before hiding, merging, or deleting. Require expected version and publish review for large taxonomy changes. Use scoped curator permissions distinct from broad `manage_operations`; allow country-specific applicability while preserving global concept identity. Hard deletion requires zero validated references and policy-approved retention; prefer reversible deprecation. Provide a bounded asynchronous import/merge workflow with dry run, validation errors, resume, and complete audit evidence.

### Dependencies / owner

Marketplace Taxonomy owns concepts and aliases; Search/Matching owns query semantics and index plans; IAM owns curator delegation; Localization owns translation review; Legal/Compliance reviews qualification applicability.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

Clearing category persists correctly. Duplicate creates return explicit conflict or approved merge preview. Two stale edits cannot overwrite silently. Five-language labels and aliases resolve to one stable concept ID. Referenced skills remain resolvable after deprecation and cannot be permanently purged accidentally. A 20,000-entry isolated import exposes every validation outcome and resumes safely. Scoped curators cannot alter global/foreign policy; all edit dialogs and category-error states pass keyboard and screen-reader checks.

## 10. Support Tickets — /admin/support

### Mission / what exists

Support Tickets is a real inbox with status/priority/ownership/search filters, claim/release, public replies, internal notes, realtime updates, availability, statistics, and ticket deep links. It links to existing agent onboarding, shifts, knowledge base, templates, metrics, SLA, performance, CSAT, QA, escalation, hierarchy, settings, and audit pages. Public request and reply paths already support request/message idempotency. Migration 600 protects terminal ticket transitions. An enterprise audit should strengthen this ecosystem's ownership and queue semantics instead of proposing that the company build an entirely new support suite.

### Evidence

UI tools: `client/src/admin/pages/SupportTickets.jsx:52`; fetch/realtime: `:576`, `:634`. API grants: `server/routes/admin.js:267`, `:274`. Server list/claim/reply: `server/controllers/supportController.js:407`, `:529`, `:689`. New-ticket fanout: `:46`, `:224`. Terminal integrity: `server/migrations/600_support_ticket_terminal_integrity.sql:5`. Existing request/reply regressions: `server/__tests__/supportRoutes.test.js:219`, `:243`; idempotency coverage: `server/__tests__/adminMessageIdempotency.test.js:197`.

### Findings

- **T10-F01 — High [confirmed defect]: competing ticket claims can both succeed and overwrite ownership.** Claim reads status/assigned_to, checks authorization, then performs an UPDATE constrained only by ID (`supportController.js:532`, `:546`). Two authorized admins reading an unassigned ticket can both pass; the later write replaces the first owner. Release has a similar read/check/write structure (`:575`). Reuse the stronger conversation-claim locking approach in the Live Support subsystem; require an atomic owner/version predicate and a typed 409.
- **T10-F02 — High [confirmed defect]: the inbox does not navigate the server's bounded result set.** List supports limit/offset with default 50 (`supportController.js:415`). The component sends only search/status/priority/assigned (`SupportTickets.jsx:583`) and exposes no list pagination state in this load path. Operators see a total exceeding reachable rows. The sequence guard already prevents stale response replacement; extend it with stable server cursors and queue-wide summary semantics.
- **T10-F03 — High [code observation]: ticket notifications and reloads fan out to all administrators.** Every new ticket emails every admin/super-admin and broadcasts an admin event (`supportController.js:46`, `:224`). The recipient query does not require an active support assignment or active account. Each subscribed inbox reloads both list and stats on every event (`SupportTickets.jsx:634`). An assumed 100-ticket burst with 200 open inboxes generates up to 20,000 reload triggers, with overlapping requests despite the stale-response guard. Scope notifications and coalesce reloads; this is workload arithmetic, not measured production load.
- **T10-F04 — Medium [design requirement]: global ticket grants do not support regional ownership delegation.** `manage_support` controls read/claim/status, while reply uses `message_users`. Existing ownership checks and separate grants are useful, but country/team responsibility, absence coverage, and supervisor reassignment need explicit task-scoped contracts. Integrate existing shifts, hierarchy, and SLA systems rather than claiming those capabilities are absent.

### Expansion scenario

After a payment incident, thousands of customers submit tickets while shifts hand off between New York, Paris, and Cairo. Two agents simultaneously claim the same urgent billing ticket. A regional supervisor needs to redistribute work from an absent employee without worldwide super-admin powers. All employees should not receive customer ticket notifications; only eligible queue members need actionable alerts. A filtered urgent queue must expose every case, including cases beyond the first 50 and cases with a failed reply-delivery attempt.

### Exact recommended controls and UX

Use atomic claims with owner/version, supervisor-authorized takeover reasons, and explicit transfer receipts. Add cursor navigation, server-side urgency ordering, unread and oldest-unassigned projections, and scope-constrained saved queues. Preserve existing terminal guards and message idempotency. Route alerts to existing teams/on-call schedules through a bounded outbox; notify inactive/off-shift staff only under approved escalation policy. Debounce/coalesce realtime invalidations and fetch lightweight counts separately from full list detail. Show reply authority, delivery state, ownership conflict, and resolution evidence in accessible controls. Bulk reassignment should be a previewed asynchronous task with per-case authorization.

### Dependencies / owner

Support Operations owns team coverage and SLA rules; Support Engineering owns claims/inbox; Messaging owns notification delivery; IAM owns territory and reply rights; SRE owns event fanout budgets. Customer privacy and retention policy require business/legal review.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

Twenty simultaneous claims produce one owner and nineteen explicit conflicts. All 5,000 filtered fixture tickets are reachable. A 100-event burst creates at most one coalesced reload per viewer per agreed one-second window. Inactive/unassigned staff receive no routine new-ticket emails. Regional supervisors can transfer only permitted cases. A 10,000-ticket reassignment resumes safely. Resolution/reopen paths honor migration 600; keyboard, locale, ownership, and failed-delivery browser scenarios execute without skips.

## 11. Live Support — /admin/support/chat

### Mission / what exists

Live Support embeds the existing SupportConversationWorkspace with availability controls. The workspace implements paginated/load-more conversations, assignments, realtime viewer presence, typing/read states, lifecycle/customer context, SLA information, reminders, attachments, transcript download, merge, redaction, internal notes, AI draft review, external-channel delivery status, and retry controls. Conversation claims already lock capacity and competing work. Email and SMS adapters exist; phone/social outbound explicitly report unsupported. The enterprise issue is strengthening these working components' authorization, query shape, global filters, and provider evidence, not inventing a missing live-support page.

### Evidence

Wrapper: `client/src/admin/pages/SupportAgentChat.jsx:6`. Workspace list/filter/events: `client/src/components/support/SupportConversationWorkspace.jsx:187`, `:518`, `:614`, `:795`, `:984`. Platform list API: `server/routes/support.js:64`, `:284`, `:297`, `:321`. Claim locking: `server/controllers/conversationAssignmentController.js:51`, `:77`. Existing tenant checks: `server/services/supportEmployerService.js:11`, `:62`; channel capabilities: `server/services/supportChannelDeliveryService.js:17`; tests: `server/__tests__/supportRoutes.test.js:173`, `server/__tests__/supportChannelDeliveryRecovery.test.js:15`.

### Findings

- **T11-F01 — High [confirmed defect]: live conversation access bypasses the delegated support grant boundary.** After general authentication, `/support/conversations` treats any admin role as platform authority (`support.js:285`, `:297`), returning global conversations unless a business filter is supplied. The legacy ticket API requires `manage_support`. Role-level access here does not check that grant or region. A business filter is optional client input, not an authorization boundary. Apply server support-read/write/export scopes consistently while preserving existing business membership checks for nonplatform agents.
- **T11-F02 — High [code observation]: the list aggregates whole message histories despite conversation pagination.** The platform list joins all conversation messages and JSON-aggregates their contents before LIMIT (`support.js:328`, `:355`, `:359`). Fifty conversations can therefore return far more than fifty lightweight records. Move message detail to the existing detail API and use bounded last-message/unread projections; paginate conversations before expensive joins where plans permit. Verify redaction and retention behavior across this raw list projection as well as detail/download.
- **T11-F03 — High [confirmed defect]: several queue filters operate only on loaded conversations.** Requests include search/channel but not status, ownership, priority, or category (`SupportConversationWorkspace.jsx:198`); those filters run locally over the loaded array (`:614`). An urgent owned conversation beyond the first page can be absent from an apparently empty filtered view. Keep load-more but make operational filters authoritative on the server and return matching totals/cursors.
- **T11-F04 — Medium [verification gap]: supported adapters and retries do not prove worldwide channel coverage.** Capabilities explicitly mark phone/social unsupported and email/SMS configured conditionally. The UI already respects capability state. No provider delivery/recovery evidence for every target country was obtained; a healthy Redis/worker snapshot or green mocked test is insufficient to claim supported live delivery everywhere.

### Expansion scenario

Three thousand long-running conversations span multiple businesses and languages during a provider outage. A regional live agent selects urgent, unassigned SMS cases and must find matching cases beyond the initial load. Another operator views a two-year conversation without downloading its full history on every queue refresh. An Arabic-speaking supervisor reviews a merge and redaction while a provider retry resumes. Country/tenant scope must constrain list, transcript, merge target, socket rooms, customer context, and retries, not only visible filters.

### Exact recommended controls and UX

Extend the existing support grant model to every REST and realtime operation; require server-resolved regions/businesses and scoped transcript-export rights. Use lightweight queue rows, cursor pagination, server filters, and lazy history retrieval. Retain existing locked claims and expose assignment/version conflicts. Preserve capability-aware buttons, but add per-market supported/configured/degraded status and delivered/failed/unknown provider receipts. Unsupported channels need an explicit owned fallback case with SLA, not a misleading sent state. Localize operational fallback strings and test bidi names, attachments, transcript redaction, and focus handling.

### Dependencies / owner

Support Engineering owns queue/state/realtime; IAM owns scope consistency; Integration Operations owns providers and market evidence; Support Operations owns fallback SLAs; Privacy owns approved redaction/retention behavior. Existing channel outbox/recovery remains authoritative.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

An admin without support grants receives 403 on list/detail/download/socket access. Nonmember tenants and foreign regions are denied on all derivatives. Priority/owner filters find cases on every page. Queue payload contains only a bounded preview per row and stays below the agreed 250 KB target for 50 rows. At 250 concurrent agents, p95 list reads stay below two seconds. Demonstrate provider timeout, restart, retry, and duplicate-webhook recovery in isolation; execute Arabic, keyboard, merge, and redaction acceptance without skips.

## 12. Quote Requests — /admin/quote-requests

### Mission / what exists

Quote Requests is an implemented intake inbox with paginated lists, status/search filters, customer contact details, request context, notes, status editing, and guarded detail fetches. Its underlying records are shared with Direct operations, which add assignment, scheduling, payment proof, catalog selections, and customer messages. The server validates statuses, negative amounts, schedule order, assignee eligibility, and some payment transitions. This existing cross-workspace record model is useful; enterprise readiness requires preserving omitted fields, enforcing the same authority on detail reads, and preventing concurrent notes/financial changes from silently overwriting one another.

### Evidence

UI: `client/src/admin/pages/QuoteRequestsInbox.jsx:72`, `:83`, `:118`, `:159`, `:198`. Routes: `server/routes/quoteRequests.js:10`, `:12`. Server list/detail/update: `server/controllers/quoteRequestsController.js:530`, `:596`, `:620`, `:742`, `:811`. Direct aliases: `server/routes/direct.js:104`, `:106`. Existing catalog-intake coverage: `server/__tests__/directQuoteCatalogLink.test.js:48`; shared record schema: `server/migrations/374_quote_requests.sql:4`.

### Findings

- **T12-F01 — High [confirmed defect]: direct request detail uses role-only administrative access.** List/update routes require `manage_operations`, but `GET /quote-requests/:id` only authenticates and its controller authorizes any admin/super-admin role (`quoteRequests.js:12`, `quoteRequestsController.js:607`). Thus a grantless admin denied the inbox list can obtain a known request's full detail. Require the same scoped read permission for the administrative branch while retaining owner access and field-appropriate customer serialization.
- **T12-F02 — High [confirmed defect]: notes/status-only edits can replace an existing quote currency with USD.** UI sends only `{status, admin_notes}` (`QuoteRequestsInbox.jsx:163`). The controller defaults omitted currency to USD (`quoteRequestsController.js:631`) and always writes it through COALESCE (`:816`). Updating notes on an EUR quote therefore changes its currency label while preserving the amount. Omitted fields must remain untouched; currency changes need a dedicated validated monetary transition with explicit intent and audit.
- **T12-F03 — High [code observation]: preflight checks and final edits are not version-bound.** Payment/schedule checks read current data, then a later UPDATE uses only ID (`:746`, `:831`). Admin notes replace the whole text (`:814`), and the UI has no expected version. Simultaneous intake and dispatch operators can lose notes or change state after validation. Preserve existing schedule guards and add transactionally locked/versioned updates, append-only notes, and conflict-aware UI diff/reload.
- **T12-F04 — High [code observation]: list API accepts unbounded limit, while headline subtotals are page-local.** The list uses a minimum but no maximum for requested limit (`:555`). UI “new” and “active” counts derive from the 20 returned requests (`QuoteRequestsInbox.jsx:205`, `:211`) beside a total from the whole query. The UI list loader also lacks the detail loader's sequence protection (`:118`). Bound requests, return clearly scoped aggregate counts, and guard list response ordering.

### Expansion scenario

A European sales team receives 25,000 requests while a North American dispatcher updates schedules on the same shared records. A Paris agent changes a French note on a euro-denominated quote; the amount/currency must remain EUR. Two operators editing a case should see a meaningful conflict and preserve both notes. A regional manager needs accurate intake aging, ownership, and conversion totals instead of counters that change merely because another page was opened. Read authority must also protect direct deep links.

### Exact recommended controls and UX

Separate read, intake-note, quote-price, assignment, scheduling, and payment-proof rights within the existing request model. Enforce territory/business scope in list/detail/update/export. Use partial-update semantics distinguishing absent, clear, and change; freeze monetary revisions once accepted/settled except through approved correction. Store note authors/time and request revisions. Return bounded cursors and server totals; show currency, region, owner, due time, and last edit in the pane. Add an explicit conflict comparison and dirty-draft protection. Asynchronous lead assignment/import can reuse existing Direct ownership and audit infrastructure.

### Dependencies / owner

Sales/Direct Operations owns intake and conversion definitions; Payments owns monetary changes; IAM owns request scope; Data Engineering owns aggregates; Support owns contact delivery. Country commercial/legal eligibility remains a separate reviewed policy.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

A notes-only patch to EUR preserves EUR and amount exactly. Grantless admins cannot read request IDs; owners retain appropriate access. Stale concurrent edits return 409 with preserved notes. Requests above the agreed 100-row cap are rejected or clamped explicitly. New/active totals reconcile across all pages and delayed old list responses cannot replace newer filters. A 25,000-request synthetic assignment run resumes safely and meets the agreed regional SLA; localized keyboard workflows execute without skips.

## 13. Direct Ops — /direct/admin

### Mission / what exists

Direct Ops is an existing multi-page operational portal with dashboard, inbox, work orders, dispatch calendar, assignment, availability/blackout blocks, calendar export/history, payment-proof review, customers, reports, team, settings, and catalog links. The main application hosts this route outside the admin SPA, as the route manifest explicitly records. The slot engine already uses holds, transactions, row/advisory locks, and post-lock capacity checks. Calendar overlap protection also exists in migrations. Enterprise readiness therefore concerns finer authority, consistent field edits, operational timezones, and trustworthy payment recovery rather than absence of dispatch or scheduling.

### Evidence

Route identity: `client/src/admin/adminRouteManifest.js:34`. Portal calendar/payment: `client/src/pages/DirectAdminPortalPage.jsx:702`, `:826`, `:1165`, `:1280`, `:1319`. API: `server/routes/direct.js:78`, `:80`, `:104`, `:108`. Proof: `server/controllers/directController.js:1753`; update guards: `server/controllers/quoteRequestsController.js:742`. Locking: `server/services/directSlotEngine.js:428`, `:562`, `:604`. Schema: `server/migrations/420_direct_schedule_conflict_guard.sql:17`, `server/migrations/589_direct_catalog_availability_capacity.sql:81`; tests: `server/__tests__/directSlotEngine.test.js:131`.

### Findings

- **T13-F01 — High [code observation]: operational rights also authorize payment-proof confirmation and payment-status edits.** Proof confirmation requires `manage_operations` (`direct.js:78`) and records confirmed status/operator metadata (`directController.js:1767`). General request patch accepts payment statuses including paid/refunded and guards only selected regressions (`quoteRequestsController.js:654`, `:758`, `:773`). These are administrative records, not proof of a bank transfer or actual refund. Separate dispatch from reconciliation/financial correction rights and tie final monetary status to durable evidence and approved recovery controls.
- **T13-F02 — High [code observation]: dispatch editing uses the browser's local timezone.** Date-key helpers and `datetime-local` conversions use JavaScript Date; schedule edits convert the browser-entered time to ISO (`DirectAdminPortalPage.jsx:702`, `:731`, `:1166`). No explicit service-location timezone selector appears in these controls. A Cairo operator scheduling an Ohio job can interpret the same wall-clock time differently. Store and display service timezone plus UTC; validate daylight-saving ambiguity independently of the operator's computer timezone.
- **T13-F03 — High [design requirement]: operations grants are global and bundle unrelated responsibilities.** Dashboard, inbox, customer history, team, catalog, scheduling, and proof share global `manage_operations`; settings and exports have separate grants (`direct.js:80–112`). This correctly denies regional grants today but does not support country dispatcher, crew supervisor, catalog curator, or finance reviewer delegation. Add domain-specific scoped permissions without promoting routine dispatch staff to super admins.
- **T13-F04 — Medium [verification gap]: existing capacity/overlap guards need one combined concurrency and recovery rehearsal.** Advisory locks and hold checks are implemented; claiming scheduling is wholly race-prone would be inaccurate. What remains unproven is consistent behavior across public holds, administrative reschedules, newly inserted blackout blocks, worker restarts, and 3,500-employee handoffs on a production-like restored dataset. CI's clean-database migration rehearsal does not establish that combined property.

### Expansion scenario

A global dispatcher schedules Ohio crews from Cairo while a local supervisor creates a weather blackout. Customers hold booking slots concurrently and a payment reviewer checks externally submitted proof. A disputed payment needs investigation without an operations employee marking “refunded” as if funds were returned. The worker restarts mid-reminder delivery. The system must preserve slot capacity, time interpretation, ownership, payment evidence, and delivery receipts across all these existing paths.

### Exact recommended controls and UX

Use a scoped dispatcher workbench with territory, crew pool, timezone, coverage, capability, and owner/SLA indicators. Preserve slot locks and database overlap guards; require expected versions for admin edits and integrate blackout changes into the same coordination boundary. Give financial reviewers a proof-evidence checklist, original currency/amount, external reference, immutable receipt, and correction history; high-impact corrections require maker/checker policy. Reuse existing ledger/provider recovery where actual financial movement occurs. Operational status and settled funds must be separate concepts, with unknown/pending/failed reconciliation visible. Market/provider approvals and payment policy need jurisdiction review.

### Dependencies / owner

Direct Operations owns dispatch; Marketplace Scheduling owns holds/capacity; Finance owns proof/reconciliation; IAM owns delegation; SRE owns recovery rehearsals; Localization owns timezone and regional formatting.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

A foreign-timezone operator schedules the same service instant as the local crew, including DST repeated/skipped hours. Fifty concurrent holds/reschedules/blackouts never overbook an assignee. Dispatch-only users cannot confirm proof or finalize paid/refunded states. Monetary recovery requires an evidence-linked receipt and replay-safe execution. An isolated restart preserves bookings and reminder outcomes. A production-like restored rehearsal records dataset, image, migration state, timings, and results; full authenticated Direct journeys execute without skips.

## 14. Direct Service Areas — /admin/direct-service-areas

### Mission / what exists

Direct Service Areas manages the coverage records used by public Direct availability and quote intake. The existing page supports search/active filtering, creating/editing an area, ZIP coordinate lookup, radius, notes, activation, and refresh/retry. The service caches geocoding and compares locations to active coverage radii. Direct coverage is different from employee administrative territory: a customer's location being serviceable must not grant an operator authority to manage that market. Worldwide expansion needs to extend this implemented coverage system for international geography, governance, spatial scale, and service-specific promises.

### Evidence

UI: `client/src/admin/pages/DirectServiceAreas.jsx:11`, `:64`, `:75`, `:83`, `:112`, `:177`. Routes: `server/routes/direct.js:101`. Service: `server/services/directServiceAreas.js:46`, `:106`, `:120`, `:146`, `:197`, `:227`, `:273`. Controller: `server/controllers/directController.js:1188`. Schema: `server/migrations/377_direct_service_areas.sql:5`. Existing tests: `server/__tests__/directServiceAreas.test.js:55`, `:81`, `:105`, `:118`.

### Findings

- **T14-F01 — High [code observation]: the managed geography path is US-specific.** The form requires and strips input to five numeric ZIP digits (`DirectServiceAreas.jsx:85`, `:123`, `:179`). ZIP geocoding calls `/us/`, and address geocoding sets `countrycodes=us` (`directServiceAreas.js:107`, `:126`). A Canadian or UK postal address cannot be managed through this flow as designed. This is a current expansion limitation, not an outage of the supported Ohio coverage. Add explicit country and postal validation with provider capability by market.
- **T14-F02 — High [code observation]: coverage and administration load all areas and compare them in application memory.** `listServiceAreas` is unbounded (`:46`), public availability maps/sorts every active area (`:201`), and the client filters locally (`DirectServiceAreas.jsx:75`). That may be adequate for a small launch footprint; it is unproven for thousands of country/service polygons and frequent public checks. Use scoped paginated management and indexed spatial matching with bounded candidates, preserving existing distance/cache semantics where appropriate.
- **T14-F03 — High [code observation]: coverage edits have neither version conflicts nor durable actor history in the inspected write path.** Update reads the row and later replaces fields by ID (`directServiceAreas.js:273`, `:285`). The controller passes no actor to the service (`directController.js:1220`). A manager disabling an area can be overwritten by another employee's stale radius edit. Require expected version, actor/reason, and an atomic change history linked to the existing Direct audit system.
- **T14-F04 — Medium [confirmed defect]: coordinate validation accepts finite but geographically invalid values.** Normalization checks finiteness and positive radius (`directServiceAreas.js:235`), not latitude/longitude ranges. The initial schema constrains radius but not coordinate bounds (`377_direct_service_areas.sql:9`). Reject latitude outside [-90,90] and longitude outside [-180,180] in API validation; verify any later constraints before migrating existing data. Also distinguish absent coordinates from empty strings that coerce to zero.

### Expansion scenario

Operations launches Toronto, London, and Paris while Ohio remains live. A postal lookup must include country and service timezone, and a river or border can make a simple radius an unsuitable service promise. A regional manager disables Toronto coverage during a staffing shortage; a stale editor must not reactivate it accidentally. Thousands of coverage regions and millions of customer availability checks must not require loading the entire worldwide directory for every request. Administrative scope remains independently enforced on every edit.

### Exact recommended controls and UX

Extend existing area IDs with country, timezone, service/category applicability, geometry, effective dates, and accountable owner. Support radius and polygon coverage with explicit exclusions and a preview map/test-address tool. Show distance units appropriate to the selected locale and provider supported/configured/unavailable states. Require versioned publish/disable with impact preview on accepted bookings, holds, and quotes; schedule coverage changes through existing Direct lifecycle controls. Paginate management, cache versioned public coverage, and invalidate it after approved changes. Provider usage/market eligibility must be reviewed with the chosen provider; do not assume existing public geocoder endpoints are approved for enterprise worldwide traffic.

### Dependencies / owner

Direct Market Operations owns coverage promises; Geospatial Engineering owns spatial indexes/providers; IAM owns country editor scope; Scheduling owns existing bookings/holds; Localization owns postal/address/unit presentation.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

Canadian alphanumeric and UK postal fixtures resolve only within the selected country. Invalid coordinate ranges return 400 without persistence. Concurrent disable/radius edits return a typed conflict and preserve actor history. A 10,000-area synthetic directory returns paged admin results and public matches within p95 500 ms under an agreed traffic workload. Coverage changes explicitly preserve or route existing obligations. Regional editors cannot change other markets; map alternatives, keyboard input, Arabic layout, and provider-outage messages pass executed browser checks.

## 15. Direct Catalog — /admin/direct-catalog

### Mission / what exists

Direct Catalog is an implemented catalog/lifecycle editor with categories, services, tools, five-language translations, review flags, search, availability/bookability, price, service area, crew size, assignee pools, bulk lifecycle transitions, archive confirmation, and AI draft preview/apply. Public catalog queries already support bounded localized search, and server publication requires complete translations. Entity updates are transactional with audit/history; optimistic conflicts exist when updatedAt is supplied. Existing manual fallback for an unavailable AI provider is tested. Enterprise enhancement must focus on the administrative payload, bulk completeness, strict concurrency, and scoped publication ownership.

### Evidence

UI: `client/src/admin/pages/DirectCatalog.jsx:10`, `:22`, `:51`, `:93`, `:121`, `:139`, `:160`, `:174`. API: `server/routes/direct.js:88`, `:98`. Service: `server/services/directCatalogService.js:514`, `:571`, `:604`, `:663`, `:742`, `:804`. Schema/history indexes: `server/migrations/390_direct_catalog.sql:57`, `414_direct_catalog_lifecycle.sql:107`, `584_direct_audit_indexes.sql:20`. Tests: `server/__tests__/directCatalog.test.js:138`, `:166`, `:257`, `:334`; performance verifier tests: `server/__tests__/directCatalogPerformance.test.js:5`.

### Findings

- **T15-F01 — High [code observation]: admin catalog returns the whole directory despite public pagination.** `getAdminCatalog` selects all categories/services/tools and resolves pools/review flags for them (`directCatalogService.js:515`). The client searches loaded arrays, chiefly English names/descriptions (`DirectCatalog.jsx:121`). Existing public bounded search should be extended to authenticated scoped management instead of claiming all catalog paths are unpaginated. Require server filters, lightweight summaries, locale-aware search, and detail-on-demand for large country catalogs.
- **T15-F02 — High [confirmed defect]: bulk selection above 100 is silently truncated.** The service deduplicates requested IDs then slices to 100 (`directCatalogService.js:808`); the client permits arbitrary selected IDs and sends them together (`DirectCatalog.jsx:156`, `:162`). The request can report success for 100 while leaving other selected entries unchanged. Reject excessive synchronous batches explicitly or return a durable job covering the entire authorized selection, with requested/processed/failed/skipped totals and a manifest.
- **T15-F03 — Medium [code observation]: optimistic concurrency is optional, not an API invariant.** The UI carries `updatedAt`, and the parser accepts it as expectedUpdatedAt (`DirectCatalog.jsx:63`, `directCatalogService.js:604`). The locked save checks conflicts only if that value exists (`:668`). A different client omitting the precondition can overwrite a stale record. Preserve the existing implementation and require a valid version on every update; bulk and archive need equivalent expected-state or reviewed-selection semantics.
- **T15-F04 — High [design requirement]: global publishing rights need regional and commercial approval boundaries.** Catalog routes share global `manage_operations`; records have serviceArea text, currency, and lifecycle, but those fields alone are not curator authorization or market approval. Add scoped curator/drafter/publisher rights and market-specific offer applicability around existing entity IDs. Five complete translations do not establish provider availability, licensing eligibility, price accuracy, or legal approval in every country.

### Expansion scenario

A curator prepares 2,000 services for three new markets while local reviewers approve translations and operations verifies crew capacity. Pausing 800 services during a staffing incident must cover all 800, not a hidden first 100. A stale translated description cannot overwrite a newly reviewed price/availability revision. Customers in Arabic should find the same service concept through localized names, while publication in one market must not enable bookings in another without readiness and provider review.

### Exact recommended controls and UX

Keep translation tabs, lifecycle rules, review flags, locked transactional saves, audit/history, and manual AI fallback. Add server-backed locale/category/lifecycle/market search and paginated rows with virtualized selection. Provide persistent batch receipts, dry run, full selection counts, per-item failures, cancellation of pending work, and resume after restart. Make versions mandatory and show conflict diffs without discarding drafts. Attach owner/reviewer/market eligibility and price revision to publication; reuse crew-pool/bookability controls rather than duplicate availability systems. Separate AI drafted, human reviewed, operationally ready, and published states; display capability availability accurately without exposing private configuration.

### Dependencies / owner

Catalog Product owns entities; Localization owns language review; Direct Operations owns operational capability; Finance owns price/currency revisions; IAM owns scoped publication; SRE owns payload/batch budgets. Legal/providers approve applicable market conditions separately.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

A 10,000-service admin fixture returns no more than 100 rows and localized server search finds all supported-language labels. An 800-item transition processes every item or explicitly rejects before writes; replay cannot duplicate history. Updates without valid versions fail, stale versions return 409, and correct versions preserve transactional audit. Publication requires the agreed regional readiness evidence. AI outages retain manual editing. Measure admin and public paths separately; verifier unit tests are not claimed as a completed live capacity test.

## 16. Messages — /admin/messages

### Mission / what exists

Messages is an implemented one-to-one administrator/customer messaging workspace with conversation search, unread counts, thread selection, optimistic sending, visible send errors, and a new-conversation dialog. List/detail fetches have stale-response protection and several controls are localized/accessibility-tested. Server routes require `message_users`; ordinary admins list their own conversations, while super admins list all. Sending uses sanitized content, transactions, conversation uniqueness recovery, optional message idempotency, audit, and notification infrastructure. The remaining problems are UI/API identity alignment, history reachability, truthful read state, replay from the browser, and team continuity.

### Evidence

UI: `client/src/admin/pages/AdminMessages.jsx:49`, `:79`, `:102`, `:115`, `:160`. Routes: `server/routes/admin.js:639`. List/thread/send: `server/controllers/adminMessageController.js:36`, `:110`, `:146`, `:153`, `:188`, `:219`. User lookup contract: `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:538`. Tests: `server/__tests__/adminMessageIdempotency.test.js:99`, `:121`, `:142`; UI contract: `client/scripts/adminMessages.test.mjs:11`.

### Findings

- **T16-F01 — High [confirmed defect]: new-message user search sends the wrong parameter.** `AdminMessages.jsx:176` requests `/admin/users?q=...`, but `getUsers` reads `search`, not `q` (`superAdminController.js:538`). The typed query is ignored and the first ten ordinary users can be returned. Also, user lookup requires `manage_users` while the page requires `message_users`, so messaging-only staff cannot find recipients through that path. Add a narrow scoped recipient endpoint or align the existing lookup without granting global user-management authority.
- **T16-F02 — High [confirmed defect]: thread loading hides recent history and marks unseen messages read.** UI requests no page/offset (`AdminMessages.jsx:88`). Server returns the first 50 messages ordered ascending (`adminMessageController.js:114`, `:161`) and first marks every unread message in the conversation read (`:146`). For a 200-message thread, the newest 150 are absent while read state can say they were seen. Fetch the latest bounded page, allow older history navigation, and mark only messages actually displayed through a separate authorized acknowledgment.
- **T16-F03 — High [confirmed defect]: super-admin list and thread selection identify different conversations.** Super list includes other admins' conversations (`adminMessageController.js:76`), but UI opens by userId alone (`AdminMessages.jsx:103`) and thread lookup always uses the current admin as customer_id (`adminMessageController.js:129`). Selecting another admin's case can show the supervisor's different thread or empty history. Use an immutable conversation ID and explicit supervisor read/participation authorization, rather than conflating all contact with a user.
- **T16-F04 — Medium [code observation]: browser sends do not activate existing idempotency support.** The UI posts content only (`AdminMessages.jsx:133`), while the backend accepts optional UUID message IDs (`adminMessageController.js:24`, `:193`). An ambiguous network failure followed by manual resend can duplicate contact despite server replay tests. Persist a message UUID per draft/send attempt and retry the same identity; preserve visible delivery uncertainty and draft recovery.

### Expansion scenario

A supervisor takes over a departing employee's 200-message customer thread while a regional agent sends a sensitive billing clarification over an unstable connection. Typing the recipient name must find the intended authorized user, not an unfiltered initial page. The supervisor must see the exact selected conversation and latest message; unread state must not erase unseen evidence. Employee turnover and shift coverage need continuity without copying private threads into a second communication platform or granting every employee all customer messages.

### Exact recommended controls and UX

Keep existing message sanitization, uniqueness recovery, audit, and notification queues. Introduce conversation-ID navigation, cursor history, explicit displayed-message read acknowledgment, and scoped recipient search. Add persisted send UUIDs, pending/sent/failed/unknown status, retry receipts, and drafts scoped to the selected thread. Extend ownership/transfer with existing support case links and team delegation; direct customer contact should retain purpose and case context. Paginate the conversation list beyond the current requested 100 rows (`AdminMessages.jsx:56`) and enforce both region and participant authority on REST/socket operations. Keep accessible dialog/status controls and test the actual send/history behavior, not only source strings.

### Dependencies / owner

Messaging Engineering owns thread identity and delivery; Support Operations owns continuity; IAM owns contact/supervisor scope; Privacy owns visibility/retention policy; Localization/Accessibility own authoring and confirmation behavior.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

Recipient queries return only matching permitted users. A 200-message thread initially shows the newest page and exposes all older pages; only displayed incoming messages are acknowledged. A supervisor selecting another admin's conversation sees that exact ID or an explicit denial. Twenty retries of one send UUID produce one message and one notification/audit effect. Regional and nonparticipant access is denied, and cross-thread draft/send races preserve recipient identity. Executed keyboard/five-language browser tests validate new conversation, transfer, history, and ambiguous delivery recovery.

## 17. Search — /admin/search

### Mission / what exists

Search is an implemented federated administrative lookup across users, jobs, messages, disputes, payments, tickets, reviews, and bids. The UI has entity results, links, query/page state, and error handling. The server bounds query length and page size, escapes ILIKE wildcards, restricts type names, parallelizes entity queries, and logs searches with a hashed IP. Existing pagination contracts cover its bounds. Enterprise readiness requires treating federated search as an authorization-sensitive data product: a single search permission must not become an alternate route to finance, private conversations, or other territories.

### Evidence

UI: `client/src/admin/pages/AdminSearch.jsx:29`, `:36`, `:107`, `:127`. Route: `server/routes/admin.js:281`. Engine: `server/controllers/adminSearchController.js:16`, `:27`, `:36`, `:52`, `:105`, `:153`, `:268`, `:303`. Initial search-audit indexes: `server/migrations/049_admin_search_and_permissions.sql:17`, `:20`. Bounds contract: `server/__tests__/adminPaginationContracts.test.js:8`. Permission behavior: `server/middleware/adminAuth.js:126`.

### Findings

- **T17-F01 — High [confirmed defect]: search exposes other domains without their permission checks.** The route requires global `view_search_logs`; the controller then searches requested/default types without finance, support, message, or user-management grants (`adminSearchController.js:27`, `:52`). Payment results include amounts, processor identifiers, and payer/payee emails (`:153`), and message results include content (`:105`). No participant or tenant/region predicate appears in those queries. This bypasses the intended separate domain permissions. Derive allowed types/fields from current task grants and intersect them with requested scope before any query.
- **T17-F02 — High [confirmed defect]: failed entity queries are returned as zero matches.** `Promise.allSettled` is sensible for partial results, but `extract` maps rejection to empty rows/zero total (`:269`). The response has no per-source failure distinction. An investigator can interpret a broken payment/message query as evidence that no record exists. Return per-entity available/denied/failed/stale status and a correlation reference while retaining successful results; do not reintroduce historic SQL assertions without current schema reproduction.
- **T17-F03 — High [code observation]: bounds limit returned rows, not federated query cost.** Up to eight wildcard queries run concurrently, often with `COUNT(*) OVER()` and OFFSET (`:52`, `:61`, `:112`, `:166`). At six million users and much larger message history, two-character substring searches and deep pages need measured index/query budgets. The English full-text index in migration 049 is on the search audit log, not evidence that all entity predicates are indexed. Inspect representative plans and choose exact ID/email/phone lookup, supported full-text/trigram strategies, or authorized search projections based on measured requirements.
- **T17-F04 — Medium [confirmed defect]: out-of-range pages can erase known totals.** Totals are read from the first returned row (`:272`); when a type has no row at the requested offset, its total becomes zero even if matching records exist on earlier pages. Keep totals independent of the page or return a cursor/hasMore contract. This matters when one shared page number spans entity types with different result counts.

### Expansion scenario

A regional investigator searches a common surname during a payment complaint. They should receive permitted customer/case matches without unrelated private conversations or processor identifiers. A finance reviewer searches an exact payment reference and must know if the payment source failed rather than conclude no payment exists. A broad two-character query from hundreds of employees must not monopolize the transactional DB. Navigating the jobs page must not reset user totals simply because that entity has fewer matches at the same offset.

### Exact recommended controls and UX

Apply field-level and entity-level rights inside the engine, with server-resolved tenant/region predicates and participant checks where relevant. Add exact-identifier search modes, minimum broad-query rules, cancellation/time budgets, query rate limits, and per-type cursors. Show denied versus unavailable versus zero results distinctly and keep authoritative record links. Use permission-aware lightweight projections or measured indexes; do not choose a separate search cluster without workload evidence. Log purpose/case, authorized scope, selected types, and outcome safely; raw query retention must follow reviewed privacy policy because queries can contain identifying information. Preserve hashed IP behavior and avoid secret/private payload logging.

### Dependencies / owner

Search Engineering owns query correctness/performance; IAM and domain teams own authorization projections; SRE owns resource budgets; Privacy owns search-log minimization; Support/Finance define investigation workflows.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

An operator with only search-log permission cannot obtain finance, support, private-message, or foreign-tenant content. Injected failure in one entity renders unavailable with a trace ID while other matches remain visible. Empty/out-of-range pages preserve correct totals or valid cursor semantics. Exact identifier p95 is below 500 ms and scoped broad search p95 below two seconds at 250 concurrent operators on representative synthetic/restored data. Search load causes less than 10% transactional latency degradation. Execute cross-domain negative-access, cancellation, keyboard, and five-language result tests without skips.

## Completion and evidence boundary

All 17 requested tabs are covered with existing implementation, at least three stable findings, source pointers, expansion scenarios, controls, ownership, and measurable acceptance criteria. Prior local verification is user-reported as 310 suites / 3,339 tests passed, with build/typecheck success; it was not repeated here. Latest e7ac9415 GitHub CI and fresh-PostGIS apply-twice rehearsal succeeded. Both authenticated-browser tests skipped, and optional live DB verification skipped. The user-supplied 08:55 UTC live readiness observation was healthy with Credit enabled. No production-like restored-dataset rehearsal, worldwide provider acceptance, full live browser matrix, or target-scale benchmark is claimed completed. Null release provenance metadata remains an observability gap, not a claim that e7ac9415 is unpushed or that the running service is down.

## 18. Businesses — /admin/businesses

### Mission and what exists

This tab moderates business accounts, allowing operators to locate organizations, inspect owners and activity, change active state, flag concerns, edit notes, and request deletion. It is an implemented screen, supported by business detail and broader business-management views, not a missing capability. The client has status/activity/sort controls, paged loading with request cancellation, preservation of previous results on fetch failure, and reason-entry confirmation. The server implements conditional state changes and soft deletion; these are useful foundations for controlled enterprise moderation rather than a reason to replace the system.

### Evidence

- [client/src/admin/pages/BusinessModeration.jsx:229](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/BusinessModeration.jsx:229) implements abortable list loading; `:274` sends moderation actions with a reason.
- [server/routes/admin.js:93](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/routes/admin.js:93) protects business endpoints with admin authentication and `manage_users`.
- [server/controllers/businessModerationController.js:7](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/businessModerationController.js:7) reads filters, `:32` builds the result projection, `:79` implements conditional toggling, and `:430` requires deletion confirmation.
- [server/migrations/161_business_moderation_enhancements.sql:4](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/migrations/161_business_moderation_enhancements.sql:4) adds moderation notes/deletion fields; `:23` adds a supporting index.

### Findings

**T18-F01 — High [confirmed defect]: deletion confirmation contracts disagree.** The client action body at [BusinessModeration.jsx:274](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/BusinessModeration.jsx:274) supplies `{reason}`; the deletion controller at [businessModerationController.js:430](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/businessModerationController.js:430) additionally requires `confirmed`. The normal confirmed UI action therefore lacks a required API field. This is a current source-level mismatch, not an inference that the business screen is absent. A browser-to-controller contract test is needed before declaring the workflow usable.

**T18-F02 — Medium [confirmed defect]: activity filtering and displayed metrics misrepresent the query.** The controller destructures `activity` at `:7` but does not use it in the predicates. Its projection sets category to null and earnings to zero at `:37` and `:43`; job counts at `:42` are attached through the owner rather than a demonstrated business attribution. Operators cannot safely interpret these values as verified business-level commercial activity. Either calculate the intended metrics or label their limits explicitly.

**T18-F03 — High [code observation]: reasons and investigation records are weaker than the UI implies.** The UI requires a reason, but the API accepts nullable reasons and catches audit failures (`:100`). Flagging at `:352` is an audit action rather than a durable, assigned investigation queue. Notes update unconditionally at `:384`, permitting stale editors to overwrite each other. The conditional toggle at `:79` deserves credit, but it does not provide an expected-version contract for all moderation changes.

**T18-F04 — Medium [design requirement]: global moderation needs bounded, delegated work.** List limits are parsed without a maximum at `:9`; the statistics query at `:58` adds correlated activity work. The global permission boundary is secure against regional grants, but regional staff cannot use this workflow with target-scoped authority. Performance on millions of businesses and related jobs remains a measurement gap, not a measured outage.

### Expansion scenario

A regional fraud team investigates 12,000 newly onboarded businesses after a referral campaign. Two analysts edit the same organization while headquarters reviews a deletion appeal. An ineffective activity filter changes prioritization, zero earnings can imply inactivity, and a lost note destroys handoff context. Granting every analyst global user-management authority would solve access friction by creating excessive reach, not safe delegation.

### Recommended controls and UX

Repair the confirmation contract and test it end-to-end. Enforce structured reason codes plus bounded explanatory text on the API. Keep soft deletion, show affected memberships and dependent work, and require a reviewed recovery policy. Add expected versions, conflict diffs, append-only case notes, assigned investigator, appeal state, and due-time escalation to existing moderation records. Expose unavailable metrics as unavailable rather than zero. Bound page size, stabilize ordering with ID, and benchmark filtered counts; use asynchronous scoped exports and reviewed batch operations with per-record outcomes. Display the active regional scope and disable unauthorized actions with clear explanations. Provide labeled dialog controls, focus restoration, localized reason labels, and RTL-safe tables.

### Dependencies and owner

Business Operations owns policy and case SLAs; Identity owns scoped permissions; business API owners own attribution and concurrency; Data/SRE owns query plans and job limits. Records management must define deletion recovery separately from engineering implementation.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

All UI actions pass authenticated contract tests; missing reasons are rejected; stale notes return 409 without loss; restricted analysts cannot read or mutate another region by direct ID. A representative million-record dataset sustains 100 concurrent moderators with list p95 below one second and limits capped at 100. Every flag has an owner and deadline, every destructive decision has durable audit evidence, and keyboard-only users can complete and cancel every dialog.

## 19. Tool Rentals — /admin/tool-rentals

### Mission and what exists

Tool Rentals is an existing moderation queue for peer equipment listings, not an absent rental system. It displays listing and owner information, supports search and availability filtering, and offers reason-confirmed Hide/Show actions with pagination. The wider application already includes bookings, reviews, favorites, invoices, owner terms, and peer-rental attestation. Enterprise expansion should connect moderation to those capabilities rather than duplicate booking or deposit machinery. The backend's conditional update protects a simultaneous availability change from a simple overwrite, which is a useful existing safeguard.

### Evidence

- [client/src/admin/pages/ToolRentalModeration.jsx:87](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/ToolRentalModeration.jsx:87) loads the queue, `:229` displays price, and `:265` implements pagination.
- [server/routes/admin.js:120](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/routes/admin.js:120) requires global `manage_operations` for rental moderation.
- [server/controllers/businessModerationController.js:118](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/businessModerationController.js:118) lists rentals; `:155` conditionally toggles availability and `:182` records the optional reason.
- [server/migrations/039_user_skills_and_tool_rentals.sql:27](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/migrations/039_user_skills_and_tool_rentals.sql:27) defines listing rates, deposits, local location, and availability.
- [server/__tests__/toolRentals.test.js:48](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/__tests__/toolRentals.test.js:48) verifies owner availability updates preserve monetary fields with mocked queries; it does not test administrator-hide persistence.
- [server/routes/toolRentals.js:20](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/routes/toolRentals.js:20) imports existing booking/review/invoice capabilities; `:128` defines the owner-attestation version and `:137` validates monetary input.

### Findings

**T19-F01 — High [confirmed defect]: owners can reverse an administrative hide through ordinary availability.** The Hide/Show handler at [businessModerationController.js:155](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/businessModerationController.js:155) changes `is_available`, also used by public visibility. The owner update at [server/routes/toolRentals.js:483](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/routes/toolRentals.js:483) checks ownership but permits that same Boolean to be reset at `:523`; public browse requires availability at `:322`. Thus a hidden owner listing can be made visible through the normal update API, without a separate moderation hold. This is a current cross-path enforcement defect established from source, not a production exploit performed here. Preserve ordinary availability but separate administrator moderation state.

**T19-F02 — Medium [code observation]: list pagination exists but its server budget is unbounded.** The controller parses caller-supplied page and limit at `:120` without a maximum, applies substring search at `:124`, and orders by creation timestamp at `:141` without a unique tie-breaker. The client’s normal 25-row pager does not constrain direct API calls. Deep offsets and count costs need representative query-plan verification before six-million-user readiness is claimed.

**T19-F03 — High [code observation]: reason enforcement and audit durability are inconsistent.** The dialog requires text, but `:158` accepts an absent reason and the audit failure is caught at `:184`. An operator can therefore change listing availability through the API without the explanation demanded by the screen. Existing moderation does not expose a durable review owner, expiry, appeal outcome, or restoration checklist for this action. These are additions to the current queue, not evidence that rental management is missing.

**T19-F04 — Medium [design requirement]: global equipment operations need locale and regional context.** The client uses a dollar prefix at [ToolRentalModeration.jsx:229](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/ToolRentalModeration.jsx:229), hard-coded availability wording at `:237`, and an unlabeled icon close control at `:39`. Global `manage_operations` cannot be safely substituted for country-specific recall authority. Currency provenance and booking-impact context must be explicit before operators communicate deposit or rental consequences across markets.

### Expansion scenario

A manufacturer recalls a generator model in three countries while 700 affected listings have future bookings. A local team must hide unsafe listings immediately without describing ordinary owner downtime as a recall, changing deposits blindly, or cancelling unrelated rentals. Headquarters needs a reproducible affected-list snapshot, and the next shift needs to distinguish restored equipment from pending evidence review.

### Recommended controls and UX

Add a moderation status/reason/version alongside existing owner availability, and calculate effective public visibility from both. Use existing bookings to show affected reservations, settlement state, and contact tasks before action; do not automatically infer refunds from a visibility change. Require API reasons and durable action audit, create scoped recall cases with owners and review deadlines, and support appeal/restoration evidence. Bound limits at 100, add stable cursor ordering and indexed model/category/location filters, and provide asynchronous batch preview, execution, cancellation, and per-item conflict reports. Show currency codes and provider-supported monetary handling, localize dates and text, label dialog controls, and preserve keyboard focus after mutations. Jurisdiction and peer-rental agreement changes require separate specialist review, not an engineering claim of approval.

### Dependencies and owner

Rental Trust/Safety owns recall decisions; booking/payment owners determine reservation and deposit effects; Identity supplies target scope; marketplace Data/SRE owns indexed lookup and job throttling; localization/accessibility owners validate operator interactions.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

An owner update cannot clear an administrator hold; ordinary availability changes do not erase hold reasons. All moderation calls require structured reasons and generate durable audit entries. Regional operators fail direct-ID cross-region tests. A 100,000-listing recall preview runs as a bounded job with resumable item results, not one browser request. List p95 stays below one second at agreed peak staff load, and five supported locales plus keyboard-only navigation complete both Hide and restore flows.

## 20. Planned Needs — /admin/planned-needs

### Mission and what exists

Planned Needs administers upcoming recurring or prearranged work before publication. The implemented screen lists schedules, exposes status and search controls, and offers cancellation of automatic activation or force cancellation with confirmation. It correctly warns that cancelling a planned need does not cancel an already published job. A date-only parsing helper avoids one common client timezone shift. The existing scheduler publishes work transactionally and checks for an already linked job, so the proper direction is hardening this lifecycle, not inventing another recurring-work engine.

### Evidence

- [client/src/admin/pages/PlannedNeedsAdmin.jsx:24](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/PlannedNeedsAdmin.jsx:24) handles date-only values; `:74` explains linked-job limitations; `:134` fetches the queue and `:155` submits reasons.
- [server/routes/admin.js:124](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/routes/admin.js:124) protects the moderation endpoints with `manage_operations`.
- [server/controllers/businessModerationController.js:194](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/businessModerationController.js:194) lists needs; `:231` cancels auto activation and `:268` force-cancels nonterminal needs.
- [server/migrations/156_planned_needs_pause.sql:1](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/migrations/156_planned_needs_pause.sql:1) introduces `is_paused` and a partial supporting index.
- [server/jobs/plannedNeedsScheduler.js:32](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/jobs/plannedNeedsScheduler.js:32) promotes impending work, `:45` selects publication candidates, and `:64` locks individual needs during publication.

### Findings

**T20-F01 — High [confirmed defect]: cancelling automatic activation does not establish a durable pause.** The action at [businessModerationController.js:231](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/businessModerationController.js:231) moves `activating_soon` back to `planned` but does not set `is_paused`. The scheduler at [plannedNeedsScheduler.js:32](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/jobs/plannedNeedsScheduler.js:32) can promote unpaused planned records again, and publication selection at `:45` includes planned records when due. An existing pause field therefore is not used by this administrative cancellation contract. The success response is stronger than the durable behavior.

**T20-F02 — High [confirmed defect]: cancellation can race a previously selected publication candidate.** The scheduler selects candidates before opening each item transaction. Its locked re-read at `:64` checks ID and `published_job_id`, but does not revalidate cancelled status or pause before inserting a job at `:98`. A force cancellation committed after candidate selection but before that lock can still be followed by publication. The transaction prevents duplicate publication in one important case; it does not protect this lifecycle transition.

**T20-F03 — Medium [confirmed defect]: queue errors resemble an empty workload.** At [PlannedNeedsAdmin.jsx:134](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/PlannedNeedsAdmin.jsx:134), failed loading logs an error and sets `needs` to an empty array rather than retaining an actionable error state. Requests lack the cancellation/generation protection found in other moderation pages. Search responses arriving out of order can therefore replace current results, and a failing queue can be read as no pending work. This is an operational visibility defect, not proof of a present server outage.

**T20-F04 — Medium [design requirement]: publication throughput needs explicit budgets and ownership.** The publication candidate query at [plannedNeedsScheduler.js:45](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/jobs/plannedNeedsScheduler.js:45) has no batch limit; the administrative controller also does not cap caller page size. The queue needs regional authorization, automation ownership, and clear scheduled-time semantics before worldwide delegated operation. Neither the existing per-item transaction nor healthy worker readiness establishes catch-up performance after an outage.

### Expansion scenario

Severe weather shuts down outdoor work in six regions shortly before local publication time. Dispatchers cancel thousands of upcoming needs while the scheduler already holds a candidate snapshot. A newly published job can still notify a helper despite a successful cancellation message. At the next timezone boundary, previously reverted but unpaused records become eligible again, creating repeated manual intervention and customer confusion.

### Recommended controls and UX

Define separate pause, cancel, and linked-job cancellation semantics using the existing fields. Revalidate status, pause, due time, and version under the same locked transaction that creates the job; use guarded updates and an idempotent publication identity. Return explicit transition results and linked-job IDs, with authorized navigation to the separate job workflow. Require API reasons and durable audit. Preserve stale results with a visible load error and retry, cancel superseded fetches, and display local due time with an explicit IANA zone and UTC detail. Introduce bounded worker batches, leases, retry/backoff, backlog age metrics, and scoped asynchronous emergency actions with dry-run counts and item outcomes. Provide accessible confirmation text distinguishing pause from irreversible cancellation.

### Dependencies and owner

Work Planning owns lifecycle policy; Dispatch owns regional shutdown decisions; scheduler/API owners own atomic publication; Jobs owns already-published work; SRE owns backlog SLOs and safe worker concurrency.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

An isolated database race test cancels between candidate selection and row lock and proves no job is created. Cancel-auto remains paused across two scheduler cycles unless deliberately resumed. Duplicate workers produce exactly one publication. A 50,000-item catch-up uses bounded batches and reports age, failures, and owner. Queue failures are visible, stale searches cannot replace current results, regional direct-ID mutations are rejected, and date-boundary tests cover DST and non-US timezones.

## 21. Payments — /admin/payments

### Mission and what exists

Payments is an implemented financial investigation list showing payer/payee, job linkage, amount, fees, status, and timestamps. It supports paged status/date filtering, cancellable requests, permission/error messaging, and retry. Its backend caps page size and uses inclusive end-date semantics. Importantly, the surrounding refund system already has durable attempts, idempotency, reserved amounts, provider reconciliation, and monotonic accounting. Those capabilities must be surfaced to operators; describing the system as having no safe refund machinery would be inaccurate.

### Evidence

- [client/src/admin/pages/PaymentsList.jsx:34](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/PaymentsList.jsx:34) loads paged filters; `:120` renders error/retry; `:193` renders financial records; `:25` formats USD.
- [server/controllers/paymentsAdminController.js:6](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/paymentsAdminController.js:6) bounds pagination and `:21` selects the payment list projection.
- [server/routes/admin.js:134](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/routes/admin.js:134) requires `view_financials`; [server/routes/payments.js:33](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/routes/payments.js:33) additionally protects refunds with `issue_refunds` and limiting.
- [server/services/paymentRefundService.js:57](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/services/paymentRefundService.js:57) reserves refunds under locks, `:137` settles monotonic totals, and `:213` uses provider idempotency.
- [server/migrations/597_payment_refund_reconciliation.sql:21](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/migrations/597_payment_refund_reconciliation.sql:21) persists attempt state; [server/__tests__/paymentRefundReconciliation.test.js:32](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/__tests__/paymentRefundReconciliation.test.js:32) covers idempotency and `:38` cumulative partial refunds.

### Findings

**T21-F01 — High [code observation]: investigation presentation omits essential recovery state.** The list projection at [paymentsAdminController.js:21](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/paymentsAdminController.js:21) exposes payment/provider references but not durable refund-attempt status, reserved totals, reconciliation age, or a case owner. The UI at [PaymentsList.jsx:193](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/PaymentsList.jsx:193) cannot distinguish a safely reserved pending refund from a settled cumulative refund or a provider outcome awaiting local recovery. This is an operator-contract gap atop existing recovery services, not a claim that reconciliation itself is absent.

**T21-F02 — High [code observation]: the list assumes currency without presenting provenance.** [PaymentsList.jsx:25](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/PaymentsList.jsx:25) formats every amount as USD, while the list projection does not include currency. Refund state processing at [paymentRefundStateService.js:24](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/services/paymentRefundStateService.js:24) already handles provider monetary evidence. The screen must either prove and label its USD-only product boundary or carry authoritative currency and minor-unit semantics. Expanding to other markets without that contract risks misleading support and reconciliation, even if the current product intentionally charges USD.

**T21-F03 — High [design requirement]: worldwide financial delegation needs narrower visibility.** The global `view_financials` grant correctly excludes regional-only grants, but a delegated global reader receives payer/payee details for all matching records. Read-finance, initiate-refund, reconcile, export, and approve-adjustment need independent scoped responsibilities. Display filters must not be mistaken for enforced tenant or regional authorization. Masked search results and just-in-time sensitive detail access would support routine local investigations without broad exposure.

**T21-F04 — Medium [verification gap]: bounded pages do not prove large-ledger query capacity.** The implementation caps limits at 100 and page at one million, which deserves credit. However, [paymentsAdminController.js:21](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/paymentsAdminController.js:21) uses offset paging ordered by creation time alone, and `:41` performs a matching joined count. Deep history, timestamp ties, and concurrent inserts require deterministic pagination and realistic cardinality tests. No measured p95 or production query plan is claimed here.

### Expansion scenario

A provider timeout interrupts a partial refund during the European support shift; a delayed webhook arrives after a US analyst opens the same payment. The refund service can preserve financial correctness, yet an operator seeing only the original status and amount may retry through the wrong workflow or promise an incorrect resolution time. Regional access, cumulative totals, and recovery ownership matter as much as the provider response.

### Recommended controls and UX

Extend the existing payment detail/investigation path with authorized attempt timelines, pending reservations, reconciled totals, provider event identifiers, last error category, and a designated recovery owner. Do not expose raw provider payloads or sensitive credentials. Use stable idempotency keys for retries and make uncertain outcomes read-only until reconciliation determines eligibility. Present currency codes, localized formatting, explicit timezone, and settled versus pending amounts. Add target-scoped finance policies, masked lists, reasoned reveal/export, and dual review for defined high-value adjustments. Adopt cursor ordering, indexed status/date/provider lookup, bounded asynchronous exports, and reconciliation-age alerts. Provider availability, supported markets, refund rules, and legal obligations require separate provider/jurisdiction review.

### Dependencies and owner

Finance Operations owns case resolution and adjustment policy; Payments owns provider state and idempotency; Identity owns scoped permissions; Security owns sensitive-data exposure; SRE/Data owns ledger query and reconciliation-age monitoring.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

Timeout/retry/webhook integration tests reconcile exactly one financial outcome and show its attempt timeline. Two analysts cannot refund beyond remaining eligible cents. Every displayed amount has verified currency semantics. Regional readers cannot retrieve foreign-region records or exports. At representative retained ledger volume, first-page and provider-reference lookup p95 remain below one second; exports are asynchronous and audited. Recovery cases beyond the agreed SLA have an owner, alert, and documented safe next action.

## 22. Orders — /admin/orders

### Mission and what exists

Orders provides a real marketplace operations screen with list filters, totals, paging, detail dialogs, shipping, cancellation, and refund actions. The client uses cancellable query-backed loading and explicit error presentation. Server list parameters have bounded pagination, parameterized search, and an allowlisted sort. The customer-search count uses matching joins, and its focused regression test exists; this report does not recycle the historical count defect. Marketplace refund recovery and database state-transition guards are also implemented, so recommendations should improve their operational use rather than introduce competing order or refund systems.

### Evidence

- [client/src/admin/pages/OrderManagement.jsx:25](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/OrderManagement.jsx:25) loads orders/stats; `:70` handles errors and `:175` renders pagination.
- [server/routes/adminOrders.js:8](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/routes/adminOrders.js:8) requires `manage_operations`; `:23` additionally requires `issue_refunds`.
- [server/controllers/adminOrdersController.js:10](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/adminOrdersController.js:10) bounds list parameters, `:116` handles detail, `:229` calls the transaction helper, and `:339` repeats that call pattern for cancellation.
- [server/utils/transaction.js:39](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/utils/transaction.js:39) defines the helper signature as `(pool, operation, name)`.
- [server/migrations/603_marketplace_order_state_integrity.sql:18](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/migrations/603_marketplace_order_state_integrity.sql:18) guards transitions; [server/__tests__/adminOrdersController.test.js:10](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/__tests__/adminOrdersController.test.js:10) tests repaired customer-search counting.

### Findings

**T22-F01 — High [confirmed defect]: shipping and cancellation call the transaction helper incorrectly.** [adminOrdersController.js:229](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/adminOrdersController.js:229) supplies only an async callback to the imported helper, while [transaction.js:39](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/utils/transaction.js:39) expects a pool first and calls `pool.connect()` at `:40`. Cancellation at [adminOrdersController.js:339](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/adminOrdersController.js:339) has the same mismatch. With this implementation, the callback is treated as the pool and cannot supply `connect`. This is deterministic source evidence of an essential mutation-path defect, not a claim that a production shipment was attempted during this audit. The list-focused test does not exercise either mutation.

**T22-F02 — High [confirmed defect]: completed refund cases cannot be investigated through normal detail.** The GET detail handler at [adminOrdersController.js:116](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/adminOrdersController.js:116) returns 409 for refunded orders. Blocking new financial mutation may be appropriate, but blocking read-only detail prevents operators from reviewing an already refunded order, its shipment, and the reason for resolution. The list and refund-service safeguards should be accompanied by immutable case inspection, not disappearance of resolved financial context.

**T22-F03 — Medium [code observation]: shipment changes lack an explicit stale-editor contract.** The shipment handler reads the order at `:230` without a row lock and updates it at `:245` without an expected version. Migration 603 supplies important terminal-state protection; it does not make two operators' tracking edits conflict visibly or preserve the client’s observed state. After repairing the helper, this path needs transactional transition validation and a versioned mutation response rather than assuming the database trigger covers every operational race.

**T22-F04 — Medium [design requirement]: operational scale needs scoped, asynchronous exception handling.** Existing bounded lists are valuable, but deep offsets, synchronous per-order work, and broad global operations authority are not a regional incident workflow. Thousands of cancellations, address issues, or carrier exceptions require item-level ownership and safe batch processing. Performance, bulk recovery, and cross-region denial remain acceptance work, not assertions of current downtime.

### Expansion scenario

A hurricane closes a fulfillment region with 20,000 pending or partially shipped orders. Local supervisors need to cancel eligible unpaid work, route paid orders to the existing refund process, and preserve carrier evidence for orders already resolved.
