Headquarters and a night shift inspect the same case. A broken cancellation call halts triage, while refusing refunded detail obscures the financial history needed to explain the outcome.

### Recommended controls and UX

Repair and directly test both transaction-helper callers before expanding operational access. Lock the target order, enforce an explicit state transition, accept an expected version, and return a conflict with current state and a readable diff. Keep refund idempotency and terminal guards; make refunded detail readable with all mutations appropriately disabled. Add carrier/tracking validation, durable before/after audit, scoped case assignment, age/SLA indicators, and an authorized timeline joining existing refunds and shipments. Provide asynchronous scoped batch preview with paid/unpaid eligibility, resumable jobs, and per-order outcomes; never implement cancellation as an implicit refund. Use stable pagination, audited exports, localized currency/date semantics, and accessible dialogs. Provider and consumer-policy decisions remain specialist approvals.

### Dependencies and owner

Marketplace Operations owns fulfillment exceptions; Orders API owns transaction and version contracts; Payments owns refunds; Identity owns regional delegation; SRE/Data owns large-order query budgets and batch reliability.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

Database-backed shipping/cancellation tests pass through the real helper. Invalid and terminal transitions are rejected, concurrent tracking edits return a conflict, and refunded detail remains readable. Two retries cannot duplicate a refund or state change. A 20,000-order incident job resumes after worker restart and reports each outcome. Regional direct-ID and export access tests deny out-of-scope records, and normal filtered lists meet a proposed one-second p95 at retained production-like volume.

## 23. Templates — /admin/templates

### Mission and what exists

Templates is an implemented administration workspace for template preview, pricing, access grants, revocation, and usage statistics. The client includes grant search, paged grant loading, pricing editors, confirmation flows, and template previews. Individual and bulk pricing endpoints already exist; bulk pricing uses a transaction. The server intentionally treats acquisition as monthly access rather than the former lifetime-purchase path. Enterprise controls should consolidate those current entitlement semantics, preserve legitimate existing access, and improve release governance rather than create another template marketplace.

### Evidence

- [client/src/admin/pages/TemplateManagement.jsx:60](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/TemplateManagement.jsx:60) implements preview dialogs; `:464` uses one-based grant paging and `:497` keeps pricing errors actionable.
- [server/routes/templates.js:28](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/routes/templates.js:28) protects pricing with `manage_settings`; `:31` protects stats with `manage_operations`; `:34`, `:43`, and `:46` protect grants only with admin authentication.
- [server/controllers/templateController.js:192](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/templateController.js:192) deprecates lifetime purchase, `:201` updates pricing, `:246` performs transactional bulk updates, and `:290` creates access grants.
- [client/scripts/adminAccessibilityContracts.test.mjs:38](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/scripts/adminAccessibilityContracts.test.mjs:38) checks template refresh/close labels statically, not focus behavior or entitlement mutation integrity.
- [server/controllers/templateController.js:345](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/templateController.js:345) revokes access and resets active templates; `:380` lists grants; `:457` aggregates usage.

### Findings

**T23-F01 — High [confirmed defect]: grant authority is less restricted than pricing authority.** Grant list, creation, and revocation routes at [templates.js:34](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/routes/templates.js:34), `:43`, and `:46` omit a permission check beyond admin identity. A general admin without `manage_settings` can still create or remove valuable entitlements by direct API call. Pricing and statistics have explicit grants, so this is a concrete inconsistency in the existing permission design, not an absence of authentication. Introduce a distinct entitlement-management responsibility with target scope.

**T23-F02 — High [confirmed defect]: revoking one access row resets profiles without evaluating remaining access.** [templateController.js:349](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/templateController.js:349) revokes the selected row, then `:361` and `:366` reset all matching business/helper active templates for that user. It does not check whether another valid grant or subscription still authorizes the same slug, and these separate writes are not one transaction. A promotion revocation can therefore disturb legitimate paid or overlapping access, while a partial failure can leave entitlement and presentation state inconsistent.

**T23-F03 — Medium [code observation]: pricing and grant changes lack mature validation/versioning.** Pricing updates at `:201` accept incoming monetary fields without the controller proving strict integer bounds or an expected version. Grant creation at `:290` checks grant types and required dates, but does not establish valid ordered date intervals. Existing database constraints may reject some invalid values; they do not replace actionable field validation. Bulk updates at `:246` lack an explicit array budget, preview, or per-item decision report.

**T23-F04 — Medium [code observation]: business semantics and discovery can mislead operators.** Lifetime pricing remains editable in [TemplateManagement.jsx:223](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/TemplateManagement.jsx:223) although the purchase path at [templateController.js:192](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/templateController.js:192) declares monthly semantics. The grant-entry interface suggests ID/email entry at [TemplateManagement.jsx:310](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/TemplateManagement.jsx:310), while submission at `:291` forwards `user_id` unchanged and [server/controllers/templateController.js:315](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/templateController.js:315) queries only the user identifier. Server grant paging exists, but usage aggregation at [templateController.js:457](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/templateController.js:457) counts active rows without demonstrating effective expiry semantics. Labels and metrics must match the entitlement resolver.

### Expansion scenario

A global campaign grants 80,000 trial extensions while enterprise customers retain paid access. One coordinator revokes a mistaken trial grant and another adjusts regional pricing. Resetting the customer’s active template despite a still-valid paid entitlement creates an avoidable commercial incident; stale price updates can silently overwrite each other. Support needs the effective entitlement explanation, not only a list of grant rows.

### Recommended controls and UX

Reuse the existing access model but centralize effective entitlement evaluation. Revoke transactionally, then reset presentation only when no valid remaining entitlement permits it. Require scoped entitlement permissions, reason codes, durable audit, expected versions, and validated date intervals. Distinguish monthly price, historical lifetime terms, and grandfathered access visibly; resolve email searches through an authorized lookup instead of sending them as identifiers. Build price-change preview and scheduled publication with explicit currency, affected cohorts, review approval, and rollback to the previous version. Convert large grants and bulk price changes to bounded asynchronous jobs with deduplication, cancellation, and per-user outcomes. Preserve the current preview dialogs while verifying focus, translated labels, and permission-specific loading rather than failing the whole workspace when one panel is unauthorized.

### Dependencies and owner

Product Monetization owns entitlement policy; Templates API owns effective access and transactions; Finance reviews pricing treatment; Identity owns delegation; Customer Success owns campaign exceptions and recovery communication.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

An admin lacking entitlement authority cannot grant or revoke through direct calls. Revoking an overlapping trial preserves valid paid access; injected transaction failure leaves no partial reset. Invalid cents/date ranges return field errors and stale prices return 409. An 80,000-grant job resumes without duplicates. Usage metrics match effective entitlements, ID/email searches behave as labeled, and pricing terms are unambiguous across supported locales.

## 24. Consulting — /admin/consulting

### Mission and what exists

Consulting provides an existing operational dashboard with request discovery, status/tier/search controls, manual intake, consultant assignment, notes, and confirmation dialogs. It fetches request, consultant, and aggregate data with cancellation support. The server can filter requests and maintains consultant records with active-work counts. This is a working intake/assignment framework, not a missing consulting module. Enterprise expansion should join this dashboard to the existing scheduling, sessions, and payment capabilities so ownership and delivery evidence follow a case throughout its lifecycle.

### Evidence

- [client/src/admin/pages/ConsultingDashboard.jsx:364](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/ConsultingDashboard.jsx:364) requests the latest 200 items and performs local filtering; `:213` changes status, `:230` assigns, and `:243` patches notes.
- [server/routes/consultingAdmin.js:7](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/routes/consultingAdmin.js:7) defines admin authentication; `:13` through `:19` expose request and assignment operations without additional permission grants.
- [server/controllers/adminConsultingController.js:16](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/adminConsultingController.js:16) implements server request filters; `:62` assigns consultants; `:101` lists consultant workload.
- [server/controllers/adminConsultingController.js:296](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/adminConsultingController.js:296) updates details and `:526` handles status changes, including a status allowlist at `:534` and audit at `:566`.
- [server/migrations/103_consulting_all_phases.sql:36](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/migrations/103_consulting_all_phases.sql:36) adds consultant assignment and `:46` introduces scheduled appointments.

### Findings

**T24-F01 — High [confirmed defect]: local filtering conceals older requests.** [ConsultingDashboard.jsx:364](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/ConsultingDashboard.jsx:364) fetches only 200 records and filters that array by search, tier, and status. There is no complete queue paging in this dashboard. A pending lead older than the newest 200 requests is invisible even if the server supports the desired filter. At expansion volume this is a queue-completeness failure, not merely slow rendering; the screen can suggest no matching workload while undisplayed work exists.

**T24-F02 — High [code observation]: ordinary admin identity grants broad consulting mutation.** [consultingAdmin.js:13](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/routes/consultingAdmin.js:13)–`:19` require authentication and `requireAdmin`, but no request-read, assignment, or transition permission. Financial routes elsewhere have grants, showing that granular enforcement exists but is not applied here. Neither UI filtering nor consultant selection establishes tenant/country scope. The issue is least privilege for internal administration, not unauthenticated access.

**T24-F03 — High [code observation]: status and notes are vulnerable to stale decisions.** The controller reads then updates status without expected-version matching at [adminConsultingController.js:526](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/adminConsultingController.js:526), despite validating the enum. Notes submitted by the client at [ConsultingDashboard.jsx:243](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/ConsultingDashboard.jsx:243) are patched as a field, and detail update at [adminConsultingController.js:296](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/adminConsultingController.js:296) replaces the value rather than appending an immutable note event. Concurrent coordinators can overwrite case context or apply a decision based on an obsolete assignment.

**T24-F04 — Medium [design requirement]: active-work counts are not staffing availability or SLA control.** Consultant listing at [adminConsultingController.js:101](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/adminConsultingController.js:101) counts pending/scheduled work; assignment at `:62` does not use the separate availability/blocked-time booking rules. A global workforce needs language, expertise, capacity, regional operating hours, backup ownership, and escalation. The existing consultant model is the appropriate foundation, but an active-count badge is insufficient proof that a consultant can accept a specific appointment.

### Expansion scenario

A launch campaign generates 50,000 consultation requests over a weekend. Older urgent leads disappear from the dashboard’s newest-200 slice. The overnight team assigns apparently underloaded consultants who are unavailable in the client’s timezone, while two coordinators replace the same notes. Monday leadership sees aggregate activity but cannot identify which uncontacted clients have exceeded the promised response time.

### Recommended controls and UX

Use server-side filter/search with bounded cursor paging and honest total/backlog indicators. Add independent scoped permissions for intake reads, assignment, and status mutation. Accept expected versions, return current-state conflict diffs, and convert notes to append-only attributed events with correction links. Define an explicit transition graph and preserve audit with the mutation. Connect assignment to existing availability, appointment duration, expertise/language, and workload reservations; do not create a second availability engine. Add assigned owner/team, first-response deadline, escalation, workload aging, handoff notes, and reassignment reasons. Offer reviewed asynchronous campaign actions with dry-run counts, bounded jobs, and per-case outcomes. Localize timestamps and statuses and ensure modal focus and keyboard navigation remain usable in a densely populated queue.

### Dependencies and owner

Consulting Operations owns intake SLAs and skill routing; scheduling owners provide reservable capacity; Identity owns scoped permissions; consulting API owners provide transition/version contracts; analytics owners define truthful backlog measures.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

An old pending case remains discoverable after 50,000 newer requests. Scoped coordinators cannot read or assign another region’s case. Simultaneous edits produce 409 rather than losing a note or assignment. Unavailable consultants cannot be reserved through dashboard shortcuts. Every open request has an owner and due time, overdue work escalates within the agreed interval, and server-filtered queue p95 stays below one second at representative retained volume.

## 25. Consulting Pay — /admin/consulting/payments

### Mission and what exists

Consulting Pay combines package management, purchase/invoice history, and manual invoice creation. The UI validates package input, supports active-state toggles, and uses optimistic rollback for some failures. The payment API already validates session ownership, prevents competing active payments, uses deterministic provider idempotency, verifies actual provider amount/currency/success, and integrates durable external-refund recovery. Database migrations and focused payment tests support these repairs. The enterprise question is whether staff can safely administer and investigate those existing financial flows, not whether consulting payments exist.

### Evidence

- [client/src/admin/pages/ConsultingPayments.jsx:9](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/ConsultingPayments.jsx:9) defines invoice tier defaults, `:36` formats USD, `:126` submits manual invoices, and `:205` loads the workspace.
- [server/routes/consultingAdmin.js:22](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/routes/consultingAdmin.js:22) permits invoice generation with `view_financials`; `:27` protects package editing only with admin authentication.
- [server/controllers/adminConsultingController.js:619](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/adminConsultingController.js:619) limits purchases to 100, `:638` limits invoices to 200, and `:663` generates manual invoices.
- [server/controllers/consultingPaymentController.js:26](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/consultingPaymentController.js:26) guards payment creation, `:150` verifies provider confirmation, and `:272` authorizes refunds by role; [server/routes/consultingPayment.js:15](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/routes/consultingPayment.js:15) has authentication/limiting but no refund grant.
- [server/__tests__/consultingPaymentStateMigration.test.js:12](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/__tests__/consultingPaymentStateMigration.test.js:12) checks terminal-trigger SQL text; this is not a restored-data payment rehearsal.
- [server/migrations/599_consulting_payment_terminal_integrity.sql:26](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/migrations/599_consulting_payment_terminal_integrity.sql:26) protects terminal state; [server/__tests__/ws3-consulting-chat.test.js:70](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/__tests__/ws3-consulting-chat.test.js:70) exercises repaired payment creation contracts.

### Findings

**T25-F01 — High [confirmed defect]: financial responsibilities are not consistently separated.** Invoice creation uses the read-oriented `view_financials` grant, package changes require only admin identity, and consulting refunds rely on a role check at [consultingPaymentController.js:272](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/consultingPaymentController.js:272) rather than the `issue_refunds` plus `requireAdmin` boundary used by ordinary payments. This permits an admin without that refund grant to reach consulting refund logic. Durable refund recovery remains valuable, but correctness of provider execution does not establish authority to initiate it.

**T25-F02 — High [confirmed defect]: manual invoice lifecycle loses recipient provenance.** The controller receives name/email/tier at [adminConsultingController.js:664](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/adminConsultingController.js:664), but the INSERT at `:689` stores none of those recipient/package fields. The email call uses transient request data and marks the invoice sent before delivery, with failures merely logged at `:702`. After reload, manual invoices without a linked user cannot reconstruct the intended recipient from the normal user join. The UI at [ConsultingPayments.jsx:126](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/ConsultingPayments.jsx:126) also does not supply the optional idempotency key supported by the controller, leaving routine retries unprotected by that mechanism.

**T25-F03 — Medium [confirmed defect]: administrative package discovery excludes inactive products.** The workspace at [ConsultingPayments.jsx:205](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/ConsultingPayments.jsx:205) loads the public packages endpoint, which returns only active packages ([consultingPaymentController.js:7](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/consultingPaymentController.js:7)). Once disabled and reloaded, a package disappears from the administrative list and cannot be reenabled there. Additionally, the editor catch at [ConsultingPayments.jsx:227](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/ConsultingPayments.jsx:227) does not propagate save failure like its toggle handler, allowing the dialog to close despite unsuccessful persistence.

**T25-F04 — Medium [code observation]: history and invoice amounts are incomplete operational contracts.** Purchase/invoice limits of 100/200 have no cursor or complete search, so older financial cases disappear. Manual invoice options use hardcoded tier prices at [ConsultingPayments.jsx:9](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/ConsultingPayments.jsx:9) rather than authoritative current package/version data. USD formatting must be labeled as a supported product constraint or generalized with verified currency; global expansion does not itself authorize another provider or accounting regime.

### Expansion scenario

An enterprise onboarding campaign generates 5,000 manual invoices. Email delivery fails for one region, and coordinators retry creation after browser timeouts. The saved records cannot reliably reveal the intended recipients, old invoices drop out of history, and disabled campaign packages vanish from management. A read-finance employee should investigate these outcomes without automatically possessing invoice-creation or refund authority.

### Recommended controls and UX

Add scoped create-invoice, manage-package, initiate-refund, and read-finance permissions with consistent active-admin/MFA enforcement. Persist recipient snapshot, package/version, currency, delivery state, and a mandatory client idempotency key. Use a delivery outbox with retry ownership and distinguish generated, queued, delivered, paid, and failed states. Provide an admin package endpoint including inactive entries; retain failed editors and show field errors. Page/search full history and expose existing payment/refund attempt timelines. Resolve invoice pricing from the selected authoritative version, with explicit authorized overrides and reasons. Require provider-supported currency and market review separately from engineering approval, and support accessible localized financial dialogs.

### Dependencies and owner

Finance Operations owns invoice/refund authority; Consulting Monetization owns package versions; Payments owns provider recovery; Messaging owns delivery evidence; Identity and Security own consistent grant/MFA enforcement.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

Read-only finance cannot generate invoices or refund through direct APIs. Retrying the same invoice creates one record and preserves its recipient after reload. Delivery failures are visible and retryable without recreating the invoice. Disabled packages remain administratively editable. Cases beyond the first 200 rows are searchable. Real-database/provider-sandbox race tests confirm one active payment and monotonic refund state, while browser acceptance verifies failure dialogs, localization, and permission boundaries.

## 26. Scheduling — /admin/consulting/scheduler

### Mission and what exists

Scheduling presents an existing consulting calendar with assignment, scheduling, cancellation, reminder, and invitation actions. It loads requests and consultants with cancellation support and provides manual session creation. Separately, the server already implements consultant availability, blocked dates, exceptions, ownership checks, platform approval, slot validation, and overlap-aware booking. That distinction is important: enterprise readiness requires routing administrative scheduling through the protected booking contract, not building a second scheduler or claiming availability does not exist.

### Evidence

- [client/src/admin/pages/ConsultingScheduler.jsx:19](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/ConsultingScheduler.jsx:19) defines fixed hours, `:65` renders calendar events, `:102` submits schedules, and `:340` loads only 200 requests.
- [server/controllers/adminConsultingController.js:526](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/adminConsultingController.js:526) patches request status and scheduled timestamp through the generic administrative path.
- [server/controllers/consultantSchedulingController.js:207](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/consultantSchedulingController.js:207) validates booking inputs, `:236` checks ownership, `:257` checks consultant approval, `:280` validates availability, and `:293` guards overlap in an update.
- [server/migrations/107_consultant_availability.sql:66](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/migrations/107_consultant_availability.sql:66) defines available-slot computation and the same migration adds timezone/availability infrastructure.
- [server/routes/consultingAdmin.js:38](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/routes/consultingAdmin.js:38) maps send-invite to the reminder handler; [server/controllers/adminConsultingController.js:815](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/adminConsultingController.js:815) implements that reminder.

### Findings

**T26-F01 — High [confirmed defect]: administrative scheduling bypasses the existing booking validation.** [ConsultingScheduler.jsx:110](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/ConsultingScheduler.jsx:110) PATCHes the generic request-status endpoint with `scheduled_at` and a selected consultant. [adminConsultingController.js:526](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/adminConsultingController.js:526) updates that parent record, rather than invoking the availability/blocked-date/approval and scheduled-child booking path at [consultantSchedulingController.js:207](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/consultantSchedulingController.js:207). A successful calendar operation therefore does not establish a valid reserved appointment. This is a source-level contract divergence, not proof that all public bookings are unsafe.

**T26-F02 — High [code observation]: displayed time has implicit operator-local semantics.** Fixed 09:00–17:00 options and date construction at [ConsultingScheduler.jsx:19](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/ConsultingScheduler.jsx:19) and `:102` lack an explicit scheduling-zone selector and duration control. The separate booking endpoint at [consultantSchedulingController.js:315](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/consultantSchedulingController.js:315) defaults missing timezone to America/New_York. A UTC timestamp alone is insufficient UX evidence of the consultant/client’s intended local appointment, particularly at DST transitions. Preserve the existing timezone field but make its interpretation explicit.

**T26-F03 — Medium [confirmed defect]: calendar completeness and invitation labeling overstate behavior.** Requests are capped at 200 before calendar placement ([ConsultingScheduler.jsx:340](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/ConsultingScheduler.jsx:340)), rather than queried by visible calendar window. Older records scheduled in the current week can be absent. The send-invite action at `:128` reaches a route mapped to `sendSessionReminder`, not a distinct calendar invitation/response lifecycle. Reminder delivery is a useful feature, but the UI must not imply accepted ICS/provider-calendar synchronization without implementing and verifying it.

**T26-F04 — High [verification gap]: overlap SQL is not proven safe under concurrent distinct appointments.** The existing conditional `NOT EXISTS` update at [consultantSchedulingController.js:293](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/consultantSchedulingController.js:293) rejects a visible overlap, but two transactions updating different target rows may both read no competing committed booking. A consultant-level lock or database exclusion/reservation invariant needs inspection and a real concurrent test. Do not call this a confirmed double-booking incident or dismiss the existing sequential conflict check.

### Expansion scenario

A London coordinator schedules a Sydney consultant for a New York client around a US daylight-saving change. Another coordinator books the same consultant through the dedicated booking endpoint. The calendar shortcut writes only the parent time, fixed work hours obscure the consultant’s actual zone, and a reminder labeled invite does not reveal whether the client accepted. The worldwide workforce needs one authoritative reservation and an explainable timezone conversion.

### Recommended controls and UX

Use the existing availability service for every administrative reservation with target-scoped assignment authority. Reserve consultant/time capacity transactionally, validate duration and both timezone offsets, and require an expected appointment version for moves/cancellations. Add explicit IANA consultant/client/display zones with UTC detail and DST ambiguity choices; derive hours from availability rather than fixed office defaults. Query bounded calendar windows and render all matching appointments through paged/virtualized results. Distinguish reminder from invitation; add provider-supported ICS lifecycle, delivery state, response tracking, cancellation identity, and retries only if that capability is approved. Provide keyboard-operable calendar events, labeled buttons, visible focus, an accessible agenda alternative, and translated time/status labels. Measure slot-query fanout at [consultantSchedulingController.js:167](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/consultantSchedulingController.js:167) before expanding bulk scheduling.

### Dependencies and owner

Scheduling API owns the reservation invariant; Consulting Operations owns capacity and reschedule policy; Messaging owns delivery; provider integration owners approve calendar capabilities; Identity owns scoped assignment; accessibility/localization owners validate agenda/calendar parity.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

Every calendar action reserves the same authoritative child appointment as public booking. Two concurrent distinct bookings for overlapping time yield one success and one conflict. DST tests cover nonexistent and repeated local times, and visible calendar windows include records older than the latest 200 requests. Invitations and reminders have accurate distinct labels and delivery states. Keyboard users can schedule, move, inspect, and cancel; regional staff cannot reserve unauthorized consultants or sessions.

## 27. Business Launch — /admin/consulting/business-launch

### Mission and what exists

Business Launch is an implemented consulting intake and referral workspace, with country/state/status/package/urgency/search filters, pagination, request detail, notes, messages, consultant assignment, and CSV export. The client uses abortable loading and explicit confirmations. Server assignment is transactional, checks contractor eligibility and referral limits, and records referral history; status changes include audit. These are meaningful capabilities. Expansion should strengthen query correctness, delegated ownership, capacity reservation, and founder communication rather than label the screen missing or create a separate launch CRM.

### Evidence

- [client/src/admin/pages/BusinessLaunchAdmin.jsx:96](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/BusinessLaunchAdmin.jsx:96) loads filtered pages; `:124` handles confirmations and `:144` exports the current page.
- [server/routes/businessLaunch.js:49](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/routes/businessLaunch.js:49) protects administration with `manage_operations`; `:109` requires `message_users` for custom messaging.
- [server/controllers/businessLaunchController.js:150](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/businessLaunchController.js:150) lists requests, `:204` projects joined rows, `:358` transactionally assigns contractors, and `:517` changes status.
- [server/controllers/businessLaunchController.js:396](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/businessLaunchController.js:396) checks referral capacity; `:534` reads existing state and `:557` writes audit in the status transaction.
- [server/migrations/155_consulting_admin_improvements.sql:59](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/migrations/155_consulting_admin_improvements.sql:59) expands launch-related statuses; [server/migrations/103_consulting_all_phases.sql:226](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/migrations/103_consulting_all_phases.sql:226) establishes consulting referral records.

### Findings

**T27-F01 — High [code observation]: the current joined list SQL contains unqualified shared columns.** The projection at [businessLaunchController.js:204](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/businessLaunchController.js:204) selects `id`, `email`, `first_name`, `last_name`, and timestamps without the `cr` prefix, while joining `users cu` at `:218`. Those names are shared by the request/user models and are therefore an ambiguity risk in the actual current statement, independent of previously repaired SQL elsewhere. This is source evidence requiring an isolated database regression test; no production list failure was reproduced and it must not be described as an observed live outage.

**T27-F02 — High [verification gap]: contractor capacity is checked without proving a shared reservation lock.** Assignment locks the request and validates active contractor acceptance, minimum price, and referral count at `:358`–`:406`. Two different requests can compete for the same contractor's final capacity slot because locking one request does not serialize the contractor-level aggregate. Existing transactional referral logging deserves credit; its capacity invariant needs a concurrent Postgres test and a contractor-level reservation strategy if it fails.

**T27-F03 — Medium [code observation]: stale status decisions and export completeness need clearer contracts.** Status mutation reads then writes without expected-version matching at [businessLaunchController.js:534](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/businessLaunchController.js:534), allowing stale decisions to overwrite a newer phase while audit references an old observation. The CSV at [BusinessLaunchAdmin.jsx:144](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/BusinessLaunchAdmin.jsx:144) exports only the displayed page and uses quoting without demonstrated spreadsheet-formula neutralization. The UI should label page-only export or offer a scoped full-result job, particularly for founder-supplied text.

**T27-F04 — High [design requirement]: country filters are not jurisdiction-scoped delegation.** Global `manage_operations` guards the endpoints, but selected country/state filters are caller input rather than authorization scope. Launch coordinators need bounded regional access, a responsible consultant/team, phase due dates, required evidence, and backup ownership. Setup-item labels are operational intake choices; they do not establish local legal authorization or completion of jurisdiction-specific obligations.

### Expansion scenario

Two launch managers in Kenya and Haiti compete for a contractor's last referral slot during a holiday surge. One changes urgency while another completes the case from stale detail. A regional coordinator exports founder names containing spreadsheet control characters to send an approved campaign. Correct referral reservations, complete export labeling, safe text handling, and an accountable local escalation chain are necessary even while the deployment remains healthy.

### Recommended controls and UX

Qualify every joined column and test all combined list filters against the actual migrated schema; do not reopen unrelated repaired SQL without evidence. Serialize capacity reservations on the contractor or a dedicated capacity row, preserving current transactional eligibility/referral checks. Add expected versions, permitted phase transitions, durable reasoned audit, owner/backup team, first-response and phase SLA, and overdue escalation. Enforce region/tenant predicates server-side for list, detail, assignment, messaging, and exports. Retain custom-message permission separation. Provide an asynchronous filtered export with immutable scope, safe CSV cell encoding, progress, expiration, and download audit. Show jurisdiction-dependent evidence checklists as pending specialist review, not automatic legal approval. Localize urgency/date labels and ensure detail dialogs are keyboard accessible.

### Dependencies and owner

Business Launch Operations owns phase outcomes; contractor operations owns capacity; API/Data owns query and version correctness; Identity owns target scope; Messaging owns delivery; jurisdiction specialists define required approvals and evidence independently of engineering.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

Migrated-schema list tests cover all joins and combined country/status/search filters. Concurrent assignments to one remaining capacity slot produce one reservation. Stale phase updates return 409. Regional users cannot access another country by changing filters or IDs. Full exports include every authorized match, neutralize formula-leading text, and run as bounded jobs. Every active launch case has an owner and due time, with escalation and evidence state visible to both shifts.

## 28. Consulting Sessions — /admin/consulting/sessions

### Mission and what exists

Consulting Sessions is a real paginated operations view with search/filter controls, session detail, manual creation, editing, completion, cancellation, no-show, reminders, follow-ups, and page export. Requests are cancellable and confirmation targets are explicit. The schema already distinguishes intake/session parents from scheduled appointment children and follow-up records. Payment creation checks those child appointments. Enterprise hardening must make this existing lifecycle consistent across screens and controllers, rather than invent an unrelated appointment or consulting-delivery system.

### Evidence

- [client/src/admin/pages/ConsultingSessionsAdmin.jsx:65](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/ConsultingSessionsAdmin.jsx:65) builds manual intake, `:107` posts creation, `:224` pages sessions, `:249` completes them, and `:282` exports displayed rows.
- [client/src/admin/pages/ConsultingSessionDetail.jsx:50](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/ConsultingSessionDetail.jsx:50) updates assignment; `:71` completes, `:92` marks no-show, and `:129` changes notes.
- [server/controllers/adminConsultingController.js:191](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/adminConsultingController.js:191) lists parent sessions, `:397` completes a scheduled child, `:442` marks child no-show, and `:488` creates a parent manually.
- [server/migrations/102_consulting_sessions.sql:2](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/migrations/102_consulting_sessions.sql:2) defines parents; [server/migrations/103_consulting_all_phases.sql:46](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/migrations/103_consulting_all_phases.sql:46) defines the scheduled child with a unique parent link; migration 106 adds follow-up records.
- [server/routes/consultingAdmin.js:30](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/routes/consultingAdmin.js:30)–`:38` exposes these actions with admin authentication but no separate session-operation grants.

### Findings

**T28-F01 — High [confirmed defect]: manual creation and completion target different required records.** Manual creation at [adminConsultingController.js:488](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/adminConsultingController.js:488) inserts the parent, including optional scheduled time, but not the child. Completion at `:403` searches `consulting_sessions_scheduled` and returns 404 when absent. A manually scheduled row shown in the parent list can consequently lack the object required for its offered completion action. The schema and payment path already provide the distinction; the administrative contract must respect it.

**T28-F02 — High [code observation]: completion/no-show do not update the state being listed.** The completion UPDATE at [adminConsultingController.js:415](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/adminConsultingController.js:415) and no-show UPDATE at `:460` modify only the child. The list at `:191` reads parent status. No synchronization mechanism was identified in the inspected lifecycle controllers/migrations. Thus the source path does not establish that the list reflects completion, and the unguarded child update can overwrite a terminal state. A real-database lifecycle test must verify all coupled states rather than assume an unseen deployment mechanism repairs them.

**T28-F03 — High [code observation]: assignment and note editing have weak identity/version contracts.** Detail assignment submits selected consultant IDs at [ConsultingSessionDetail.jsx:50](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/ConsultingSessionDetail.jsx:50), while the model contains both legacy consultant-record IDs and user UUID assignments. This requires explicit mapping tests, not an unsupported claim that every assignment fails. Generic details at [adminConsultingController.js:296](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/adminConsultingController.js:296) replace notes/fields without an expected version. Separate read, schedule, complete, no-show, and edit-note grants are absent from [consultingAdmin.js:30](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/routes/consultingAdmin.js:30).

**T28-F04 — Medium [design requirement]: package delivery and follow-up need measurable case ownership.** Migration 103's scheduled child has a unique parent link; packages can represent multiple units of consulting service. The operational model needs an explicit relationship between entitlement, consumed appointment, completion evidence, and follow-up owner. This is a design/verification requirement, not a claim that multiple scheduled children already exist for one parent. Page-only export and hard-coded detail labels further limit global handoff.

### Expansion scenario

A customer purchases a multi-session package. A coordinator manually schedules the next consultation, a specialist marks the previous appointment complete, and a night-shift agent records a no-show dispute. The parent queue, child appointment, payment eligibility, and remaining service entitlement must agree. Otherwise a completed session can remain actionable or a newly scheduled manual row cannot be completed at all, undermining SLA and customer explanations.

### Recommended controls and UX

Define one lifecycle service that atomically creates required appointments, validates transitions, synchronizes parent summary, and records completion/no-show evidence. Keep the existing tables but give each appointment a clear identity and entitlement-consumption policy. Map consultant records to user identities explicitly. Require scoped action permissions, expected versions, append-only notes, audit, and reviewable reversal reasons. Display appointment, parent case, remaining entitlement, follow-up owner/deadline, and delivery state separately. Make reminders/follow-ups retryable through existing delivery infrastructure without declaring them sent before evidence. Clearly label current-page export or supply an audited asynchronous full-result export. Translate detail statuses, show explicit zones, and test modal focus and confirmation wording for keyboard users.

### Dependencies and owner

Consulting Delivery owns completion evidence and no-show policy; session/scheduling API owners own atomic lifecycle; Payments owns entitlement/financial linkage; Messaging owns follow-up reliability; Identity owns scoped action grants.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

Manual scheduling followed by completion works against the migrated database and updates the list consistently. Cancelled/completed appointments cannot be overwritten by stale no-show calls. Consultant IDs map deterministically to the authorized user. Concurrent note edits do not lose history. Every package unit has an auditable appointment/consumption outcome, each follow-up has an owner and deadline, and multilingual keyboard-driven session workflows pass authenticated browser acceptance.

## 29. Org Chart — /admin/corporate/org-chart

### Mission and what exists

Org Chart is an implemented corporate directory view grouped by department and title seniority, with country selection and expandable department sections. The server already returns active assignments, title information, regional fields, reporting-manager references, and a country/global filter. Corporate hierarchy data therefore exists. The enterprise task is to render and delegate that information safely for 3,500 employees, including people with multiple assignments, rather than describe the org chart or corporate structure as absent.

### Evidence

- [client/src/admin/pages/super/CorporateOrgChart.jsx:35](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/super/CorporateOrgChart.jsx:35) groups assignments and expands departments; `:108` loads data; `:147` accepts country input.
- [server/routes/corporateRoles.js:21](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/routes/corporateRoles.js:21) and `:44` require `manage_admin_accounts` for catalog/org-chart access.
- [server/controllers/corporateRolesController.js:771](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/corporateRolesController.js:771) builds the org chart; `:796` sorts assignments; `:819` returns grouped and flat representations.
- [server/migrations/246_corporate_hierarchy.sql:474](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/migrations/246_corporate_hierarchy.sql:474) establishes corporate assignments, including country/region and reporting relationships.
- [server/services/corporateRolesService.js:105](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/services/corporateRolesService.js:105) resolves active title assignments, demonstrating an existing corporate authorization layer separate from basic user roles.

### Findings

**T29-F01 — High [design requirement]: directory reads are coupled to account-management authority.** Org-chart access requires `manage_admin_accounts`, a broad administrative responsibility, rather than a dedicated directory-read permission. Ordinary employees cannot simply use the admin-only endpoint even though corporate assignments can include employees. A global workforce needs selective directory visibility without encouraging account-management grants for staff who merely need an escalation contact. Public-versus-private fields, tenant boundaries, and regional emergency access must be explicit.

**T29-F02 — Medium [code observation]: the response and rendering are full-dataset oriented.** The controller at [corporateRolesController.js:771](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/corporateRolesController.js:771) returns all matching assignments without pagination and includes email/role detail. It returns the same records grouped and flat at `:819`, increasing payload and exposing unnecessary duplication. The client initially expands all departments at [CorporateOrgChart.jsx:35](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/super/CorporateOrgChart.jsx:35). Multiple titles per employee can increase cardinality well beyond headcount. This is a measured-load verification gap, not a demonstrated memory or latency incident.

**T29-F03 — Medium [confirmed defect]: country changes can display stale directory results.** Loading at [CorporateOrgChart.jsx:108](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/super/CorporateOrgChart.jsx:108) has no abort or generation guard, while the country text input at `:147` can start requests as characters change. A slower previous response can overwrite the latest country selection. The result is particularly misleading for an incident operator trying to locate the correct regional accountable owner. Server filtering does not prevent this client response-order error.

**T29-F04 — Medium [code observation]: hierarchy and accessible navigation are underused.** The API returns reporting references, but the UI at [CorporateOrgChart.jsx:35](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/super/CorporateOrgChart.jsx:35) presents seniority groups rather than a manager tree or validated escalation chain. Expand buttons at `:51` do not expose `aria-expanded`/control relationships. Title seniority is not equivalent to incident command, acting ownership, or availability; those must be represented as operational context rather than inferred from position order.

### Expansion scenario

During a regional service disruption, support staff need the acting logistics owner and backup for the affected country. Several employees hold global and local titles. A stale country response and a seniority-only list send the escalation to the wrong executive, while giving account-management access to everyone who needs a directory would broaden privileges far beyond that task.

### Recommended controls and UX

Add a dedicated scoped directory-read policy with minimal fields, masked contact details where appropriate, and separate privileged relationship editing. Reuse `reports_to_user_id` to render a validated manager hierarchy plus a flat searchable directory, explicitly marking multiple assignments, acting dates, vacant posts, and backup/on-call contacts from their authoritative systems. Abort superseded requests, debounce country selection, validate ISO codes, and show active scope while loading. Remove duplicate payload representations unless requested; page searchable people and virtualize large departments. Provide an accessible tree or table alternative with labeled expand controls, keyboard navigation, localized title descriptions, RTL layout, and explicit timezone/availability. Directory export should be authorized, audited, scoped, and asynchronous rather than a global employee-data download.

### Dependencies and owner

People Operations owns reporting accuracy; Identity owns directory versus management authority; corporate API owners own hierarchy validation and response shape; incident management owns on-call references; frontend/accessibility owners own navigation.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

An employee directory reader cannot manage admin accounts or retrieve restricted fields. Regional readers cannot change country parameters to escape their scope. A delayed prior-country response never replaces the current selection. A 3,500-person, multi-assignment fixture yields a bounded initial payload and proposed sub-one-second directory p95. All reporting chains resolve or show explicit vacancies/cycle errors, and keyboard/screen-reader users can find an acting owner and backup without expanding every department.

## 30. Corporate Titles — /admin/corporate/assignments

### Mission and what exists

Corporate Titles manages actual staff title/department assignments with country, region, reporting manager, notes, editing, and ending assignments. The UI loads staff/catalog choices, has assignment filters, uses abortable main loading, and confirms ending an assignment. The API supports employees as well as admins, validates active staff and titles, applies seniority/C-suite restrictions, audits transactional creation, and locks a row during editing. These controls should be preserved. Titles also feed a separate permission resolver; they must not be confused with automatic basic admin-role or delegated-grant membership.

### Evidence

- [client/src/admin/pages/super/CorporateAssignments.jsx:50](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/super/CorporateAssignments.jsx:50) loads staff choices; `:98` submits scoped assignment fields; `:283` loads catalog/assignments; `:321` confirms ending.
- [server/controllers/corporateRolesController.js:332](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/corporateRolesController.js:332) lists assignments with `LIMIT 500` at `:355`; `:388` creates and `:519` updates them.
- [server/controllers/corporateRolesController.js:438](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/corporateRolesController.js:438) checks hierarchy restrictions, `:460` validates region existence, and `:469` validates a reporting user.
- [server/services/corporateRolesService.js:31](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/services/corporateRolesService.js:31) implements a process-local cache, `:105` selects active assignments, and `:227` invalidates cached entries locally.
- [server/routes/corporateRoles.js:21](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/routes/corporateRoles.js:21) applies account-management permissions; [server/migrations/246_corporate_hierarchy.sql:474](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/migrations/246_corporate_hierarchy.sql:474) establishes assignment relationships.

### Findings

**T30-F01 — High [confirmed defect]: assignment discovery truncates the enterprise workforce.** The API at [corporateRolesController.js:355](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/corporateRolesController.js:355) silently caps results at 500 with no pagination or continuation metadata. At 3,500 employees, especially with multiple titles, staff assignments outside that slice are undiscoverable through a broad view. Filter controls mitigate some searches but cannot replace a complete administration contract. The staff chooser at [CorporateAssignments.jsx:50](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/super/CorporateAssignments.jsx:50) also loads a broad list rather than a scoped searchable staff lookup.

**T30-F02 — High [code observation]: relationship existence does not establish hierarchy validity.** Creation verifies a region exists and a reporting user exists at [corporateRolesController.js:460](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/corporateRolesController.js:460) and `:469`; it does not establish a country/region match or a cycle-free reporting graph. Editing at `:519` locks the current row but accepts changed relationship fields without an expected client version. This can serialize writes yet still overwrite decisions based on stale UI state. Self-reference, multi-node cycles, and cross-scope reporting need explicit graph and policy validation.

**T30-F03 — High [code observation]: effective dates are not part of active permission selection.** The resolver at [corporateRolesService.js:105](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/services/corporateRolesService.js:105) selects `is_active = TRUE` without filtering `started_at` or `ended_at`, despite returning those dates. An assignment marked active can therefore contribute permissions regardless of its recorded time interval. Product policy might define dates as descriptive only, but that must be stated clearly; treating them as scheduled authority without changing resolution is unsafe.

**T30-F04 — High [verification gap]: distributed revocation and regional delegation are not demonstrated.** Cache TTL and invalidation at [corporateRolesService.js:31](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/services/corporateRolesService.js:31) and `:227` are process-local. Multiple application replicas may retain different effective permissions until expiry; acceptable revocation latency needs explicit tests. Seniority checks and `manage_admin_accounts` do not themselves supply target-country authority. No proof is claimed that this overlay controls every route, so its integration with admin grants must be documented rather than assumed.

### Expansion scenario

A reorganization moves 900 employees and creates next-week acting assignments across regional teams. People Operations needs to discover every affected record, prevent two departments reporting to each other, schedule authority precisely, and revoke an outgoing manager immediately. A 500-row slice, descriptive dates interpreted as enforcement, and replica-local cache invalidation produce different organizational answers depending on the reader or application instance.

### Recommended controls and UX

Add complete bounded cursor paging and scoped typeahead for staff, manager, and region selection. Validate manager graph, active manager eligibility, country/region consistency, and allowed cross-region exceptions transactionally. Require expected versions with relationship diffs; keep existing seniority/C-suite protections and durable audit. Define effective-from/until semantics and enforce them in permission resolution, or visibly label dates non-authoritative. Publish cache invalidation across replicas with a stated maximum revocation delay, and test overlay precedence against delegated grants and basic roles. Introduce dedicated scoped People Operations privileges instead of universal account-management authority. Provide reviewed asynchronous reorganization previews, impact reports, and per-assignment conflicts. Localize dates instead of fixed en-US formatting and label modal close/relationship controls for assistive technology.

### Dependencies and owner

People Operations owns hierarchy and acting-role policy; Identity owns effective authority and distributed revocation; corporate API owners own graph/version validation; SRE owns replica consistency; Security approves privileged assignment review.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

All 3,500 employees and multi-title assignments are discoverable through paging/search. Self and multi-node cycles fail validation; unauthorized country/region mismatches are rejected. Future/expired assignments cannot grant effective authority under a time-bound policy. Cross-replica revocation meets a proposed five-second maximum. Concurrent edits return 409, a 900-person reorganization has reviewed item outcomes, and direct API tests verify seniority, scope, and permission-overlay precedence.

## 31. Logistics Ops — /admin/logistics

### Mission and what exists

Logistics Ops is an existing dashboard into a substantial logistics system: lanes, suppliers, sourcing/quotes, pooled purchases, purchase orders, shipments, warehouses, allocations, claims, compliance/customs, pricing, profitability, and privileged control. The dashboard exposes multiple operational links, hides super-only control, and uses abortable statistics loading. Backend state-machine helpers and lane/country/product access policies already exist, including fail-closed behavior. Internal operations/compliance workspaces also recheck current approval for non-admin staff. Expansion should connect those safeguards consistently, not duplicate logistics infrastructure or confuse engineering evidence with legal customs clearance.

### Evidence

- [client/src/admin/pages/logistics/LogisticsAdminDashboard.jsx:17](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/logistics/LogisticsAdminDashboard.jsx:17) defines operational navigation; `:68` loads stats and `:101` renders summaries.
- [server/routes/logisticsAdmin.js:39](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/routes/logisticsAdmin.js:39) exposes stats, `:88` shipments, `:122` super-only pricing changes, and `:131` compliance workspaces.
- [server/middleware/requireInternalLogisticsWorkspace.js:54](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/middleware/requireInternalLogisticsWorkspace.js:54) bypasses workspace checks for admin roles; `:58`–`:70` validates non-admin role/session/live approval.
- [server/controllers/logisticsAdminController.js:33](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/logisticsAdminController.js:33) performs six global aggregate queries; [server/controllers/logisticsShipmentsController.js:85](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/logisticsShipmentsController.js:85) updates tracking directly.
- [client/scripts/adminLogisticsLocalization.test.mjs:45](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/scripts/adminLogisticsLocalization.test.mjs:45) and [:64](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/scripts/adminLogisticsLocalization.test.mjs:64) verify localized contracts/catalog keys statically across en/es/fr/ht/ar; they do not prove live keyboard use or target authorization.
- [server/services/logisticsStateMachine.js:9](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/services/logisticsStateMachine.js:9) defines transitions; [server/services/logisticsAccessService.js:39](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/services/logisticsAccessService.js:39) enforces existing lane/access eligibility and `:163` fails closed on errors.

### Findings

**T31-F01 — High [confirmed defect]: shipment tracking mutation lacks target authorization.** [logisticsShipmentsController.js:85](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/logisticsShipmentsController.js:85) updates carrier, booking reference, tracking, and ETA with only `WHERE id=$5`. The operations-workspace route admits approved internal operators but does not pass a shipment-owner/region predicate into that direct SQL. Unlike access-aware detail/service paths, this mutation does not demonstrate that the caller may administer the target shipment. Global admin reach may be intentional; unscoped internal-operator updates require correction and direct-ID denial tests.

**T31-F02 — High [code observation]: workspace bypass broadens ordinary admin logistics access.** [requireInternalLogisticsWorkspace.js:54](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/middleware/requireInternalLogisticsWorkspace.js:54) accepts any admin role without route-specific internal approval. Several administrative routes at [logisticsAdmin.js:43](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/routes/logisticsAdmin.js:43) also rely on role rather than separate capability grants. This is authenticated behavior, not unauthenticated exposure, and super-only pricing is correctly stricter. Enterprise delegation needs supplier, shipment, claims, warehouse, compliance, and profitability responsibilities distinct from merely possessing an admin role.

**T31-F03 — Medium [confirmed defect]: dashboard receipt wording does not match counted state.** The UI labels warehouse receipts as active, while [logisticsAdminController.js:43](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/logisticsAdminController.js:43) counts all joined inventory receipts without an active/open predicate. Historical receipts can inflate a current-workload impression. The six aggregates at `:37` are global and have no time/scope budget; their scale is a verification gap. Counts should disclose whether they represent backlog, lifetime volume, or snapshot state.

**T31-F04 — Medium [design requirement]: exceptions need accountable ownership and safe concurrent/provider updates.** Tracking updates at [logisticsShipmentsController.js:90](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/logisticsShipmentsController.js:90) lack expected versions and explicit mutation audit. The dashboard emphasizes totals rather than oldest exception, missing documents, carrier freshness, or handoff owner. Existing states and sourcing assignment should be extended into an operational control tower with carrier/provider capability evidence, not assume integrations or jurisdiction approvals from the presence of a tracking number or compliance screen.

### Expansion scenario

A thousand shipments encounter customs holds across several lanes. A regional coordinator corrects ETAs while a carrier import updates the same records; another team reallocates warehouse receipts. Operators must see which holds exceed SLA, who can modify each shipment, and whether carrier evidence is stale. Global lifetime totals and last-writer tracking updates cannot support a reliable night-shift handoff.

### Recommended controls and UX

Apply target-aware lane/region/organization policies to every direct shipment mutation and separate action grants from general admin role. Preserve existing access-service fail-closed decisions and super-only pricing. Add versioned tracking changes, normalized carrier references, source/event timestamps, durable audit, and idempotent ingestion with reconciliation for late/out-of-order events. Define provider-supported integrations and show manual versus verified tracking honestly. Replace ambiguous metrics with scoped aged backlog and snapshot timestamps; benchmark aggregates and use precomputed summaries where justified. Reuse existing state machines for owned exception queues, due dates, document completeness, escalation, and backup assignment. Add bounded asynchronous carrier imports, bulk recalls, and allocation jobs with preview and per-item results. Compliance/customs milestones remain evidence workflows subject to specialist jurisdiction review, not automated legal determinations.

### Dependencies and owner

Logistics Operations owns lane SLAs and handoffs; warehouse/sourcing teams own inventory evidence; Identity owns workspace/action scope; API owners own tracking integrity; integration/SRE teams own carrier reconciliation and query budgets.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

Approved operators cannot mutate an unauthorized shipment by ID. Stale tracking writes conflict and duplicate carrier events do not regress state. Dashboard active/backlog counts match fixture definitions and display scope/freshness. A thousand held shipments have owners, deadlines, and visible escalation. Large imports resume after restart with item results, and read-only carrier/provider tests verify supported capabilities before claiming automated tracking or clearance.

## 32. Learn Submissions — /admin/learn/submissions

### Mission and what exists

Learn Submissions is an existing moderation screen with status-filtered pagination, expanded review cards, approve/reject actions, reviewer notes, video queues, and flagged-video workflows. The client has cancellable list loading and disabled actions while review is pending. The server validates decisions, publishes approved submissions transactionally with a savepoint, and runs AI tagging afterward. Video publication, provider allowlists, creator flags, and focused tests are real capabilities. Enterprise readiness depends on reviewers seeing authoritative content and resolving publication failures rather than adding a second learning-content system.

### Evidence

- [client/src/admin/pages/LearnSubmissionsAdmin.jsx:76](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/LearnSubmissionsAdmin.jsx:76) expects full review content; `:169` renders tools; `:290` loads paged rows; `:724` passes those rows to expanded review.
- [server/controllers/learnController.js:389](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/learnController.js:389) lists summary fields; `:436` already provides submission detail; `:460` reviews and `:493` attempts publication.
- [server/controllers/learnController.js:1293](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/learnController.js:1293) lists video work and `:1394` processes eligible publication batches.
- [server/routes/learn.js:91](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/routes/learn.js:91) defines video moderation controls/limits and `:139` provides flag workflows.
- [server/__tests__/learnGuideVideos.test.js:203](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/__tests__/learnGuideVideos.test.js:203) tests publication, `:277` conflicts, and `:412` flags; [server/__tests__/learn.test.js:73](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/__tests__/learn.test.js:73) tests submission validation.

### Findings

**T32-F01 — High [confirmed defect]: reviewers receive summary rows rather than the full submission.** The list projection at [learnController.js:405](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/learnController.js:405) omits intro, steps, skills, tools, video, and license evidence. Expansion at [LearnSubmissionsAdmin.jsx:724](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/LearnSubmissionsAdmin.jsx:724) passes that same list row into a component expecting those fields, without fetching the existing detail endpoint. Reviewers can approve/reject while the screen lacks the actual instructional payload. This is a current UI/API mismatch, not a claim the detail capability is missing.

**T32-F02 — High [confirmed defect]: review decisions do not enforce pending/version state.** The transaction at [learnController.js:472](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/learnController.js:472) reads without locking and updates at `:484` by ID alone. Two reviewers, or a stale direct API request, can overwrite approved/rejected state and reviewer attribution despite UI actions being shown only for pending rows. The transaction groups writes but does not establish an exclusive moderation decision. Appropriate re-review must be a separately authorized transition with preserved history.

**T32-F03 — High [confirmed defect]: approval can succeed without publication and without actionable recovery UX.** A publication failure rolls back only to the savepoint at [learnController.js:502](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/learnController.js:502); approval commits and responds with `published_guide_id: null` at `:508`. The client review handler at [LearnSubmissionsAdmin.jsx:305](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/LearnSubmissionsAdmin.jsx:305) reloads without turning that null outcome into an assigned publication-recovery case. Savepoint resilience is intentional and useful, but approved/unpublished must not be indistinguishable from published success.

**T32-F04 — Medium [confirmed defect]: linked-tool rendering shadows the translation function.** In [LearnSubmissionsAdmin.jsx:169](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/LearnSubmissionsAdmin.jsx:169), the tool variable is named `t`, then `:175` calls that object as a translation function when `buy_url` exists; rental links have the same pattern. Once full detail is properly loaded, linked tools can trigger a TypeError. This currently latent branch needs a direct component fixture, not simply a build-success claim.

### Expansion scenario

A multilingual campaign produces 5,000 how-to submissions, including instructions requiring specialist review. Two moderators open the same item, one approves while another rejects, and catalog publication temporarily fails. A summary-only card and lost decision ownership allow weak review; approved-but-unpublished content becomes an invisible backlog. Timed video publication also needs capacity planning so specialist holds are applied before automated eligibility.

### Recommended controls and UX

Fetch authoritative detail before enabling a decision and show attachment/license/content version plus a reviewed-content acknowledgement. Lock or conditionally update pending/version state, append decisions to immutable history, and require a reason for rejection/reversal. Keep the savepoint but persist publication state, error category, retry identity, owner, and SLA; expose a retry/recovery queue. Rename the tool iterator and test links with real detail fixtures. Add scoped moderation roles and specialist routing by language/category/risk, assigned workload, oldest pending age, and explicit auto-publication holds using existing video controls. Bound bulk decisions as reviewed asynchronous jobs rather than unchecked mass approval. Ensure video/content alternatives, captions, translated statuses, keyboard review controls, and RTL presentation. Professional/legal review requirements need specialist definitions, not unsupported blanket claims.

### Dependencies and owner

Learn Trust/Content owns review standards and specialist escalation; Learn API owns decision/publication integrity; frontend owners own complete review payloads; video workers own timed backlog; localization/accessibility owners validate global review.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

A reviewer sees complete steps/tools/video evidence before approval. Concurrent decisions yield one committed result and a 409 for the stale reviewer. Injected publish failure produces visible approved/unpublished state and idempotent retry. Linked-tool cards render without TypeError. A 5,000-item workload shows owners, oldest age, and held-video deadlines, while authenticated multi-locale keyboard tests verify the complete review flow rather than relying on the skipped CI matrix.

## 33. Learn Product Links — /admin/learn/product-links

### Mission and what exists

Learn Product Links is an implemented curation workspace for tool/material links, including affiliate retail, owned inventory, partners, and internal rentals. It supports global or guide-specific overrides, search, editing, activity toggles, and confirmed deactivation. The server exposes public active links and admin CRUD, while AI tagging reuses curated matches rather than inventing arbitrary purchase URLs. Guide-specific priority and case-insensitive tool lookup already exist. Enterprise expansion should improve this curated registry and publication governance, not create a competing link-management system.

### Evidence

- [client/src/admin/pages/LearnProductLinksAdmin.jsx:35](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/LearnProductLinksAdmin.jsx:35) defines link types; `:47` validates URLs; `:327` loads links and `:365` filters them locally.
- [server/routes/learn.js:162](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/routes/learn.js:162)–`:165` protects CRUD with `moderate_content`.
- [server/controllers/learnController.js:600](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/learnController.js:600) returns public active links; `:619` lists admin links; `:651` creates, `:696` partially updates, and `:746` deletes/deactivates.
- [server/migrations/290_learn_product_links.sql:12](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/migrations/290_learn_product_links.sql:12) defines override scope, attribution fields, and active state; `:30` adds lookup indexes.
- [server/services/learnTagging.js:102](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/services/learnTagging.js:102) resolves curated links and `:119` gives guide-specific overrides priority; [server/__tests__/learn.test.js:64](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/__tests__/learn.test.js:64) tests fallback availability.

### Findings

**T33-F01 — High [confirmed defect]: retail-domain validation accepts lookalike hostnames.** Client validation at [LearnProductLinksAdmin.jsx:47](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/LearnProductLinksAdmin.jsx:47) and server validation at [learnController.js:662](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/learnController.js:662) use a prefix regex with no hostname boundary. A string such as `https://amazon.com.evil.invalid/item` matches the allowed prefix despite its actual host being unrelated. This is a demonstrable string-validation defect, not an assertion about any live stored URL. Other link types also lack a unified server URL-protocol/host policy in these handlers. Source comments describing compliance are not proof of provider approval.

**T33-F02 — High [confirmed defect]: partial updates do not validate the effective record.** [learnController.js:713](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/learnController.js:713) checks a changed buy URL only when one is supplied. Changing an existing row to the affiliate type without sending `buy_url` skips validation of its retained destination. Neither a client-only tag-presence check nor the generic allowed-field list establishes that the final combined type, destination, affiliate identifier, and guide scope are valid. This requires merged-record validation under a versioned transaction.

**T33-F03 — Medium [code observation]: discovery and public refresh are unbounded.** Public active links at [learnController.js:600](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/learnController.js:600) and administrative links at `:619` load all matching rows without paging. The UI at [LearnProductLinksAdmin.jsx:365](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/LearnProductLinksAdmin.jsx:365) searches locally after loading them. With tens of thousands of overrides, repeated public refreshes and staff discovery need indexed, scoped lookup and cache/version budgeting. Existing indexes deserve credit, but they do not reduce a full-table response by themselves; no production latency claim is made.

**T33-F04 — High [design requirement]: global overrides need release and regional governance.** The current schema supports guide-specific/global scope but no explicit country/locale program selection or immutable edit history. Partial updates have no expected version, and permanent deletion at [learnController.js:746](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/learnController.js:746) shares the same content-moderation grant as ordinary deactivation. Duplicate normalized tool/scope records are not prevented by migration 290’s indexes, making tie resolution important. Add reviewed commercial-link responsibilities rather than treating all content moderators as global affiliate administrators.

### Expansion scenario

A seasonal rollout updates 80,000 learning links for local inventory, supported retail programs, and rental alternatives. Two regional editors change the same global override, and a mistyped destination passes prefix validation. The platform needs to roll back the exact publication version without losing historical click attribution or forcing every user refresh to download the entire registry. Region-specific eligibility cannot be inferred from one global affiliate tag.

### Recommended controls and UX

Parse URLs with a standard parser, require HTTPS for external destinations, compare exact normalized hostnames against an approved allowlist, and validate internal paths separately. Reject credentials, unsupported schemes, and lookalike subdomains; normalize then validate the merged record on every update. Add scoped commercial-link grants, expected versions, durable before/after history, reviewed publication versions, and rollback. Define normalized uniqueness and deterministic guide/global/locale priority using the existing override model. Prefer deactivation with retained attribution; require stronger authority and dependency preview for physical deletion. Replace full-list loading with bounded server search and guide-specific public lookup/cache versions; use asynchronous bulk preview/jobs with item errors. Show link origin, region/locale, verification age, and provider-program status accurately. Program terms and jurisdiction-dependent disclosures require specialist/provider review, not assurances from regex checks.

### Dependencies and owner

Learn Commerce owns curation and release review; Security owns URL validation; API/Data owns deterministic lookup and cache versions; Identity owns regional commercial grants; partnership specialists own provider eligibility and program approvals.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

Lookalike hosts, unsafe schemes, and invalid merged updates fail server tests. Duplicate normalized scope records are rejected or resolve by documented deterministic precedence. Concurrent edits return 409. A published version can be rolled back while retaining click attribution. An 80,000-link update is a resumable bounded job; public guide lookup returns only relevant links with a proposed sub-300-ms p95 cache path. Regional editors cannot alter global or foreign-region overrides without explicit authority.

## 34. Learn AI Suggestions — /admin/learn/ai-suggestions

### Mission and what exists
