Learn AI Suggestions is an existing human-review queue for AI-proposed tools, materials, and services, with guide/status filters, expandable suggestions, analysis triggering, approval, and rejection. The client uses abortable loading. The server deduplicates pending suggestions, locks the suggestion and guide during approval, prevents repeat decisions, validates service categories, and uses curated product/rental matches. Tagging has a timeout and fail-soft reasons for missing configuration, throttling, network, and invalid JSON. These are real safeguards; readiness requires scalable job execution, truthful capability reporting, and provenance, not unreviewed automatic publication.

### Evidence

- [client/src/admin/pages/LearnAiSuggestionsAdmin.jsx:93](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/LearnAiSuggestionsAdmin.jsx:93) displays confidence, `:232` loads guide choices, `:243` loads suggestions, and `:263` runs analysis.
- [server/controllers/learnController.js:939](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/learnController.js:939) awaits tagging; `:956` lists a fixed 200 suggestions; `:986` locks and approves; `:1066` conditionally rejects pending rows.
- [server/services/learnTagging.js:18](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/services/learnTagging.js:18) defines timeout/result limits, `:36` builds prompts, `:82` matches rentals, `:140` deduplicates, and `:160` returns typed failure reasons.
- [server/migrations/628_learn_ai_tagging.sql:10](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/migrations/628_learn_ai_tagging.sql:10) defines suggestion provenance/status/reviewer fields; `:36` establishes pending deduplication.
- [server/__tests__/learnTagging.test.js:63](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/__tests__/learnTagging.test.js:63), `:89`, and `:97` test unavailable/throttled/timeout behavior; [server/__tests__/learnMarketplace.test.js:123](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/__tests__/learnMarketplace.test.js:123) tests approval and `:183` decision conflict.

### Findings

**T34-F01 — High [confirmed defect]: review history and backlog are silently truncated.** [learnController.js:970](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/learnController.js:970) fixes the list at 200 with no cursor or total. The UI at [LearnAiSuggestionsAdmin.jsx:247](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/LearnAiSuggestionsAdmin.jsx:247) falls back to the returned row count as total and has no continuation mechanism. After a tagging campaign, older pending suggestions are invisible even if they are the oldest SLA breach. Existing guide/status filters help narrow subsets but do not establish complete queue access.

**T34-F02 — Medium [confirmed defect]: analysis failures are presented as duplicate counts.** Tagging returns `{skipped: 'no-key'}`, `'timeout'`, `'rate-limited'`, and other reasons at [learnTagging.js:160](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/services/learnTagging.js:160)–`:217`. The successful HTTP handler passes that result through, while the UI at [LearnAiSuggestionsAdmin.jsx:270](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/client/src/admin/pages/LearnAiSuggestionsAdmin.jsx:270) formats `skipped` as “already existed.” Missing provider capability or a timeout can therefore look like a completed analysis with duplicate results. Fail-soft backend behavior is useful, but it needs a typed capability/error UX and accountable recovery.

**T34-F03 — High [code observation]: review lacks source/model version evidence.** Migration 628 stores raw JSON, timestamps, and reviewer identity, but not a guide-content hash/version, model identifier, prompt revision, or catalog snapshot. Prompt building at [learnTagging.js:36](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/services/learnTagging.js:36) uses title/category/intro rather than full instructional steps. Approval locks current guide content, but does not show whether that content changed after analysis. Rental confidence at `:82` is a name-match heuristic, not demonstrated calibrated probability across languages; the UI must explain that distinction.

**T34-F04 — Medium [design requirement]: synchronous tagging cannot serve large controlled campaigns.** The controller at [learnController.js:939](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/controllers/learnController.js:939) awaits a provider call with a 25-second timeout; [learnTagging.js:224](/Users/oxsteed/Documents/GitHub/6/server/services/learnTagging.js:224) then performs per-item matching/insertion. A six-million-user content platform needs bounded concurrency, quota/cost budgets, resumable jobs, and provider-failure ownership. That is a scale requirement, not proof of current provider outage. Numeric confidence also needs an explicit API type contract: schema NUMERIC at migration 628 line 21 does not by itself guarantee the JavaScript number expected by the client.

### Expansion scenario

After a Direct catalog revision, editors retag 12,000 guides across five languages. Specialists concurrently review suggestions while guide authors correct content. Provider throttling must pause jobs visibly, not report duplicates; old suggestions must remain discoverable, and approval must reveal stale source/catalog evidence. English-oriented name matching cannot silently become a universal confidence claim.

### Recommended controls and UX

Keep human approval, locking, service validation, and deduplication. Add complete bounded queue paging, oldest-age counts, assigned reviewer/team, and scoped moderation authority. Return typed outcomes distinguishing created, deduplicated, unavailable, throttled, and failed. Move campaigns to durable idempotent jobs with content/catalog hashes, bounded concurrency, backoff, retry ownership, cancellation, quota/cost ceilings, and operational metrics. Store model/prompt revision and provenance with each suggestion; require reanalysis or explicit informed override when source changes. Show confidence as a explained heuristic with normalized numeric serialization, and evaluate precision/recall by language/category before expanding thresholds. Reuse curated destinations and retain the no-arbitrary-AI-URL boundary. Provider/data-processing approval and jurisdiction review remain separate from engineering implementation; no credentials or live provider calls are needed for these controls.

### Dependencies and owner

Learn AI/Product owns suggestion quality; Content Operations owns review SLAs; API/worker owners own jobs and provenance; SRE owns provider budgets; partnerships/privacy specialists own external-provider approvals; localization owns multilingual evaluation.

### Measurable acceptance criteria

Every pending item beyond 200 is reachable and counted. Missing configuration, timeout, and throttling render distinct non-success outcomes. Stale guide/catalog versions cannot be silently approved. A 12,000-guide job resumes without duplicate pending suggestions and obeys configured provider/concurrency budgets. Concurrent approval produces one result and a conflict, confidence serializes consistently, and language/category evaluations meet an agreed quality threshold before wider automatic matching is enabled.

# Super-admin tab reviews

## 35. Super Dashboard — `/admin/super/dashboard`

**Mission / what exists.** This is an executive and incident overview, combining user growth, jobs, revenue, verification, payouts, activity, geography, health, and durable security incidents. The current page offers period selection, refresh, revenue charts, incident acknowledgement and resolution. It is implemented, with separate auxiliary requests that allow partial rendering. Its role restriction is enforced by the API, not only navigation. It should become the entry point for accountable global operations while allowing regional managers to see only authorized business information through an explicitly scoped mode.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/Dashboard.jsx:141` distinguishes super administrators; `:150` fetches primary statistics; `:160` independently loads activity, geography, monitoring health, and security events; `:202` and `:216` invoke incident actions. `server/routes/admin.js:161` protects dashboard/chart/activity/geo endpoints with `requireSuperAdmin`. `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:172` constructs statistics, `:190` performs multiple user-count subqueries, `:235` aggregates jobs, `:268` aggregates the platform ledger, and `:399` returns statistics. `client/scripts/adminRouteContracts.test.mjs:9` checks dashboard support links, but is a source contract test rather than a rendered executive workflow test.

**Findings.** **T35-F01 — High [confirmed defect]: completion-rate cohorts disagree.** At `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:235`, cancelled jobs are counted over all history, while completed jobs for the period are counted separately. At `:356`, the denominator combines period completions with lifetime cancellations. A seven-day performance card can deteriorate solely because the platform has accumulated old cancellations. This is a direct arithmetic-definition defect, independent of traffic or deployment health. Use the same event-time cohort for numerator and denominator and expose the definition in the card.

**T35-F02 — High [code observation]: query failure can look like a valid zero.** The core aggregation at `:199` catches failure into an empty object; `:362` then converts absent fields to zero. Optional panels also use empty activity/geography fallbacks at `client/src/admin/pages/super/Dashboard.jsx:182`. Consequently, a dependency-specific failure can resemble no business activity even while the endpoint returns 200. Preserve partial availability, but return per-metric validity, error code, source watermark, and last successful observation; render unavailable values distinctly from zero.

**T35-F03 — Medium [code observation]: refreshed dashboards repeatedly request history-wide aggregation.** Counts at `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:192`, job aggregates at `:235`, and ledger totals at `:268` are not supplied by an inspected snapshot cache. This is a scale exposure, not proof that current queries are slow. Six million users and years of jobs make refresh fan-out worth benchmarking. Incremental daily aggregates and bounded freshness would let executive refreshes avoid competing with checkout and support writes.

**T35-F04 — Medium [design requirement]: shared incident visibility needs regional accountability.** The super-only route protects global data, but global operators cannot safely delegate this whole view to shift leads. Extend existing incident and grant mechanisms with scoped summaries, an incident owner/team, acknowledgement deadline, escalation clock, and handover history. Do not create a second incident system. Distinguish security-event samples from the authoritative incident queue and link every actionable card to its filtered operational workspace.

**Expansion scenario.** During an Asia-Pacific launch, a local holiday increases cancellations while a European shift is investigating an unrelated queue failure. An executive sees both in a global card; a regional lead needs the affected market, valid comparison period, source freshness, owner, and deadline. Wrong cohorts or silent zero fallbacks would misdirect staffing even though `/ready` remains green.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Add explicit UTC/reporting-zone selection, consistent cohort definitions, cached aggregates with drill-through, partial-data banners, and accessible textual chart equivalents. Retain translated labels and verify Arabic direction, keyboard incident controls, focus restoration, and reduced motion in a configured authenticated matrix.

**Dependencies / owner.** Analytics owns definitions; SRE owns freshness and incident handover; IAM owns scoped access; admin frontend owns rendering. Coordinate with Money and Regions rather than separately inventing scopes.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** A fixture containing 100 historic cancellations and ten current completions must not alter the current-period rate unless current cancellations change. Inject one failed source and verify an unavailable marker with no fabricated zero. At six million seeded users and declared transaction history, 100 concurrent operator refreshes should meet p95 two seconds, with less than five-minute aggregate lag and recorded query plans. Every critical incident must have an owner and an escalation deadline; the authenticated matrix must execute with zero fixture-related skips.

## 36. Money — `/admin/super/money`

**Mission / what exists.** Money is an existing cross-business commercial map, grouping direct revenue, marketplace volume, partner flows, and configured programs. It includes period switches, recent movements, fee settings, and links to deeper operating pages. This distinction is valuable: marketplace GMV and an affiliate payout are not automatically platform revenue. The endpoint already attempts to account for subscriptions, service fees, consulting, marketplace orders, API products, affiliates, franchises, and launch applications. The scale objective is to make the map an explainable, scoped management instrument with a consistent reporting basis.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/MoneyPage.jsx:22` defines channel categories and `:49` defines tracking-status badges; `:68` formats amounts as USD; `:270` renders period controls. `server/routes/admin.js:247` requires `view_financials`; `server/middleware/adminAuth.js:126` and `:171` restrict delegated permission checks to global grants. `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:1633` builds the overview, `:1659` checks optional tables, `:1676` defines recognized ledger sources, `:1690` aggregates movements, `:2021` combines ledger revenue and consulting receipts, and `:2039` constructs channel definitions. `client/scripts/adminRouteContracts.test.mjs:62` checks server authorization wiring for Money.

**Findings.** **T36-F01 — High [code observation]: the report lacks a currency grouping at its aggregation boundary.** The ledger query groups by source at `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:1699`, while `client/src/admin/pages/super/MoneyPage.jsx:68` renders a USD amount. Consulting and marketplace additions are likewise represented as cent totals in the overview. The inspected endpoint does not establish an exchange-rate snapshot or explain conversion. This does not prove existing rows contain mixed currencies, but it makes worldwide reporting unsafe until the currency invariant is demonstrated. Return native-currency totals and separately identified reporting-currency conversions with rate source, timestamp, and rounding policy.

**T36-F02 — High [design requirement]: operational classification is not yet a finance-approved reporting contract.** The “tracked revenue” calculation at `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:2021` adds selected positive ledger sources to completed consulting payments, while other channels are explicitly volume or pipeline signals. Retain those distinctions. Add a versioned source mapping, provenance, elimination rules, and a cross-tab bridge explaining why Money, Revenue, and Financials differ. A possible duplicate consulting posting is a verification question, not a confirmed duplicate. Finance must approve the bridge before employees use these numbers for a close or management forecast.

**T36-F03 — Medium [code observation]: live global aggregation has substantial request fan-out.** Schema probes at `:1659` precede lifetime sums at `:1692`, then service, consulting, commerce, affiliate, and franchise queries. The code handles optional tables, but no inspected consistent snapshot encompasses all those reads. Under sustained business writes, channel totals can reflect different instants. Precompute a coherent reporting snapshot, expose its watermark, and benchmark query amplification; do not claim a present performance outage without timings.

**T36-F04 — High [code observation]: regional financial delegation remains all-or-nothing.** `requirePermission` deliberately rejects regional-only grants on this global API. That is a correct defensive boundary. However, a regional business lead has no scoped Money request in the inspected page. Introduce entity/market filters backed by server-resolved business ownership and a reporting permission limited to that scope. Merely adding a country dropdown would not create authorization.

**Expansion scenario.** A regional general manager compares consulting receipts, marketplace sales, and franchise commitments across Canada and Japan during a monthly business review. The existing badges help separate concepts, but a single USD presentation and different observation times could overstate a market or obscure unapplied pipeline value. The manager must drill from each figure to its records without gaining access to another region's customers or affiliate earnings.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Offer native versus reporting currency, market/legal-entity scope, accounting-basis badges, source freshness, and a reconciled bridge to the ledger. Preserve the existing channel map. Add accessible table equivalents for summary graphics, locale-aware numbers, saved filters, and an explicit “configured, no recognized receipts” state rather than implying provider readiness.

**Dependencies / owner.** Finance analytics owns mappings and FX policy; IAM owns server scope; business-domain owners validate source semantics; data engineering owns snapshots; admin frontend owns drill-through and accessibility.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** USD/CAD/JPY fixtures must remain separated until a declared FX snapshot is applied, with no implicit two-decimal assumption for native JPY. Each channel total must reproduce from an identified source set and watermark. A regional admin must receive 403 for unauthorized entities on list and drill-through APIs. Benchmark 100 concurrent reads against declared multi-year history with p95 two seconds and a published freshness budget. A finance-approved bridge must explain every difference between Money and the other financial tabs.

## 37. Restaurant Credit — `/admin/super/restaurant-credit`

**Mission / what exists.** Restaurant Credit is an existing global oversight console for isolated restaurant wallets. It has search, status/restaurant filters, server pagination, wallet details, balances/reservations, load requests, hold release, suspension/reactivation, operator history, and restaurant conversion-policy controls. The important boundary is restaurant wallet isolation: this tab should oversee that existing ledger rather than introduce a competing wallet. Its global super-admin restriction is explicit on both read and write routes, and financial mutations carry a limiter.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/RestaurantCreditAdmin.jsx:413` manages page/filter/detail state, `:429` loads paginated overview data, `:449` fetches a wallet, and `:483` dispatches actions. `:243`, `:256`, `:269`, `:281`, and `:289` slice detail histories to small samples. `server/routes/admin.js:166` applies no-store responses; `:168`–`:175` require super-admin access and limit mutations. `server/controllers/restaurantCreditAdminController.js:180` parses pagination, `:189` runs overview queries, `:307` returns pagination, and `:504` locks status changes inside a transaction with operator audit at `:527`. `server/__tests__/restaurantCreditAdmin.test.js:89` checks the overview contract and `:115` validates status-filter rejection.

**Findings.** **T37-F01 — Medium [code observation]: the UI protects against mixed-currency display, but the overview lacks an actionable currency breakdown.** At `server/controllers/restaurantCreditAdminController.js:197`, balance, reserved, and available amounts are summed without currency grouping; the response at `:322` supplies totals plus a currency count. Importantly, `client/src/admin/pages/super/RestaurantCreditAdmin.jsx:521`–`:522` masks mixed totals with a localized “Multiple” label, and `:541`–`:544` uses that guard. This is not a misleading mixed-currency amount displayed by the current UI, nor evidence of cross-wallet spending. Nevertheless, finance cannot inspect each currency's aggregate liability from these overview cards. Return grouped native balances and render a currency breakdown; deprecate unqualified scalar totals for multi-currency API consumers unless a separate, documented conversion is applied.

**T37-F02 — Medium [code observation]: wallet history is sampled twice without historical navigation.** The server limits detailed histories at `:389` and `:396`; the client further slices load requests, holds, transfers, ledger entries, and audit events at the lines above. This is not a missing detail page. It is an incomplete investigation workflow for an old receipt or hold. Add independent cursor pagination, date filters, provider/order reference search, and an export tied to the account and restaurant scope. Clearly label the first screen as recent activity and disclose completeness.

**T37-F03 — High [code observation]: locking serializes status updates but does not identify stale operator intent.** At `:511` the account is locked, closed wallets cannot be reactivated at `:517`, and unchanged targets become no-ops at `:518`. Those controls are valuable. The write accepts a desired status without an expected status or version, however. A stale operator can reactivate a wallet that another shift just suspended for an investigation. Require an expected state/version and a reason for reactivation, present the intervening change, and route exceptional overrides through the existing audit and approval mechanisms.

**T37-F04 — High [design requirement]: global super-admin operation cannot be the workforce model.** Routes at `server/routes/admin.js:168` remain global even when the UI filters one restaurant. Introduce restaurant/tenant-scoped reviewer and operator grants, with thresholds and independent approval for material releases or loads. Preserve existing merchant ownership checks on ordinary wallet services. Test the oversight route's scope itself rather than assuming restaurant filtering enforces it.

**Expansion scenario.** A restaurant group has hundreds of outlets and seasonal wallet promotions. A Canadian finance investigator must examine a months-old transfer while a Caribbean operator suspends a suspicious wallet. Current recent-history samples and stale reactivation intent make this handover fragile. Filters, conflict feedback, and scoped delegation must support the investigation without weakening the existing ledger isolation.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Extend the current console with currency-separated totals, historical cursors, reasoned reactivation, conflict dialogs, case ownership, pending-request age, and bulk review jobs with previews and per-row results. Financial exports and bulk approvals must reauthorize each restaurant. Preserve localized credit formatters; test keyboard dialog focus, Arabic layout, and accessible status announcements.

**Dependencies / owner.** Restaurant payments owns wallet invariants and recovery; IAM owns restaurant scope; risk operations owns review thresholds and SLAs; frontend/data engineering own history navigation and aggregates.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** A two-currency fixture produces two totals. A wallet with 10,000 entries must expose its earliest entry through bounded requests. Racing suspension/reactivation with the same starting version must yield one success and one 409. An operator assigned restaurant A cannot read or mutate B, including direct account IDs. A 10,000-request review job must be resumable, idempotent, and produce complete row results with no duplicate ledger effects.

## 38. OxSteed Credit — `/admin/super/oxsteed-credit` and all 13 subpages

**Mission / what exists.** OxSteed Credit is a substantial existing control plane, not an absent page or a placeholder-only feature. It separates its shared credit journal from restaurant wallets and includes maker/checker approvals, append-only financial history, reservations, disputes, reconciliation, per-currency overview, partner agreements, health, customer accounts, withdrawals, and cards. `client/src/admin/adminRouteManifest.js:110`–`:123` declares the overview and thirteen child routes; `client/src/admin/pages/super/oxsteedCredit/shared.jsx:420` defines their navigation. All inspected administrative Credit routes use `requireSuperAdmin` (`server/routes/admin.js:347` onward), with no-store responses, export limits, and mutation limiters. The worldwide requirement is to operate these capabilities safely through many scoped teams, not duplicate the accounting rail.

**Evidence / complete subpage inventory.** Each child route below is relative to `/admin/super/oxsteed-credit`; source pointers identify real workflows and their next scale requirement.

1. **Approvals — `/approvals`:** `client/src/admin/pages/super/oxsteedCredit/ApprovalsPage.jsx:28` loads pending actions; `:55` issues step-up; `:71` approves; `:88` rejects with a reason. The page fetches only 50 pending actions. Add older-page navigation, assignment, age sorting, and execution-result drill-through.
2. **Disputes — `/disputes`:** `client/src/admin/pages/super/oxsteedCredit/DisputesPage.jsx:65` loads paginated disputes and `:118` resolves with documented outcomes. Migration `640_oxsteed_credit_governance.sql:72` adds dispute type, SLA, and compensation references. Extend existing SLA state with accountable ownership and cross-shift reassignment rather than another dispute store.
3. **Settlements — `/settlements`:** `client/src/admin/pages/super/oxsteedCredit/SettlementsPage.jsx:149` loads history; `:215` creates; `:237` transitions with expected state; `:264` reverses; `:287` reconciles. Retain these recovery controls. Add a bounded settlement-cycle work queue and receipt-linked provider ambiguity handling.
4. **Reconciliation — `/reconciliation`:** `client/src/admin/pages/super/oxsteedCredit/ReconciliationPage.jsx:86` loads internal and external history; `:127` triggers external reconciliation. `server/services/oxsteedCreditExternalReconciliation.js:534` fails a run when Stripe is unavailable rather than declaring a match. Extend the existing run history with asynchronous progress, ownership, and checkpointed large-period processing.
5. **Compliance — `/compliance`:** `client/src/admin/pages/super/oxsteedCredit/CompliancePage.jsx:52` loads identity, sanctions, and case queues; `:69` decides items using idempotency headers at `:88`. `server/services/complianceCaseService.js:211` combines three queues, but fixes verification and sanction offsets at zero. Add independent complete paging and counts; jurisdiction review determines policy, not this audit.
6. **Reserves — `/reserves`:** `client/src/admin/pages/super/oxsteedCredit/ReservesPage.jsx:29` loads currency policies and `:44` queues updates. The reserve-policy system exists. Add market/entity views, required evidence freshness, and scenario previews of liability coverage; keep policy changes in existing approval flow.
7. **Partners — `/partners`:** `client/src/admin/pages/super/oxsteedCredit/PartnersPage.jsx:203` transitions agreements with expected state; `:230` exports partners; `:389` displays a searchable, paginated directory. Enrollment, agreements, entitlement controls, and partner ledger functionality already exist. Add scoped onboarding ownership and scalable searchable selectors for the bounded supporting lists.
8. **Health — `/health`:** `client/src/admin/pages/super/oxsteedCredit/HealthPage.jsx:67` loads overview and rail state; `:88` toggles using `expectedEnabled`; `:103` freezes; `:117` unfreezes. `client/src/admin/pages/super/oxsteedCredit/shared.jsx:24` lists workers. Add last-good watermark, incident assignment, and controlled regional incident views while retaining global freeze authority.
9. **Audit — `/audit`:** `client/src/admin/pages/super/oxsteedCredit/AuditPage.jsx:37` loads paginated events and `:70` exports filtered history. `server/services/oxsteedCreditAdminService.js:562` implements bounded audit filtering. Add asynchronous evidence bundles, scoped redaction, and independent integrity-verification status; do not replace the existing journal/audit chain.
10. **Customers — `/customers`:** `client/src/admin/pages/super/oxsteedCredit/CustomersPage.jsx:46` lists filtered accounts with offset paging; `:79` loads value; `:98` adjusts; `:119` changes status with stable idempotency keys. Add scoped customer search, case context, and reproducible action outcomes without granting regional operators global money authority.
11. **Money Out — `/money-out`:** `client/src/admin/pages/super/oxsteedCredit/MoneyOutPage.jsx:20` embeds the existing component; `client/src/admin/pages/super/OxsteedCreditMoneyOut.jsx:78` and `:89` load withdrawals/conversions with offset zero. Approval integrates pending actions (`:156`). Add complete queue pagination and separate reconciliation/return handling per provider outcome.
12. **Cards — `/cards`:** `client/src/admin/pages/super/oxsteedCredit/CardsPage.jsx:23` fetches overview and embeds the existing card workspace. `client/src/admin/pages/super/OxsteedCreditCards.jsx:72` paginates and filters; `:145` advances physical fulfillment. `server/services/oxsteedCreditCardService.js:5` explicitly models cards as revocable credentials resolving to existing credit accounts. This is evidence of an internal credential-card ecosystem, not evidence of external network acceptance or provider-issued bank cards. Add fulfillment integration proof, shipping exceptions, and market capability badges before representing a supported external channel.
13. **Ledger — `/ledger`:** `client/src/admin/pages/super/oxsteedCredit/LedgerPage.jsx:63` pages transactions, `:95` inspects a transaction, `:108` submits an idempotent refund, and `:142` exports. Extend with cursor/snapshot consistency, recovery-case ownership, and large export jobs; preserve compensating entries rather than editing posted history.

**Findings.** **T38-F01 — High [design requirement]: global super-admin restriction prevents least-privilege workforce delegation.** The server routes at `server/routes/admin.js:365`, `:391`, `:436`, and `:441` protect customer, mutation, approval, and compliance operations globally. This is secure fail-closed behavior, but supporting many market teams by making each a super-admin is not an acceptable enterprise operating model. Add granular scoped Credit read/review/initiate/approve permissions to the existing IAM system, resolve tenant/partner/entity scope on the API, enforce amount thresholds, and preserve independent checker identity.

**T38-F02 — High [confirmed defect]: compliance paging cannot advance two queue kinds.** `server/services/complianceCaseService.js:215` and `:216` explicitly pass offset zero, even when the composite endpoint receives another offset at `:217`. Counts at `:225` are returned page lengths, not complete queue counts. The client at `client/src/admin/pages/super/oxsteedCredit/CompliancePage.jsx:57` requests no pagination at all. Operators cannot reliably establish workload completeness beyond the first verification/sanction page. Return independent cursors, totals, SLA aggregates, and assigned queues.

**T38-F03 — High [code observation]: approvals and Money Out hide older work behind first-page requests.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/oxsteedCredit/ApprovalsPage.jsx:32` asks for the newest 50 actions, while the service supports offsets at `server/services/oxsteedCreditPendingActionService.js:568` and orders newest first at `:580`. Money Out always requests offset zero at the pointers above. Older pending actions could remain unseen under continuous intake; that scenario is not a measured current backlog. Provide age-first queues, complete navigation, assignment, expiry sweeps, and explicit replay/result states.

**T38-F04 — Medium [code observation]: bounded exports still execute synchronously in application memory.** `server/controllers/oxsteedCreditController.js:1292` accumulates partner pages before constructing CSV at `:1304`, and rejects oversized exports at `:1299`. This is a real safeguard, unlike Revenue's unbounded export. At six million users it also means a legitimate large investigation needs a job with immutable filter/scope snapshot, bounded chunks, checksum, download expiry, and cancellation. Retain existing row caps for interactive downloads.

**T38-F05 — High [verification gap]: provider execution and recovery are not proven by internal readiness.** Existing external reconciliation explicitly records failure and incidents at `server/services/oxsteedCreditExternalReconciliation.js:514`; its provider-less path fails safely at `:536`. Verify late webhooks, ambiguous transfers, returned payouts, duplicate delivery, provider unavailable, and account restriction in isolated supported provider environments. Demonstrate that each unsupported market is visible and blocked before money moves. Do not describe provider approvals or jurisdiction permissions as already obtained.

**T38-F06 — Medium [code observation]: repeated global summaries and offset pages require a scale plan.** `server/controllers/oxsteedCreditController.js:850` sums all accounts by currency; many child pages fetch that overview. `server/services/oxsteedCreditAdminService.js:824` and `:995` use offset lists. Existing indexes and bounded limits matter, but do not establish six-million-user latency. Introduce cached scoped summaries and immutable ordered cursors where concurrent inserts would otherwise shift pages. Benchmark partner coverage aggregation at `server/controllers/oxsteedCreditController.js:863` separately.

**Expansion scenario.** A partner payout disruption generates 20,000 disputes, pending approvals, and withdrawal inquiries across several currencies. Night-shift compliance officers need oldest-first assigned work, treasury needs reconciliation progress, and regional support needs safe read access. Existing reserves, freezes, journal integrity, and approval machinery should coordinate that response. First-page queues or global-only roles would force central administrators to become a bottleneck despite healthy workers.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Extend existing systems with scoped role bundles, entity/currency selectors, assignment and SLA handovers, independent queue pagination, durable jobs, provider capability states, and accessible outcome receipts. Reuse `client/src/admin/pages/super/oxsteedCredit/shared.jsx:47` dialog focus trapping and credit currency/localization utilities. Avoid blanket claims that accessibility or currency precision is absent; the remaining requirement is executed coverage across every child workflow.

**Dependencies / owner.** Credit engineering owns accounting and replay; treasury owns reconciliation/reserves; risk owns compliance/disputes; IAM owns scope; fulfillment/provider integration teams own cards and money-out evidence; SRE owns queue capacity. Qualified market reviewers and providers decide external approval requirements.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** Browse and assign the oldest of 10,000 items in each queue; counts must include unseen pages. A maker must receive 403 for self-approval and concurrent checkers must produce one execution, preserving the existing tests in `server/__tests__/oxsteedCreditGovernance.test.js:4`. Export one million authorized records through a resumable job with bounded memory. Demonstrate six million seeded accounts, p95 scoped-list latency under two seconds, and zero cross-tenant records. Provider failure drills must leave an auditable unresolved/reconciled outcome without duplicate credit. Run all thirteen child routes and overview in authenticated locale, keyboard, and regional-negative tests with zero fixture skips. `client/scripts/creditAdminIdempotency.test.mjs:5` already tests key reuse; retain and extend it rather than reimplementing idempotency.

## 39. Revenue — `/admin/super/revenue`

**Mission / what exists.** Revenue is an implemented period-based view of platform-ledger receipts, subscriptions, job fees, negative movements, growth, and recent transactions. It includes CSV export, translated transaction/status labels, loading/error handling, and retry. The endpoint joins user information to ledger entries and returns a deliberately small recent list. The enterprise mission is a reproducible commercial report that finance analysts can explore and export safely, with transparent source semantics. It should remain distinct from Money's cross-business map and Financials' ledger inspection.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/Revenue.jsx:13` requests the selected period; `:26` formats a dollar amount; `:31` downloads a blob; `:107` identifies the table as recent transactions. `server/routes/admin.js:248` and `:249` protect both summary and export with `view_financials`. `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:1522` builds the report, `:1541` counts all positive amounts as total revenue, `:1544` labels all negative amounts refunds, `:1574` caps the table at 50, and `:1593` implements export. `client/scripts/adminRouteContracts.test.mjs:63` checks summary-route permission wiring; this does not test large exports, financial classification, or browser behavior.

**Findings.** **T39-F01 — High [code observation]: the export has no inspected row or memory bound.** The query at `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:1605` selects the entire chosen period without LIMIT, and `:1618` maps all rows into strings before joining a second CSV representation at `:1623`. `client/src/admin/pages/super/Revenue.jsx:34` then receives a complete blob. A year of receipts for millions of users can consume substantial database, server, and browser resources. This differs from capped Credit exports. Use queued export jobs with immutable period/scope snapshots and bounded chunks; retain a small interactive export limit.

**T39-F02 — High [code observation]: sign-based labels overstate their accounting meaning.** `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:1541` includes every positive ledger source in total revenue; `:1544` treats every negative amount as a refund, and `:1567` generates transaction status from amount sign. Those are current software definitions, not validated accounting rules. A negative adjustment or outgoing fee is not proven to be a customer refund. Introduce a finance-approved versioned classification using source type, original transaction, and accounting purpose. Preserve the original ledger amount and show an unmapped category explicitly.

**T39-F03 — High [design requirement]: currency, entity, and reporting-zone controls are required.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/Revenue.jsx:28` renders `$` plus two decimals, and the selected-period query at `:1545` has no currency/entity grouping. Do not infer that the production ledger already contains mixed currencies. Establish that invariant or group currencies before worldwide rollout, and distinguish native and converted figures. The current global grant boundary correctly blocks regional-only grants; add scoped reporting APIs instead of treating a client market filter as an access control.

**T39-F04 — Medium [code observation]: CSV quoting is conditional only on a comma.** `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:1620` quotes a field only when it contains a comma; quotes and newlines alone are not sufficient, and spreadsheet formula prefixes are not neutralized. The exported user-email field is source data, so the consumer contract must handle untrusted cells. Use a tested CSV serializer and explicit spreadsheet-safe text handling. This finding describes the serializer's behavior, not proof of a current malicious email record.

**Expansion scenario.** A revenue analyst exports a year for a regional board review while support teams are busiest. A large synchronous request competes for resources, and the downloaded file may conflate adjustments with refunds. The analyst needs a scoped, complete export that survives a browser reconnect, carries its classification version, and gives another analyst the same result from the same reporting cutoff.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Add asynchronous export progress/cancellation, bounded preview, saved date/entity/currency filters, source definitions, original-transaction drill-through, and snapshot provenance. Explicitly retain the “recent” label for the 50-row sample. Provide a complete paginated drill-down rather than turning the current overview into an unbounded table. Use locale-aware money and date formatting, accessible filter names, and status announcements for export completion.

**Dependencies / owner.** Finance owns classifications; data engineering owns snapshot/export processing; IAM owns scope; admin frontend owns filtering and accessible progress; application security reviews exported-cell handling.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** Export one million synthetic records without application memory growing with total file size, with resumable progress and a complete row count/checksum. CSV fixtures containing commas, quotes, newlines, and formula prefixes must remain valid and inert in approved consumers. Negative adjustments must be distinct from refunds. Two analysts using the same cutoff must reproduce identical totals. Regional direct API and export attempts outside scope must return 403; authenticated browser tests must execute rather than skip.

## 40. Fee Config — `/admin/super/fees`

**Mission / what exists.** Fee Config already supports individual fee edits, required numeric/range validation, confirmation dialogs, optional reason capture, change history, reset, and subscription-price synchronization. It uses the existing `fee_config` store and fee calculator. The server makes writes super-admin-only while allowing financially authorized admins to read and preview. This is a useful global policy surface, but a worldwide workforce needs controlled releases, accurate before/after evidence, and provider-state visibility for each change.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/FeeConfig.jsx:82` submits key/value/reason; `:175` confirms; `:188` displays history; `:253` loads configuration/history; `:277` resets. `server/routes/feeConfig.js:6`–`:10` enforce read/write separation. `server/controllers/feeConfigController.js:13` performs strict numeric parsing, `:92` checks stored min/max, `:101` inserts history, `:116` updates configuration, `:141` synchronizes subscription prices, and `:225` resets rows. `server/migrations/017_phase6_fee_config.sql:9` defines numeric values, limits, and timestamps. Its schema provides a foundation; no new fee database is needed.

**Findings.** **T40-F01 — High [code observation]: a fee change and its history are not atomic or versioned.** The read at `server/controllers/feeConfigController.js:67`, audit INSERT at `:101`, and UPDATE at `:116` are separate pool queries. There is no transaction or expected revision in this controller. Two operators can log the same old value and then overwrite each other; an UPDATE failure can leave a history entry claiming a change that never took effect. Use a transaction, row lock or compare-and-swap revision, and transactional audit. Expose conflicts instead of silently replacing stale intent.

**T40-F02 — High [code observation]: provider synchronization failure still returns success.** `syncSubscriptionToStripe` catches errors at `:184`, logs them, and returns; the outer handler responds success at `:134`. The store can therefore hold the new fee while the plan/provider state remains old. Add a durable provider-sync operation with status, retry key, receipt, and visible “pending/failed synchronization” outcome. Do not claim Stripe is unavailable now; this is the implemented failure path. The USD provider currency at `:155` also needs explicit applicability before regional subscription policy expands.

**T40-F03 — High [code observation]: reset can partially apply without equivalent synchronization.** The reset loop at `:233` separately logs and updates each changed key, then invalidates only the fee cache at `:254`. It does not call the subscription synchronization path or the trial-cache invalidation used by individual writes. An interrupted reset can leave a mixed policy revision. Release a complete policy version atomically, with one operation receipt and the same downstream propagation rules as ordinary updates.

**T40-F04 — Medium [confirmed defect]: preview substitutes defaults for valid zero values.** At `:203`, `:206`, and `:207`, JavaScript `||` replaces zero percentage inputs with nonzero defaults. A requested zero protection/broker percentage cannot be represented faithfully in this preview. Whether every zero rate is allowed in production is a policy question; the preview must either validate/reject it explicitly or calculate it exactly. Reuse the authoritative fee calculator so preview and checkout do not drift.

**Expansion scenario.** Treasury schedules a regional promotion while another operator restores ordinary rates after a provider incident. Concurrent saves and partially propagated subscription changes could produce different prices across checkout and administrative screens. A release must show market applicability, affected products, expected net payouts, and propagation status before the shift hands over.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Extend existing edit/history/preview controls with revisions, required change tickets, impact previews, effective dates, independent approval for material changes, and staged rollout. Display old/new values and units clearly, including whether they are percentages or minor units. Add complete history paging beyond the current `LIMIT 100` at `:48`, a diff view, and a governed rollback operation. Verify labeled numeric inputs, keyboard confirmation, translated units, and locale-specific decimal input.

**Dependencies / owner.** Payments owns authoritative calculation; billing owns Stripe synchronization; finance owns release thresholds; SRE owns cache propagation; admin frontend owns conflict and status UX. Market rules and approvals require jurisdiction/provider review.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** Two saves from one revision yield one success and one 409, with exactly one matching audit row. Inject failures between audit/update and verify no partial commit. Zero-rate previews must match calculator output or return explicit validation. Provider timeout must show pending/failed rather than unconditional success. Reset must apply one policy revision and propagate trial/subscription changes consistently. Verify all serving replicas converge within an agreed 30-second budget.

## 41. Pricing Config — `/admin/super/pricing`

**Mission / what exists.** Pricing Config is a real six-field editor for public tier prices and labels. It loads `/config/pricing`, allows edits, asks for confirmation, and reports save failures. The server stores allowed keys transactionally and writes an admin audit record using the same transaction. It is separate from Fee Config's runtime calculations and Pricing Overrides' country subscription prices. The enterprise mission must make that separation explicit: employees should know whether they are editing customer-facing copy, a chargeable numeric amount, or the actual billing policy.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/PricingConfig.jsx:11` seeds the six fields, `:23` loads public pricing, `:36` sends the form, `:68` defines editable fields, and `:106` confirms immediate change. `server/routes/config.js:6` exposes public reads and `:10` restricts writes by super-admin role. `server/controllers/configController.js:38` defines defaults, `:48` loads current values, `:75` validates and updates, `:88` requires numeric `_price` values, and `:96`–`:117` transactionally stores and audits them. This transaction is a positive existing control, unlike the inspected fee update path.

**Findings.** **T41-F01 — High [confirmed defect]: the default form cannot pass its own server validation unchanged.** Both `client/src/admin/pages/super/PricingConfig.jsx:16` and `server/controllers/configController.js:43` use `tier3_price: 'up to 17%'`. The client submits the entire form at `:40`; the server requires each `_price` entry to match a numeric-only regex at `server/controllers/configController.js:91`. Therefore changing only a label or tier-two price while retaining the default tier-three display string causes validation failure. This is a directly observed contract inconsistency, not a historic SQL claim. Separate display copy from typed charge values or define a consistent explicit schema for percentage-range display fields.

**T41-F02 — High [code observation]: read failures masquerade as valid defaults.** `server/controllers/configController.js:62` catches read errors and responds 200 with `DEFAULT_PRICES` at `:65`. The public page may need a fallback, but the administrative editor cannot distinguish a real stored policy from dependency failure. In combination with a whole-form save, an operator could overwrite valid values using fallback content. Return freshness/provenance and an administrative degraded state that blocks publication until the canonical revision is available.

**T41-F03 — Medium [code observation]: transactionality does not prevent lost updates.** The upsert at `server/controllers/configController.js:101` updates each allowed key without an expected version or read revision. Two regional communications teams can replace each other's labels; the existing audit captures the new state at `:112` but no full before-state comparison. Add a policy revision, compare-and-swap publication, a diff, and a conflict view that retains unsaved edits. Do not duplicate the existing transactional audit machinery.

**T41-F04 — Medium [design requirement]: pricing copy needs market/locale applicability and charge consistency.** Default labels at `:40`–`:44` are free text, while the UI uses English defaults and untyped text fields. The confirmation at `client/src/admin/pages/super/PricingConfig.jsx:109` says changes take effect immediately. A worldwide enterprise needs localized copy, currency/billing-period context, effective dates, previews, and a link to the charge policy used by the relevant plan. It is not established that this editor updates Stripe or runtime fees; expose the intended contract rather than imply it does.

**Expansion scenario.** A French communications lead updates a public tier label while billing publishes a new regional offer. The editor should clearly identify approved French copy and its billing revision. Today, the default tier-three string can reject an otherwise simple save, and a transient database failure can silently load fallback text. Neither issue is evidence that the running deployment is unhealthy overall.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Define typed price/display fields, locale-specific labels, publication preview, draft revision, effective time, and rollback to an audited prior revision. Show public-versus-charge policy explicitly and warn on mismatches supported by actual linked data. Associate each label with its input using `htmlFor`/ID; the current labels at `client/src/admin/pages/super/PricingConfig.jsx:85` lack that explicit connection. Reuse shared accessible confirmation dialogs and validate all supported locale layouts in executed browser tests.

**Dependencies / owner.** Pricing product owns semantics; billing owns charge linkage; localization owns translated copy; IAM verifies the administrative authentication contract; frontend owns publication UX. The general role middleware in `server/routes/config.js:10` should be checked against the admin authentication standard instead of assuming equivalent MFA enforcement.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** Save the default form unchanged and edit only a label successfully under the chosen schema. A failed canonical read must not enable a destructive fallback publication. Concurrent same-revision saves must conflict deterministically. Every displayed tier must identify its locale, currency, applicability, and linked billing revision or explicitly state that it is presentation-only. Keyboard and screen-reader tests must identify every field and confirmation outcome; fixture-dependent tests must not skip.

## 42. Pricing Overrides — `/admin/super/pricing-overrides`

**Mission / what exists.** Pricing Overrides already implements country-level premium pricing, currency comparison statistics, inline edits, reason fields, confirmation, server history, batch updates, and real Stripe synchronization. The client displays configured countries and grouped currency comparisons; the service resolves country prices with a US fallback and local caching. This is an existing regional pricing system, not a missing capability. Enterprise readiness depends on consistent numeric/provider revisions, controlled market delegation, and reproducible rollouts rather than an additional pricing engine.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/PricingOverrides.jsx:18` loads overrides/comparison, `:31` opens an edit, and `:54` saves. `server/routes/pricingOverrides.js:6`–`:12` require super-admin access for list/history/write/batch/sync. `server/controllers/pricingOverrideController.js:43` validates writes, `:112` runs batch updates, and `:179` checks provider configuration before syncing. `server/services/pricingService.js:101` updates prices, `:198` loads history, and `:224` synchronizes real Stripe prices. `server/migrations/160_location_based_pricing.sql:6` defines country, currency, price, optional floors/ceilings, and provider ID. `server/__tests__/pricingServiceStripeSync.test.js:4` tests synchronization and `:63` rejects absent configuration.

**Findings.** **T42-F01 — High [confirmed defect]: changed overrides retain a provider ID that synchronization then skips.** The upsert at `server/services/pricingService.js:147` updates numeric price/currency but does not reset or version `stripe_price_id`. The synchronization loop at `:240` skips any existing ID as already synchronized. Thus an override previously synced at amount A can be saved at amount B and still retain provider price A through another sync call. The current test at `pricingServiceStripeSync.test.js:57` deliberately covers skipping an existing ID, but does not establish equivalence after editing. Bind provider IDs to amount/currency/revision and resynchronize changed revisions idempotently.

**T42-F02 — High [code observation]: pricing history and mutation are separate operations.** `server/services/pricingService.js:125` reads an old value, `:133` inserts history, and `:141` upserts the price using independent pool queries. Concurrent operators can record misleading before-values; failed writes can leave misleading history. Country-level editions also have no expected revision in `client/src/admin/pages/super/PricingOverrides.jsx:56`. Apply transactionality, a revision check, and atomic audit, with a conflict diff that preserves the proposed regional price.

**T42-F03 — Medium [code observation]: batch work has no inspected workload cap or durable result handle.** `server/controllers/pricingOverrideController.js:116` requires a nonempty array, then `:123` processes updates in one HTTP request. Each row can succeed independently, and `:164` returns overall success with a separate errors array. Partial success is reasonable when explicit, but retried requests need an operation ID, per-row idempotency, progress, and resumable failed rows. The country count is finite; thousands of duplicate updates in an unrestricted request still create needless audit/provider workload.

**T42-F04 — High [code observation]: numeric and currency contracts need stronger validation.** The controller at `:50` checks truthiness and negativity, floors input at `:66`, and checks only a three-character currency length at `:53`. Client edit/display logic divides by 100 at `client/src/admin/pages/super/PricingOverrides.jsx:33` and `:111` regardless of currency exponent. Migration floors/ceilings at `160_location_based_pricing.sql:11` exist, but are not consulted by this update service. Require supported country/currency pairings, safe integer minor units, valid exponent formatting, and enforced policy bounds.

**Expansion scenario.** A Japanese market operator proposes a new price while European billing prepares another launch. The country comparison is useful, but a changed amount with an old provider price can make administration and checkout disagree. An interrupted batch also needs a precise receipt showing which markets published, which still await approval, and which failed synchronization.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Extend the existing table with scoped market ownership, scheduled effective revisions, maker/checker release thresholds, provider-sync state, complete history, and rollback to a verified price revision. Preserve the existing real Stripe implementation; add changed-price and ambiguous-provider-result coverage. Make fallback-to-US explicit to operators and customers where product policy requires it, instead of silently treating fallback as local configuration.

**Dependencies / owner.** Billing owns provider revision binding; pricing owns supported currencies/bounds; regional finance owns proposals; IAM owns market scope; admin frontend owns conflict/partial-result UX. Provider currency support and market approvals require provider/jurisdiction review.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** Sync A, edit to B, and sync again: the active override must reference a provider price matching B and its currency. Race two revisions and obtain one conflict with accurate audit. Test JPY and a three-decimal currency without dividing every amount by 100. Enforce floors, ceilings, integer bounds, and unsupported currency rejection. A bounded batch must return durable row receipts and replay without duplicate price creation. Regional operators must never publish outside assigned markets.

## 43. Financials — `/admin/super/financials`

**Mission / what exists.** Financials is an existing platform-ledger inspector with type tabs, page controls, gross/job-fee/subscription/refund summary cards, and CSV export. The client uses `/admin/financials`; the super-prefixed path is the SPA navigation route. Server reads require `view_financials`, while the separate generic export route requires `export_data`. It should support an accountant tracing a movement to its business event, with a stable filtered population and an unambiguous export, rather than pretend to be a complete general-ledger or close product today.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/Financials.jsx:21` fetches type/page/limit 30, `:31` exports using `/admin/export/revenue`, `:78` renders totals, and `:119` changes type/reset page. `server/routes/admin.js:242` protects ledger reads; `:261` protects generic exports separately. `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:1297` implements ledger paging and totals, `:1303` filters rows by type, `:1305` computes unfiltered totals, and `:2427` exports generic data with a bounded limit at `:2440`. `client/scripts/adminRouteContracts.test.mjs:60` verifies the read permission contract. Existing Financials must not be described as absent because its API path differs from its browser route.

**Findings.** **T43-F01 — Medium [code observation]: filtered rows and summary cards describe different populations.** A type condition is used in the row query at `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:1304`, but totals at `:1312` scan the entire ledger. Global totals may be intentional context; the response has no explicit summary-scope contract distinguishing them from the visible subset. Label these cards lifetime/global or return filtered and global totals separately. Provide a visible count and selected date range so an accountant can reconcile the population rather than infer it from a tab name.

**T43-F02 — High [code observation]: export does not preserve the view's filter.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/Financials.jsx:33` calls the generic revenue export without type or page/date scope. `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:2440` exports a newest-first bounded ledger selection. The export can therefore differ from the table and be incomplete for large history. Bounded extraction is safer than an unbounded response, but the UI must show the export scope and completeness. Introduce a full filtered snapshot job with a result count, cap disclosure, and checksum; keep quick samples explicitly labeled.

**T43-F03 — Medium [code observation]: offset paging lacks a stable snapshot and total contract.** `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:1304` orders only by created time and offsets into live rows; `:1314` returns page/limit without a total or immutable cursor. New entries can shift pages and equal timestamps do not provide a deterministic tie-breaker. Add `(created_at,id)` ordering and snapshot/keyset pagination, with totals computed against the same cutoff. This is a query design exposure, not proof that entries were lost from the ledger itself.

**T43-F04 — High [design requirement]: worldwide oversight needs currency/entity and financial-close context.** Card amounts at `client/src/admin/pages/super/Financials.jsx:84` explicitly render US dollars using `en-US`; totals at `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:1307` are not grouped by currency or entity. Establish the existing ledger's currency invariant before treating these as worldwide consolidated numbers. Add period status, classification version, source reference, original-versus-compensating entry links, and reconciliation status. A complete tax or statutory accounting product is not asserted as a legal requirement by this engineering audit.

**T43-F05 — High [confirmed defect]: related manual financial recovery omits a successful HTTP response.** The Financials page itself is a ledger viewer; the associated job-refund API is `server/routes/admin.js:250`. `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:1355` delegates to the existing refund-attempt service, and `:1363` responds 202 for unresolved provider reconciliation. For a succeeded refund it proceeds through audit handling at `:1369`–`:1394` and ends without `res.json`, `res.send`, or another response. The caller can time out after money has already been refunded. Fix the success receipt using the existing attempt ID/status/request ID; retain reconciliation and replay controls. The source-only assertion at `server/__tests__/ws5-audit-evidence.test.js:162` checks audit wording, not response completion. Require an HTTP behavior test of succeeded, pending, replayed, and audit-failure outcomes.

**Expansion scenario.** A quarter-end investigator selects job fees, receives a list of those entries, and downloads a file that also includes subscriptions and adjustments but excludes older records beyond the generic cap. Another investigator enters new movements during paging. The team needs reproducible evidence showing exactly which entries were examined and whether the extract is complete for that period.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Preserve read/export permission separation, but hide or explain an export action when its grant is absent. Add date/entity/currency filters, a stable snapshot badge, scope labels, reference search, complete export jobs, and drill-through to source transactions. Make summaries and table footers explicit about global versus filtered totals. Use locale-aware currency formatting and accessible type selection, table headings, and pagination announcements.

**Dependencies / owner.** Finance controls owns reporting definitions and close snapshots; ledger engineering owns immutable references; data engineering owns pagination/export; IAM owns scoped exports; frontend owns scope clarity. Coordinate with Revenue's export and Money's reporting bridge instead of adding a second ledger.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** A job-fee filter must return a matching filtered total or visibly labeled global total. An export must reproduce the selected cutoff and population exactly, with truncation never implicit. Insert rows during paging and confirm each snapshot record appears once. Benchmark deep history at declared production-like cardinality with p95 two-second pages. A view-only admin must be unable to export by direct API; regional and currency fixtures must preserve their authorized populations and formatting.

## 44. Payouts — `/admin/super/payouts`

**Mission / what exists.** Payouts is an existing helper-payment oversight page with pending, processing, completed, and failed tabs, monetary summary cards, a detail drawer, and a retry confirmation. Its API derives the list from payment records and connected-account information. Failed retries are super-admin-only, guarded by a conditional state update, and completed payments are replay-safe no-ops. This is meaningful recovery functionality. Enterprise operation should make the distinction between gross payment, helper entitlement, transfer, and bank payout explicit and expose complete exception queues.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/Payouts.jsx:17` renders the drawer, `:60` fetches a status list, `:76` requests retry, `:174` identifies failed rows, and `:178` displays `p.amount`. `server/routes/admin.js:243` grants read access and `:246` restricts retries. `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:1398` bounds paging, `:1404` maps tab names to payment states, `:1414` returns gross and helper amounts, `:1430` sums helper payouts, and `:1460` implements retry. Database safeguards also exist in `server/migrations/601_payout_transaction_state_integrity.sql:17` and the payout idempotency index in `620_money_criticals.sql:14`; these protect `payout_transactions`, not automatically every `payments` operation.

**Findings.** **T44-F01 — High [confirmed defect]: displayed row amount is the gross payment, not the helper payout used by summaries.** The API returns both `amount` and `helper_payout` at `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:1414`. Summary totals use `helper_payout` at `:1432`, but the table at `client/src/admin/pages/super/Payouts.jsx:178` and drawer at `:22` show `amount`. With a 100-unit payment and 83-unit helper entitlement, the page presents a 100-unit row against an 83-unit aggregate. Show net helper payout as the principal amount and gross/platform fee as separately labeled detail.

**T44-F02 — Medium [confirmed defect]: successful database states receive the wrong visual treatment.** The completed tab selects `released` records at `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:1407`; the client at `client/src/admin/pages/super/Payouts.jsx:182` assigns the green success style only to `completed`. Likewise, `captured` processing rows do not match the `processing` branch at `:184`. These records fall into the red fallback. Normalize presentation states in one response adapter, preserve raw provider/payment states in details, and test every mapped state rather than comparing incompatible vocabularies.

**T44-F03 — High [code observation]: the UI cannot navigate the bounded API population.** The request at `client/src/admin/pages/super/Payouts.jsx:63` sends status but no page; the page has no list-page state or next-page control. The server defaults to 25 at `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:1401`. A finite list size is appropriate, but millions of users require complete paging, oldest-pending search, provider reference filters, and queue counts. A first-page retry tool cannot constitute a complete global recovery queue.

**T44-F04 — High [code observation]: retry mutation has incomplete durable operating evidence.** The guarded UPDATE at `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:1485` prevents simultaneous retries from both changing a failed row, and `:1474` safely handles already-released payments. Retain those controls. However, the audit call is standalone at `:1502`, its failure catch is empty at `:1513`, and the retry accepts no operator reason. It clears `failure_reason` at `:1487` without an inspected attempt-history receipt in this handler. Make requeue, reason, prior failure, and audit atomic or backed by a durable event, with explicit downstream processing ownership. This is not a claim of double payment today.

**Expansion scenario.** A provider disruption affects 8,000 helper payments. Regional recovery teams need to distinguish blocked accounts, unknown transfer outcomes, refunded payments, and genuinely retryable failures. The current “failed” filter includes refunded/disputed rows (`:1408`), while only actual failed rows can retry. Keep that guard, but make exception types and next actions intelligible and assign work across shifts.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Add complete cursor paging, gross/net breakdown, normalized status badges, accountable exception ownership, age/SLA filters, and provider receipt links. Scoped retry permissions should include amount limits and a reconciliation prerequisite for ambiguous outcomes. Bulk recovery must be a previewed asynchronous job rechecking each payment state, not an unchecked mass status reset. Make the drawer keyboard-accessible with dialog semantics, focus trapping, Escape, and focus restoration; current custom markup at `client/src/admin/pages/super/Payouts.jsx:30` needs executed accessibility verification.

**Dependencies / owner.** Payments and provider integration own state mapping/retry safety; treasury owns reconciliation and threshold policy; IAM owns regional initiation rights; SRE owns worker pickup and alarms; frontend owns amounts/status/complete navigation.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** A gross 100/net 83 fixture shows net 83 consistently. Released and captured rows receive the correct successful/processing labels. All 8,000 exception records must be reachable without unbounded requests. Concurrent retries yield one requeue; completed/refunded/disputed records cannot be replayed incorrectly. Inject audit and provider failures and retain a durable reasoned receipt plus a visible unresolved state. Run keyboard drawer tests and regional direct-API negative tests with zero fixture skips.

## 45. Conversations — `/admin/super/conversations`

**Mission / what exists.** Conversations is an existing privileged investigation console: searchable/status-filtered customer/helper threads, message history, linked quotes, proof of payment, agreements, bookings, and block/archive/reactivate controls. Opening a thread requires a documented break-glass reason. The client sends that reason in a POST body, and the server records a dedicated access row before returning messages. This is a meaningful privacy safeguard. The enterprise mission is controlled case investigation with complete evidence, narrowly delegated authority, and audited decisions across regions and support shifts.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/ConversationsAdmin.jsx:45` loads thread detail; `:51` documents POST-body break-glass; `:268` renders linked artifacts; `:336` requests the list; `:353` changes status; `:515` renders the access-reason modal. `server/routes/admin.js:215`–`:221` require super-admin access for all these operations. `server/controllers/conversationsAdminController.js:12` writes the dedicated access log, `:96` lists conversations, `:167` validates thread reason, `:193` logs access before reading, `:200` fetches messages, and `:238` changes status. `server/__tests__/chatPopAgreementsAdmin.test.js:143` tests break-glass reason normalization; this is not a complete authorization/browser investigation test.

**Findings.** **T45-F01 — High [code observation]: complete history retrieval is unbounded while list navigation is incomplete.** Thread messages at `server/controllers/conversationsAdminController.js:200` have no limit or cursor. The list uses raw parsed page/limit values at `:98` and `:136`, without the bounded parser used by finance. The client request at `client/src/admin/pages/super/ConversationsAdmin.jsx:337` sends search/status but no page, and its footer at `:478` only states showing count. Add bounded list and chronological message cursors; ensure an old thread is reachable without fetching its entire history.

**T45-F02 — High [code observation]: API status writes do not enforce the UI's reason or stale-state expectations.** The client requires a reason before confirmation at `:183`; the server allows `reason || null` at `server/controllers/conversationsAdminController.js:258`. It reads status and performs an unconditional UPDATE at `:249`, then treats audit as best effort at `:261`. An API caller can bypass the reason prompt, and competing shifts can reverse each other's moderation decisions. Enforce reason, expected status/version, and transactional audit; preserve the existing allowed status list.

**T45-F03 — Medium [code observation]: linked-evidence failures resemble absence.** Artifact queries at `server/controllers/conversationsAdminController.js:44`–`:81` catch failures into empty arrays. The client displays only the first eight items per category at `client/src/admin/pages/super/ConversationsAdmin.jsx:283`, `:293`, `:303`, and `:313`. This is a real artifact view, but it does not establish investigation completeness. Distinguish unavailable from empty, expose total counts/cursors, and show whether payment requests returned by the API are represented in the investigation UI. An investigator should not infer “no agreement” from an unavailable source.

**T45-F04 — High [design requirement]: reason-only global break-glass is insufficient workforce delegation.** The super-admin restriction prevents ordinary agents from indiscriminate reads, but global access should remain exceptional. Add assigned-case access, scoped investigative permissions, purpose/ticket reference, expiry, read audit review, and supervisor escalation for cross-region cases. Retain the dedicated access log's fail-before-read behavior at `:193`. Do not route every normal support interaction through an all-powerful account or recreate the existing support inbox.

**Expansion scenario.** A cross-border marketplace dispute spans a year of messages, several payment requests, and dozens of agreement versions. One team archives the thread for retention review while another blocks it for safety. Investigation requires a stable case record, earliest messages, complete linked artifacts, and reasoned conflict handling. Current first-page navigation and unrestricted message retrieval make this difficult at a six-million-user scale.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Extend this investigation view with case assignment, scoped access, expiring break-glass sessions, complete evidence cursors, and explicit unavailable-source badges. Provide a sanitized asynchronous evidence bundle only under authorized scope, with retention settings reviewed by qualified stakeholders. Add debounced search, reference lookup, redacted previews, original-language display, and accessible transcript navigation. The custom modal at `client/src/admin/pages/super/ConversationsAdmin.jsx:515` needs dialog role, accessible naming, focus confinement/restoration, and executed screen-reader/keyboard tests.

**Dependencies / owner.** Trust/support operations own investigation workflow; privacy engineering owns access review and redaction; IAM owns purpose/scope; messaging owns bounded history and artifact availability; frontend owns accessibility and evidence completeness.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** Navigate a 100,000-message synthetic thread using bounded pages and reach the earliest message. A missing reason on direct status API must return 400; concurrent same-version decisions must yield one 409. Deny read when access-log persistence fails. Inject an artifact-source error and render unavailable, not absent. Every privileged read must identify actor, purpose, case, scope, and time. Execute translated modal/transcript tests and regional-negative authorization checks without matrix skips.

## 46. Admin Accounts — `/admin/super/admin-accounts`

**Mission / what exists.** Admin Accounts implements creation, role selection, activation/deactivation, activity inspection, and forced logout. The account-management grant delegates ordinary administrator management, while creating or disabling a super-admin remains restricted to a super-admin. Server admin authentication checks active status, token revocation, and TOTP enrollment. Disabling an account updates sessions and sends a live logout signal. The enterprise mission is controlled joiner/mover/leaver administration for authorized employees without converting the entire 3,500-person workforce into privileged platform administrators.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/AdminAccounts.jsx:23` creates an account, `:83` selects role, `:210` changes status, and `:222` forces logout. `server/routes/admin.js:224`–`:228` enforce `manage_admin_accounts`; `:188` makes force logout super-admin-only. `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:2885` lists accounts, `:2929` creates, `:2973` toggles status, and `:3014` pages activity. `server/middleware/adminAuth.js:29` requires TOTP, `:52` checks active users, and `:19` checks revocation. `server/__tests__/adminPermissionScope.test.js:23` covers scoped-grant rejection; `server/__tests__/adminAuthSecurity.test.js:41` covers revoked admin tokens. Existing role and revocation safeguards must be credited accurately.

**Findings.** **T46-F01 — Medium [code observation]: account discovery loads all admins and historical activity aggregates.** `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:2887` returns the entire administrative population, with correlated COUNT/MAX audit subqueries at `:2891` and `:2892`, and no pagination. This may be acceptable for a small privileged group, but auditors and regional managers need bounded search, manager/department scope, status/MFA filters, and aggregate activity summaries. The expected workforce count does not mean all employees should appear as admins; preserve the separate employee/internal-staff model at `:2905`.

**T46-F02 — High [verification gap]: the promised first-login change is not established by account creation.** The response at `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:2965` says users must change their password on first login. The INSERT at `:2950` stores no mandatory-change or invitation state; the inspected authentication/migration searches did not establish a linked first-login enforcement mechanism. This is a missing proof, not a claim that password hashing is absent: hashing at `:2948` is present. Prefer a user-owned enrollment invitation tied to identity, expiry, and MFA. If temporary enrollment remains, demonstrate enforced rotation before privileged action and avoid administrator-mediated distribution as the scalable onboarding model.

**T46-F03 — High [code observation]: status changes have a race guard but incomplete transactional lifecycle.** `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:2986` compares the old active state and returns 409 on concurrent change, which is a positive control. Session invalidation at `:2991`, live notification at `:2995`, and standalone audit at `:2996` occur after the account update. A later failure can leave an uncertain UI outcome; reactivation can also expose old grants that remain active. Use an explicit lifecycle operation receipt, durable audit, and reactivation review of grants/assignments.
