Do not describe inactive users as retaining admin API access: the middleware independently rejects them.

**T46-F04 — High [design requirement]: delegated management needs organizational bounds and separation of duties.** The global `manage_admin_accounts` grant can create/manage ordinary admins across the platform; the server protects super-admin targets at `:2982`, but no departmental management scope is resolved in these handlers. Add team/entity-scoped account managers, privileged-role approval, sponsor ownership, access-review due dates, and HR/identity-provider lifecycle integration where the enterprise selects that model. These enhancements should feed the existing account/grant stores rather than create shadow identities.

**Expansion scenario.** A 200-person regional support center joins, transfers to a contractor organization, and later offboards. Their managers need to approve roles, confirm MFA readiness, remove privileges promptly, and retain audit evidence. Unbounded lists and manually administered enrollment cannot make that chain reproducible; an inactive flag alone does not show whether all assigned access has been reviewed before later reactivation.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Add sponsor/team metadata, search/pagination, enrollment state, MFA/device/session indicators, expected-state actions, expiring elevated roles, and a reviewed reactivation checklist backed by actual data. Show that force logout is unavailable to a delegated manager until authorized, rather than inviting an action that the API correctly rejects. Make custom creation/activity modals accessible with focus management and explicit field labels; verify supported locales rather than claiming translations do not exist.

**Dependencies / owner.** IAM/security owns lifecycle and approval; people operations owns employment signals/sponsors; regional managers own access reviews; admin frontend owns discoverability; SRE verifies prompt revocation across sockets and sessions.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** Onboard 200 synthetic employees through individual invitation/enrollment records without granting super-admin by default. Prove first-use rotation/enrollment gates or correct the promise. Offboarding must block fresh admin API calls and revoke live sessions within 60 seconds. Reactivation must show reviewed grants, not silently resume expired employment authority. Same-version status races must conflict. Unauthorized team managers must receive 403 on cross-team creation/status/activity APIs; executed keyboard and locale tests must cover custom dialogs.

## 47. Permissions — `/admin/super/permissions`

**Mission / what exists.** Permissions implements a scope catalog, time-limited grants, active/expired/revoked displays, creation, and revocation. The API validates supported permissions, future expiry no more than one year, active regular-admin grantees, and optional region IDs. Only super-admins can create/revoke; delegated account managers can inspect according to the server's narrower visibility rule. Global APIs explicitly refuse regional-only grants. This is an existing permission system to expand, not a reason to construct another role database.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/PermissionGrants.jsx:61` creates the modal, `:63` defines its form, `:73` loads active admins, `:276` fetches grants/scopes, and `:292` revokes. `server/routes/admin.js:295` provides self-lookup and `:300`–`:303` protect catalog/list/write operations. `server/controllers/adminPermissionController.js:7` defines scopes, `:71` lists grants, `:126` creates, `:143` bounds expiry, `:166` supports region scoping, and `:202` revokes. `server/migrations/049_admin_search_and_permissions.sql:42` defines grants and `:54` their indexes. `server/__tests__/adminPermissionScope.test.js:23` verifies a regional grant cannot authorize a global financial endpoint.

**Findings.** **T47-F01 — High [confirmed defect]: the UI cannot express existing region-scoped grants.** The creation form at `client/src/admin/pages/super/PermissionGrants.jsx:63` includes grantee, permissions, expiry, and notes, but no region ID or region selection. It posts that form at `:94`; the server defaults absent region to null at `server/controllers/adminPermissionController.js:129`. Administrators using this screen therefore create global grants even though the API and schema support a region. Add an explicit scope picker with a clearly marked global option, resolved eligible regions, and confirmation of the resulting reach. This is not a missing region API.

**T47-F02 — High [code observation]: list caps obscure review completeness.** `server/controllers/adminPermissionController.js:114` limits grant inspection to 200 rows with no pagination metadata; self-lookup at `:55` limits active grants to 50. `client/src/admin/pages/super/PermissionGrants.jsx:306` computes active counts from the returned slice. An access reviewer can mistake the visible population for the whole workforce, and the client guard can omit older effective grants while the server still recognizes them. Add complete paginated review and effective-permission computation on the server, preserving fail-closed client behavior.

**T47-F03 — High [code observation]: authorization changes and audit are not committed together.** Grant insertion at `server/controllers/adminPermissionController.js:176` precedes a standalone audit call at `:183`; revocation at `:216` likewise precedes standalone audit at `:223`. The audit helper's default failure behavior is explicitly best effort (`server/services/auditService.js:12` and `:60`). Add transaction-bound audit and idempotent request receipts, so reviewers never have to infer whether a permission change committed after an error. Use conditional revoke/state checks to identify simultaneous review decisions.

**T47-F04 — High [design requirement]: the catalog needs an effective-capability contract and organizational delegation.** Some catalog entries promise operations that remain super-admin-only, such as verify/delete user routes at `server/routes/admin.js:183` and `:185`; catalog text at `server/controllers/adminPermissionController.js:13` and `:14` should not imply that granting those scopes authorizes those routes. Conversely, financial global-only checks are deliberately restrictive. Show which endpoints each scope currently enables, which require extra constraints, and which are reserved. Extend the existing catalog with team/tenant/region constraints, approval thresholds, incompatible-role rules, and recurring access attestation.

**Expansion scenario.** A global access review includes several grants per employee, temporary incident permissions, and regional holiday coverage. The latest 200 grants are insufficient to find a long-standing high-risk grant. A reviewer must see effective permissions, overlaps, sponsor, expiry, business scope, and the exact operations enabled, then revoke in bounded batches without accidentally elevating every grant to global.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Add explicit scope, searchable assignees, role templates built from the existing catalog, effective-permission previews, conflict checks, sponsor/reason, and time-zone-aware expiry. Use a dry-run bulk grant/revoke job that previews affected users and reauthorizes each action. Preserve API expiry validation and super-admin boundaries until constrained delegation is implemented. Replace native confirmation-only review with accessible context showing which grant and reach will be revoked.

**Dependencies / owner.** IAM owns effective capability and grant transactions; security owns incompatible access and review cadence; department owners sponsor privileges; admin frontend owns scope visibility and complete lists.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** Create a regional grant through the UI and verify persisted region plus denied global access. Inspect 20,000 grants with complete totals and locate the oldest active record. An effective-permission endpoint must include all valid grants beyond the current 50-row slice. Inject audit failure and verify atomic rollback. Every catalog scope must map to tested route behavior. Bulk review must return per-user receipts without privilege expansion; translated expiry controls and keyboard dialogs must pass configured authenticated tests.

## 48. Regions — `/admin/super/regions`

**Mission / what exists.** Regions implements geographic territory CRUD, search/type filtering, assignment counts, and boundaries for zip ranges, city/state, radius, country/region, and whole countries. The API validates known country/region values and blocks ordinary deletion while active grants reference a region. Regional matching exists on the server, including a retryable fail-closed response when region lookup fails. The enterprise mission is stable geographic authorization with clear ownership, international identifiers, and change previews; a geographic region must not be confused with a tenant or legal entity.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/RegionsManager.jsx:266` loads regions, `:283` filters client-side, and `:322` confirms deletion. `server/routes/admin.js:340`–`:343` protect CRUD with super-admin checks. `server/controllers/adminRegionController.js:18` validates boundaries, `:54` supports country/region, `:95` lists, `:155` updates, and `:221` deletes. `server/middleware/regionAuth.js:91` dispatches matching, `:119` loads active grants, `:146` marks lookup unavailability, and `:163` enforces target-user access. `server/__tests__/regionAuthFailClosed.test.js:25` tests lookup failure, while `:34` deliberately preserves unrestricted legacy behavior for an empty successful region result.

**Findings.** **T48-F01 — High [code observation]: expiry can remove a geographic restriction instead of denying all scope.** Region hydration at `server/middleware/regionAuth.js:135` selects only active grants; the target check at `:178` allows an empty region list. Where an administrator has another valid global action grant, an expiring region restriction can therefore broaden their geographic reach. This is the explicit legacy contract, not a database lookup failure bypass—the latter was repaired and fails closed at `:168`. Replace the empty-array interpretation with explicit unrestricted versus restricted/empty authorization states, and bind regions to the permission being exercised rather than collecting every regional grant indiscriminately.

**T48-F02 — High [code observation]: deletion safety relies on a nontransactional check and a nulling foreign key.** `server/controllers/adminRegionController.js:230` counts active references, then `:240` deletes separately. `server/migrations/115_super_admin_regions.sql:32` makes the grant-region foreign key `ON DELETE SET NULL`, repeated in migration 119. Null means global in `server/middleware/adminAuth.js:145`. An interleaved grant creation and region deletion could broaden the new grant if not otherwise blocked. This is a concurrency exposure requiring a real-database test, not a claim that ordinary protected deletions currently succeed. Use retirement, restrictive references, or a transactional locking protocol shared by grant creation/deletion; never convert restricted authority into global through deletion.

**T48-F03 — High [code observation]: boundary edits instantly change existing authority without a revision.** The update at `server/controllers/adminRegionController.js:193` applies a new definition to the same ID, with no expected version or affected-user preview; audit at `:198` is best effort. An accidental expansion changes every active grant referencing that ID. Add immutable boundary revisions, an impact preview, independent approval for large expansions, effective dates, and transactional audit. Retain duplicate-name handling and existing boundary validation.

**T48-F04 — Medium [code observation]: legacy boundary types are geographically ambiguous.** Zip matching at `server/middleware/regionAuth.js:24` parses the first five characters as a number and does not bind country; city/state at `:35` also compares no country. New country-specific types already exist and should be preferred. Radius validation at `server/controllers/adminRegionController.js:46` checks numeric coordinates but not latitude/longitude bounds. Add explicit country/CRS metadata, coordinate ranges, postal normalization, overlap analysis, and address-quality states. These are source observations, not claims about a specific misassigned production user.

**Expansion scenario.** An employee receives country-specific authority plus a temporary region restriction during a launch. Their assignment expires overnight while another administrator edits a postal territory shared by multiple teams. Support managers need to predict which customers and businesses move into scope, identify overlap, and transfer ownership without changing privileges accidentally.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Extend the existing region editor with country-qualified definitions, boundary revision history, preview counts/maps, overlap warnings, owner/sponsor, and retirement instead of privilege-changing deletion. Add server-side search/pagination when territory counts grow; current list at `server/controllers/adminRegionController.js:97` is unbounded with per-region grant counts. Implement scoped action routes using matching and capability checks together: `server/routes/admin.js:325` still requires a global `manage_users` grant, so regional-only delegation is not complete. Tenant isolation needs an additional ownership boundary.

**Dependencies / owner.** IAM owns explicit scope semantics and deletion safety; geography/data owns normalized boundaries; regional operations owns territory ownership; frontend owns previews and accessible editing. Verify current database constraints in a restored isolated rehearsal before changing historical assumptions.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** Expire the last scoped assignment while another action grant remains and prove authority does not broaden. Race grant creation/deletion repeatedly in PostgreSQL and prove no scoped grant becomes global. Same-revision edits must conflict. Reject out-of-range coordinates and cross-country postal collisions. Preview affected identities for a million-user territory using bounded work. Geographic and tenant-negative tests must pass independently, including missing-address and lookup-outage cases.

## 49. Internal Access — `/admin/super/logistics-internal-access`

**Mission / what exists.** Internal Access is a real staff access-management interface, listing eligible employees and administrators, showing corporate departments and eligible workspaces, searching/filtering setup state, and editing operations/compliance/admin-portal access. It extends existing internal workspace and corporate assignment models. The underlying gate has hashed access material, active-user eligibility checks, route eligibility, lockout handling, login-history records, and recovery logic. Enterprise readiness requires reliable UUID-based provisioning, accountable access changes, and a scalable joiner/mover/leaver workflow without treating gate enablement as complete business-data authorization.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/LogisticsInternalAccess.jsx:95` loads records, `:137` filters them, `:209` updates the selected user's record, and `:623` confirms save. `server/routes/admin.js:230`–`:234` require super-admin access for self/list/other-user provisioning. `server/controllers/logisticsInternalAccessController.js:84` saves access, `:139` checks route eligibility, `:281` lists corporate-linked staff, `:377` provisions a target user, and `:813` implements one-use recovery. `server/migrations/276_logistics_internal_access.sql:3` declares the user foreign key UUID and `:15` creates case-insensitive name uniqueness; migration `277_logistics_internal_access_admin_portal.sql:1` adds portal eligibility. `server/utils/internalWorkspaces.js:63` checks active corporate department assignments. `server/__tests__/accessGateRateLimit.test.js:39` covers lockout checks, not this full provisioning flow.

**Findings.** **T49-F01 — High [confirmed defect]: other-user provisioning rejects UUID identities.** The client at `client/src/admin/pages/super/LogisticsInternalAccess.jsx:209` puts `selectedRecord.user_id` into the URL. The listing returns `u.id` at `server/controllers/logisticsInternalAccessController.js:285`; users are UUIDs (`server/migrations/001_initial_schema.sql:122` and `276_logistics_internal_access.sql:3`). Yet `server/controllers/logisticsInternalAccessController.js:379` converts the path value with `Number()` and rejects nonpositive/noninteger values at `:380`. Normal UUID staff IDs therefore return 400 before `saveAccessRecord` is reached. Self-setup uses a separate handler at `:254`, so this does not establish that every access flow is broken. Correct and test the shared ID contract before scaling provisioning.

**T49-F02 — High [code observation]: administrative access updates lack durable actor/reason evidence in the inspected save path.** `saveAccessRecord` performs the update at `:143` and returns `updated_by_user_id` only in response data at `:183`. The provisioning handlers at `:254` and `:377` do not persist an admin audit record or change reason for enablement, disablement, or route changes. Successful gate-login history is a different event. Add transactional access-change evidence with actor, subject, before/after route state, purpose, and operation ID. Keep sensitive access material out of these records.

**T49-F03 — High [code observation]: concurrent updates have no expected revision or proven active-session invalidation.** The UPDATE at `:154` selects only by user ID; there is no revision check. A stale operator can restore route enablement or active state after another shift disables it. The save path does not establish revocation of previously issued sessions/gate tokens. Whether every downstream workspace rechecks eligibility is a verification gap, not a confirmed bypass. Add compare-and-swap state and a tested revocation/version mechanism covering already-issued access, while preserving the ordinary admin MFA gate.

**T49-F04 — Medium [code observation]: full staff enumeration and per-user eligibility work need a bounded workflow.** The list at `:283` joins users, access records, and corporate assignments, has no LIMIT through `:336`, then derives eligible routes. The client filters all records at `client/src/admin/pages/super/LogisticsInternalAccess.jsx:139`. With 3,500 employees, this may still function, but has no inspected scoped manager view or durable bulk onboarding. Add indexed server search, bounded pages, department eligibility totals, and previewed asynchronous enrollment/revocation jobs. Reuse corporate assignments; do not create a second employee directory.

**Expansion scenario.** A warehouse opens with 300 staff; some require operations, others compliance, and finance staff require a corporate workspace. The workforce administrator currently encounters the UUID mismatch when provisioning another user. After that defect is fixed, concurrent access changes and shift handover still need durable receipts, department ownership, and timely revocation. A “ready” badge must describe valid route eligibility, not imply unrestricted tenant/warehouse authority.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Correct UUID handling, add access revision/reason/audit, sponsor/expiry, department-scoped management, and lifecycle synchronization. Show exact workspace eligibility and downstream data scope separately. Link existing lockouts and recovery without exposing private access material. Provide bulk dry-run, per-row conflicts, and rollback/revoke outcomes for the authorized workforce. Validate names and labels with associated form controls, keyboard confirmation, Arabic layout, and accessible result announcements.

**Dependencies / owner.** IAM owns provisioning IDs, lifecycle, and revocation; people/corporate operations owns employment and assignment changes; logistics owns warehouse data scope; security owns access-change audit and gate policy; frontend owns complete search and safe bulk UX.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** Provision and disable a real-format UUID staff fixture through the actual route and obtain the correct persisted result. Reject invalid IDs before database writes. Same-version concurrent edits must produce one 409. Every route enable/disable change must persist a redacted actor/reason/before/after receipt atomically. Previously issued access must stop authorizing revoked workspaces within 60 seconds in executed tests. Onboard 300 synthetic staff through bounded jobs with complete row outcomes, and prove an assigned manager cannot provision outside their department/warehouse scope.

## 50. Gate Lockouts — `/admin/super/access-gate-lockouts`

**Mission / what exists.** This tab restores employee access after repeated failures at the internal gate while retaining evidence of suspicious attempts. It is an implemented, localized super-admin page, not a missing security feature. Operators see IP/name pairs, failure counts, first-failure time, expiry, relative time remaining, and recent attempts. Refresh, failed-only filtering, error recovery, and a reason-oriented confirmation dialog already exist. The database rate limiter is shared through PostgreSQL; recovery-email and CLI paths also exist. Admin API authentication rechecks active users, token revocation, and configured two-factor authentication, so a recovery proposal should preserve that boundary rather than create a new authentication system.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/AccessGateLockouts.jsx:22` loads lockouts and 100 attempts; `:43` performs the lift. `server/routes/admin.js:237` restricts all three endpoints to super-admin. `server/controllers/logisticsInternalAccessController.js:663` fixes the lockout list at 200, `:673` handles lifts, and `:702` accepts attempt filters. `server/services/accessGateRateLimitService.js:281` bounds and offsets list queries; `:302` records lift actor/reason/source with a conditional update. `server/migrations/278_access_gate_rate_limit.sql:34` supplies attempt indexes and `:65` a partial unique active-pair index. `server/__tests__/accessGateRateLimit.test.js:39` covers lockout checks, including expiry; these are source-inspected tests, not a concurrent production trial.

**Findings.** **T50-F01 — P1 [confirmed defect]: the server permits a reasonless lift.** The controller trims the reason but passes `reason || null` at `logisticsInternalAccessController.js:679`; the service stores null at `accessGateRateLimitService.js:313`. A direct request therefore bypasses the reason-oriented UI expectation. This is an accountability mismatch, not proof of unauthorized access: super-admin authorization still applies.

**T50-F02 — P2 [code observation]: the visible incident queue is truncated.** The controller returns at most 200 active rows with no continuation metadata, while the page has no lockout pagination and loads only 100 recent attempts. Service-level offset support does not make older cases reachable from this tab. A widespread gate incident can hide affected staff behind the newest rows without a visible “more results” warning.

**T50-F03 — P1 [design requirement]: worldwide recovery cannot depend on unrestricted super-admin staffing.** Both client route and API require that role. Regional help desks have no scoped lockout-review capability in this route chain. Existing global permission middleware intentionally rejects regional grants (`server/middleware/adminAuth.js:126`); that is protective behavior, not a regional-scope bypass. Safe delegation must identify the staff account and its assignment before authorizing a lift, since a claimed name and IP alone are weak scope identifiers.

**Expansion scenario.** At an Asia-Pacific shift change, 280 staff behind several shared egress addresses are locked out while a headquarters security analyst is investigating deliberate failures from another region. The first 200 lockouts cannot serve as the entire incident inventory. Several help-desk staff need to recover known employees without viewing every claimed identity or lifting a suspicious pair belonging to a different incident. Concurrent lifts should show “already resolved,” preserve the original actor, and avoid encouraging repeated requests.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Enforce a bounded nonblank reason and incident identifier server-side; expose account resolution confidence and require stronger review where identity cannot be resolved. Add stable cursor pagination, totals or an explicit estimated count, country/workspace filters, absolute UTC plus operator-local timestamps, and a visible truncation indicator. Extend existing audit/recovery paths with an expiring scoped recovery grant, shift owner, escalation timer, and reviewed emergency procedure. Make success and already-lifted responses distinguishable and accessible. Recovery-email delivery should expose provider failure and a tested alternate channel; do not infer successful delivery from a generic acknowledgement.

**Dependencies / owner.** Security Engineering owns authorization and recovery policy; Identity owns staff/account association; regional service desks own incident response; SRE owns database contention and email-provider rehearsal.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** Blank reasons return 400 without updating a row. A regional operator cannot list or lift another region’s resolved staff pair through direct API calls. A synthetic 10,000-row queue remains fully traversable, with p95 list response below one second at 50 simultaneous readers. Fifty concurrent lifts produce one recorded transition. A staffed recovery exercise resolves 95% of verified employee cases within 15 minutes, including keyboard-only operation in all five supported UI languages.

## 51. Create User — `/admin/super/users/create`

**Mission / what exists.** This tab creates a complete account for an administrator-assisted customer or employee onboarding journey. `CreateUser` delegates to a real shared form and navigates to the resulting user detail. The form supports numerous account types, including operational employees, logistics, business personas, admin, and super-admin; country, region, and postal inputs use existing country helpers. The API validates identity fields and geography, normalizes email and state, maps account type to role/default mode, hashes the submitted password, chooses default currency, and creates helper profiles where needed. User creation and structured auditing share a transaction. Those are substantial foundations that should be extended for workforce onboarding rather than replaced with another user database.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/CreateUser.jsx:33` mounts `CreateUserForm`. `client/src/components/CreateUserForm.jsx:18` defines account types, `:85` constructs the request, and `:356` exposes verification toggles. `server/routes/admin.js:315` makes creation super-admin-only. `server/controllers/superAdminUserController.js:234` validates inputs, `:272` checks normalized-email uniqueness, `:296` starts transactional creation, `:303` writes verification flags, `:315` marks onboarding complete, and `:341` records the user action. `server/migrations/116_admin_user_action_log.sql:10` provides the structured log; `server/__tests__/adminAuthSecurity.test.js` and `server/__tests__/adminPermissionScope.test.js:23` address adjacent admin boundaries, not proof of the full onboarding lifecycle.

**Findings.** **T51-F01 — P1 [code observation]: creation treats completion and verification as administrative assertions.** The insert sets onboarding/contact/profile/terms fields true even though the form is not a user acceptance ceremony (`superAdminUserController.js:315`). Email, identity, and background-check flags are accepted from the body. That can be intentional assisted setup, but the resulting account needs provenance distinguishing an administrative assertion from provider verification and a versioned user acceptance. It should not silently inherit the assurance level of an independently checked employee.

**T51-F02 — P1 [design requirement]: bulk workforce onboarding and ordinary provisioning require narrower privileges.** Creating one employee currently requires the same global role able to create another super-admin. The route correctly blocks regular admins, yet no bounded HR provisioner workflow is exposed here. At 3,500 staff, forcing every joiner through headquarters increases privileged-account use and delays shift staffing. Existing role mappings and corporate assignments should remain the source of access; a provisioner must not assign arbitrary admin or verification flags.

**T51-F03 — P2 [code observation]: there is no request-level recovery contract for assisted creation.** The form sends one POST without an idempotency key (`CreateUserForm.jsx:101`); the controller performs a pre-insert uniqueness lookup and returns raw error detail for failures (`superAdminUserController.js:370`). A network timeout after commit can leave an operator uncertain whether to retry. Email uniqueness limits duplication but is not an operator-friendly replay/result contract. Also, `business_name` is collected and destructured but not persisted in the displayed creation insert; the UI should clarify what creating a business persona actually creates.

**Expansion scenario.** HR must onboard 400 seasonal logistics employees in four countries before Monday, while a support supervisor creates a corrected customer account. Operators need staged imports, row-specific errors, invitations, assignments, and reconciliation against existing emails. They must not distribute administrator-chosen permanent passwords or mark applicants background-checked merely to finish a form. A partially interrupted import needs a resumable result list, not hundreds of manual duplicate lookups.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Add an invitation-first option to the existing account creation flow, with expiring activation, user-set credentials, and explicit enrollment requirements. Require evidence and separate permissions for assurance overrides and privileged roles; capture who asserted each flag, why, and its source/reference. Use the existing legal acceptance store for later genuine acceptance instead of treating a boolean as equivalent evidence. Add a scoped provisioner grant restricted to allowed employee types, country, department, and business assignment. Implement a dry-run import followed by a bounded durable job using per-row idempotency and downloadable results; show created, invited, pending evidence, and failed states separately. Bind labels to inputs and focus validation summaries so localized field errors are usable by assistive technology.

**Dependencies / owner.** Identity Engineering owns creation, invitations, and assurance provenance; HR Operations owns imported workforce records; Security approves privileged-role workflow; Legal Operations reviews acceptance wording and evidence requirements by jurisdiction.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** Replaying a creation key returns the original user ID. A scoped provisioner cannot create an admin, set assurance flags, or cross the permitted geography through direct HTTP calls. A 3,500-row synthetic import supports dry run, cancellation, restart, and per-row reconciliation without duplicate users. Every successful assisted account visibly distinguishes administrative completion from user acceptance. Keyboard and screen-reader review reaches every required field and its error in English, Spanish, French, Haitian Creole, and Arabic.

## 52. Search Logs — `/admin/super/search-logs`

**Mission / what exists.** Search Logs supervises privileged discovery of platform records, helping security and operational leads investigate who searched for sensitive users, messages, jobs, or businesses. The tab already offers a 50-row page, query and administrator filters, summary counters, top queries, top searchers, debounced input, abortable loads, retry, and date formatting. Its API escapes wildcard characters in the search-log query, returns totals, and caps results at 200. This is an administrative search audit, not an end-user search analytics dashboard; recommendations should preserve that mission and avoid conflating popularity with legitimate employee access.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/SearchLogs.jsx:40` establishes filters/paging; `:52` retrieves logs; `:65` separately retrieves statistics; `:78` implements a shared debounce timer. `server/routes/admin.js:284` restricts the list to super-admin. `server/controllers/adminSearchController.js:338` builds the list query, `:349` escapes ILIKE input, `:375` counts results, and `:395` computes statistics. `server/migrations/049_admin_search_and_permissions.sql:7` stores search evidence; `:17` adds administrator/time indexes and an English full-text expression index. `server/__tests__/adminPaginationContracts.test.js:8` inspects general pagination source contracts; it is not a measured search-log query plan or a specific negative-limit behavioral test.

**Findings.** **T52-F01 — P2 [code observation]: statistics repeatedly aggregate the audit population.** The summary scans the entire log for counts/distinct administrators/average results, and top lists group a 30-day window (`adminSearchController.js:397`). List pagination also performs an exact count. At global workforce search rates, historical events can grow much faster than the six-million-user registry. A full-text index on `to_tsvector('english', query)` does not directly index the displayed substring ILIKE predicate; a planner/load check is required before declaring any measured bottleneck.

**T52-F02 — P2 [confirmed defect]: the filter debounce can lose a field update.** Query and admin-ID inputs share one timer. A change to the second field within 400 milliseconds cancels the first pending update; the callback commits only its own key (`SearchLogs.jsx:79`). The visible inputs can therefore disagree with effective request filters. This matters during an investigation where an operator believes a query is constrained to one employee and collects the wrong evidence.

**T52-F03 — P1 [design requirement]: privacy review needs purpose-bound delegation and disclosure controls.** The UI/API require unrestricted super-admin access while returning administrator email, raw query text, and IP-related evidence. Query strings can themselves contain personal data. Existing permission machinery is available, including historical `view_search_logs` terminology, but this route uses a super-admin boundary. Add a specific reviewer capability and scope-aware read path rather than weakening the existing global grant rule or giving every regional auditor a global role.

**T52-F04 — P2 [confirmed defect]: negative list limits are not lower-bounded.** `Math.min(parseInt(limit) || 50, 200)` at `adminSearchController.js:356` allows a negative value through to SQL. This is a request-validation defect; it does not establish SQL injection or an existing production outage. Clamp or reject invalid values consistently with other admin endpoints.

**Expansion scenario.** A security analyst investigates repeated lookups of a prominent restaurant owner by staff in two continents over six months. Several supervisors need restricted evidence, while a central analyst needs cross-region correlation. The operator changes both filters quickly and expects an exact evidentiary subset. Whole-table statistics must not compete with service traffic, and a statistical leaderboard must not be interpreted as proof of misconduct.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Commit both debounced input values as one snapshot, reset continuation consistently, and display effective filters. Add bounded date ranges, stable `(created_at,id)` cursors, summarized time buckets, and separately refreshed/cached statistics with an “as of” timestamp. Evaluate appropriate substring/full-text indexes with actual representative plans; do not simply add a redundant index. Add purpose, case ID, masking by default, audited sensitive-value reveal, region-aware reviewer grants, retention ownership, and asynchronous evidence export linked to immutable filter metadata. Present top-search statistics as activity context, not accusations. Preserve localized labels and add announced loading/results states.

**Dependencies / owner.** Security Analytics owns investigation semantics and access; Data Platform owns partitioning/rollups/query plans; Privacy Operations owns disclosure and retention decisions; frontend maintainers own filter consistency.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** Rapid edits to both fields produce the combined filter after debounce. Limits -1, zero, malformed, and oversized are handled deterministically without SQL errors. A 100-million-event synthetic log meets p95 list latency under one second for bounded indexed queries and statistics freshness within five minutes. Reviewer scope tests deny cross-region reads and sensitive reveal without permission. Export manifests reconcile event counts and carry the effective filter, actor, time zone, and case identifier.

## 53. Settings — `/admin/super/settings`

**Mission / what exists.** Settings governs global fees, job parameters, system controls, and feature flags. This is a mature implemented tab: grouped settings, search, dirty indicators, reset, individual and save-all actions, direct rollback, flags, proposal/preview/approval/scheduling, and change history exist. The API validates value types and numeric bounds, locks individual settings, increments versions, and commits audited changes transactionally. Rollback uses an audited previous state as a new forward change. The separate change-management path already enforces proposer/reviewer separation. The enterprise requirement is to make these mechanisms consistent and mandatory where risk warrants it, not propose a second settings service.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/PlatformSettings.jsx:319` defines the change-request API; `:995` checks `approve_settings`; `:1101` saves directly; `:1172` implements sequential save-all. `server/routes/admin.js:253` exposes direct mutation/rollback with `manage_settings`; `server/routes/settingsChanges.js:21` separates proposal from approval permissions. `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:2205` locks, validates, versions, and audits direct changes; `:2248` restores an audited value; `:2311` strictly validates feature flags. `server/migrations/591_admin_control_plane_hardening.sql:8` adds bounds/version metadata. `server/__tests__/adminControlPlaneHardening.test.js:55` covers transactional audit and `:111` rollback; `server/__tests__/settingsChangeRequests.test.js:213` covers separation of duties.

**Findings.** **T53-F01 — P1 [code observation]: approval remains optional for direct global controls.** A user with `manage_settings` can directly PUT a setting or flag and roll it back, even though the proposal workflow needs a separate approver. The direct controller does not select a risk class or enforce an approved request. For global fees, identity requirements, maintenance mode, or release-delay policy, a well-intentioned operator can bypass the very review process the tab provides. This is a current authorization-path difference; whether it violates policy depends on the organization’s adopted policy.

**T53-F02 — P1 [confirmed defect]: a setting version is incremented but not used to reject stale edits.** The client submits `{value}` at `PlatformSettings.jsx:1105`; the server reads the current row under lock then overwrites it without an expected-version comparison (`superAdminController.js:2213`). Row locks serialize writes, but two editors can still overwrite each other’s decisions. The same concern applies to rollback targets that an operator reviewed before another change occurred. A counter alone is not optimistic concurrency control.

**T53-F03 — P2 [code observation]: save-all is a series of independent writes.** The loop at `PlatformSettings.jsx:1183` can commit some values before another request fails. The UI counts successes, which is preferable to pretending atomicity, but coupled fee and escrow parameters need a reviewed change set with explicit all-or-partial semantics. Restoring a previous setting changes future configuration; it cannot undo already executed settlements, refunds, or contractual commitments.

**Expansion scenario.** Europe proposes a fee adjustment for a Monday launch while the Americas incident commander changes maintenance behavior and an escrow delay. A stale headquarters browser then clicks save-all. Individual writes may succeed in an unexpected mixture, and later rollback could restore a setting unrelated to the operator’s intended incident. Worldwide teams need bounded authority, clear ownership, a scheduled effective instant, and evidence that every consuming worker has observed the approved configuration version.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Extend existing change requests with risk-class enforcement at every mutation endpoint, including flags and rollback. Permit direct low-risk changes where explicitly allowed, and preserve a tightly audited emergency path with expiry and retrospective review. Send expected versions; return 409 with before/current/proposed diff and preserve the operator’s draft. Package coupled settings in one reviewed change set with atomic validation, previewed affected consumers, reason, owner, effective time, and explicit rollback target. Add propagation acknowledgements and stale-cache alarms. Explain fee units, currencies, and future-only rollback effects. A financial recovery plan must reconcile downstream transactions through existing ledger/reconciliation workflows, with finance and jurisdiction review where relevant.

**Dependencies / owner.** Platform Control Plane owns policy/schema; Finance Operations owns fee and settlement impact; Security owns privileged emergency access; SRE owns worker propagation and incident handoff.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** A stale expected version returns 409 and writes neither value nor audit transition. Sensitive direct writes without the required approved change return 403/409 through direct API calls. A multi-key invalid change commits zero values. Approved schedules execute once and every consumer reports the new version within 60 seconds. Rollback previews identify the exact audited target and clearly exclude reversal of already posted financial activity. Simultaneous regional change exercises retain both drafts and a complete actor/reviewer history.

## 54. Audit Log — `/admin/super/audit-log`

**Mission / what exists.** Audit Log provides the general administrative activity record, supporting incident reconstruction, supervision, and evidence collection. It already has action/type filters, 50-row paging, refresh, a last-loaded indicator, error display, and localized copy. The API supports an administrator filter beyond the visible basic controls and requires the global `view_audit_log` permission for delegated admins. The underlying log is more than an ordinary editable table: migrations implement hashes, predecessor links, immutable updates, and a read-only integrity verifier. A scheduled verification job records a critical incident for broken links. These mechanisms are existing strengths; enterprise recommendations must build on them and distinguish a chain-integrity check from complete event coverage or independent evidence custody.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/AuditLog.jsx:19` fetches page/filter results and `:90` renders filters. `server/routes/admin.js:260` grants list access; `server/controllers/superAdminController.js:2407` handles missing schema, caps page/limit, and orders by timestamp. `server/migrations/406_admin_audit_hash_chain.sql:49` serializes chain inserts through an advisory transaction lock; `:79` prevents updates. `server/migrations/407_admin_audit_hash_chain_verification.sql:15` scans ordered chain evidence. `server/jobs/adminAuditChainVerification.js:32` runs verification and `:44` creates an incident. `server/__tests__/auditServiceFailClosed.test.js` and `server/__tests__/ws5-audit-evidence.test.js` inspect adjacent audit guarantees; the inspected job explicitly calls external anchoring future work at `:15`.

**Findings.** **T54-F01 — P2 [code observation]: deep browsing is offset-based and lacks stable tie ordering.** The server caps page number at one million but computes offset as page times limit, then orders only by `created_at` (`superAdminController.js:2412`). A page-size cap does not bound database work at large offsets. Concurrent activity and equal timestamps can move evidence across pages. The route does not expose a date window, target key, correlation ID, or integrity checkpoint despite storing richer records.

**T54-F02 — P1 [design requirement]: sensitive audit access is globally delegated or unavailable.** Current permission middleware rejects regional grants, correctly preventing a regional grant from authorizing a global read (`server/middleware/adminAuth.js:151`). The route therefore cannot support regional auditors with least-privilege evidence access. A UI filter is not an authorization boundary. A region/tenant-aware projection must handle global setting events separately and avoid leaking personal before/after states across business or workforce boundaries.

**T54-F03 — P2 [verification gap]: chain throughput and evidence custody have not been demonstrated at target scale.** The advisory lock is an intentional consistency mechanism, while verification scans the chain. No load measurement or restored-data rehearsal was performed here. The daily job’s own scope note states that a complete recomputed tail requires external anchoring to detect. Successful links also do not prove every administrative writer emitted an event; transactional settings writes coexist with nonblocking audit paths elsewhere, for example user-creation legacy logging at `superAdminUserController.js:354`.

**Expansion scenario.** A financial incident spans support, settings, and a country launch across three time zones. The investigator needs the precise sequence of related requests and configuration changes, while local reviewers need only their business scope. Six million user records generate many service events, and 3,500 staff can create bursts of admin actions. Repeated deep paging and a full-chain scan must not stall the same database serving customers. Missing events must be visible as a coverage failure, not confused with valid chain links.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Add stable `(created_at,id)` cursors, bounded date filters, target-key and correlation search, saved case views, and explicit source/integrity status. Preserve existing chain verification and incident reporting; measure lock waits and verification duration before deciding on segmentation or incremental checkpoints. Publish reviewed chain heads to independently controlled append-only storage with auditable retrieval. Add a writer coverage catalog defining which actions fail closed, which have durable retry, and who owns unresolved gaps. Produce asynchronous case exports with manifest, count, hashes, filter scope, and last verified checkpoint. Present audit events as evidence, never as a one-click financial undo mechanism. Provide masked details by default and accessible expansion controls.

**Dependencies / owner.** Security Engineering owns audit guarantees and reviewer access; Data Platform owns query/index/retention design; SRE owns verification health and anchoring custody; Finance owns interpretation of financial activity.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** A concurrent-write pagination test visits every event in a fixed snapshot once, including identical timestamps. Regional reviewers cannot request global or foreign-tenant evidence by changing filters. At a synthetic 100-million-event volume, common bounded reads meet p95 under one second and chain writes meet a documented latency budget. A deliberate chain break creates one deduplicated incident; a recomputed tail disagrees with the independent anchor. Every privileged mutation category has a documented writer guarantee and evidence owner.

## 55. User Action Log — `/admin/super/audit-log/user-actions`

**Mission / what exists.** This tab exposes structured per-field changes to user accounts, complementing the general administrative log. It already supports actor, target, action, region, and date filters, paginated reads, CSV download, and before/after detail. The service bounds list requests to 500 rows and offset to one million; CSV uses formula neutralization and non-cacheable response headers. Creation writes structured evidence transactionally, and sensitive field redaction is part of the logging service. This existing system is the correct place to improve account-change traceability, rather than invent a competing history store or assert that account auditing does not exist.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/AuditLogUserActions.jsx:30` builds filter/offset requests; `:76` downloads the CSV. `server/routes/admin.js:616` grants both list/export to `view_audit_log`, with the strict limiter on export. `server/controllers/auditController.js:19` maps filters, `:48` escapes spreadsheet values, and `:57` exports. `server/services/auditService.js:180` assembles the query and `:203` clamps pagination. `server/migrations/116_admin_user_action_log.sql:26` provides action/time/region indexes; `server/migrations/124_admin_user_action_log_nullable_ids.sql:24` permits actor/target links to become null during deletion. `server/__tests__/auditExportSafety.test.js:22` checks inert CSV values and cache headers; it does not establish complete multi-page exports or deleted-identity visibility.

**Findings.** **T55-F01 — P1 [confirmed defect]: export silently represents only the first 500 matching records.** The controller fixes `limit:500, offset:0` and writes those rows (`auditController.js:66`). It discards the total returned by the service. The UI offers “export CSV” without a partial-result manifest. With multiple field changes per account, one onboarding batch can exceed that cap. Bounding a synchronous response is sensible, but a partial evidence file must be labeled as such or completed by a durable job.

**T55-F02 — P1 [confirmed defect]: retained events can disappear from the viewer when identity links are null.** Migration 124 intentionally permits null actor/target links so evidence survives deletion. The data query still uses inner joins to both user rows (`server/services/auditService.js:217`), whereas the count query counts log rows alone (`:225`). Null-linked events remain stored but cannot appear in results, and totals can exceed visible rows. This is a deterministic query/schema mismatch; it is not an assertion that historical data has already been erased.

**T55-F03 — P1 [design requirement]: read and export permissions need separate scope and disclosure decisions.** The existing route permits export with the same global viewing grant, even though structured old/new values can include contact or assurance information. Regional filtering is supplied by the caller; middleware intentionally only accepts unscoped grants. Safe regional access needs a server-derived record scope and handling for events whose region is null, not reliance on `region_id` in a query string. Preserving identity snapshots must be balanced with approved minimization/retention policies.

**Expansion scenario.** A six-month investigation reviews 700 employee account updates and 20,000 customer corrections. Some targets have closed accounts, and a former administrator no longer has an active user record. The analyst expects the displayed count, CSV manifest, and retained history to reconcile. A regional HR reviewer needs local employee changes, while Security needs cross-region provenance. The organization must retain explanatory context without automatically restoring access to deleted accounts or disclosing every personal field to HR.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Use left joins and a clear “deleted/unavailable actor” representation; store policy-approved immutable identity references so present-day email changes do not rewrite the meaning of historical evidence. Add request/correlation grouping, stable cursor pagination, explicit effective region/business scope, and authorized reveal of sensitive values. Extend the existing CSV implementation with an asynchronous export using the same validated filter and scope as the list, durable progress, replay protection, manifest counts, expiry, and audited download. Either require a dedicated export permission or document deliberate equivalence with sensitive viewing. Show partial results and unavailable notification evidence explicitly rather than treating booleans as delivery proof.

**Dependencies / owner.** Identity owns account-change contracts; Security owns audit retention and disclosure; Privacy Operations approves identity snapshots; Data Platform owns complete exports and consistent joins.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** Synthetic events with null actor, null target, and deleted identities remain visible and reconcile with totals. A 25,000-event filtered export returns all authorized rows exactly once or declares its incomplete state and resumable job ID. CSV formula/caching protections remain intact. Direct API tests prevent regional reviewers from widening scope or downloading unapproved sensitive fields. Replaying an export request returns one job, and cancellation/restart produces a reconcilable manifest without duplicate evidence.

## 56. Legal (all subpages) — `/admin/legal/{acceptances,audit-log,compliance-metrics,ccpa-requests}`

**Mission / what exists.** Legal has four genuinely routed administrative subpages: Acceptances, Legal Audit Log, Compliance Metrics, and CCPA Requests. They have localized copy, a shared subnavigation, filters, charts or metrics, paginated tables, detail panels, revocation controls, CSV actions, and request-status/notification controls. Separately, repository components exist for Documents, New/Edit Document, and Approval: intended paths are `/admin/legal/documents`, `/admin/legal/documents/new`, `/admin/legal/documents/:id/edit`, `/admin/legal/documents/:id/approve`, and `/admin/legal/approvals`. They are present in source but are not registered by the current admin manifest/app route declarations. Their backing document API also exists. This distinction avoids incorrectly calling all Legal functionality missing while still identifying incomplete publication workflow wiring.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/components/LegalSubNav.jsx:5` lists the four live tab paths; `client/src/admin/AdminApp.jsx:292` mounts them. `client/src/admin/pages/AdminLegalAcceptancesPage.jsx:183` reads acceptance results and `:222` exports; `client/src/admin/pages/AdminLegalAuditLogPage.jsx:115` reads audit results and `:147` exports; `AdminCCPARequestsPage.jsx:296` reads requests; `AdminComplianceMetricsPage.jsx:235` renders document-status counts. `client/src/pages/admin/AdminLegalDocumentsPage.jsx:28` loads documents; `AdminLegalDocumentEditorPage.jsx:59` loads a document and `:126` requests approval; `AdminLegalApprovalPage.jsx:31` creates fixture approvals. `server/routes/legalDocuments.js:13` defines process-local drafts, `:128` begins document administration, `:253` begins acceptance administration, and `:325` routes privacy requests. `server/controllers/legalDocumentsController.js:198`, `:307`, `:383`, and `:488` implement acceptance, metrics, audit, and revocation respectively. Migration `588_legal_audit_log_reconcile.sql:38` repairs writer/reader columns; `:65` drops incompatible historical constraints. `server/__tests__/legalAuditLogContract.test.js:94` covers that reconciliation. Those repaired column/taxonomy issues are not repeated here as current defects.

**Findings.** **T56-F01 — P1 [confirmed defect]: current acceptance, audit, and request count queries still bind the wrong parameter arrays.** Fresh source inspection found `params.slice(0, paramCount - 2)` at `legalDocumentsController.js:265`, `:449`, and `ccpaController.js:222`. After N filters, `paramCount=N+1` and pagination adds two more values. The count query requires N values, while the slice yields N−1 for filtered requests; without filters, it supplies the limit to a query with no placeholders. A read-only in-memory harness extracted the actual three functions and replaced only database access with placeholder-arity checking. All three failed for both unfiltered and one-filter cases: required/supplied counts 0/1 and 1/0. No real database or production request was used. This is newly verified current code evidence, not a copied historical SQL allegation. CI success and readiness do not cover it away.

**T56-F02 — P1 [confirmed defect]: list and UI response contracts disagree even after binding is corrected.** Controllers return `pagination.total` for acceptance and audit (`legalDocumentsController.js:295`, `:477`) and for requests (`ccpaController.js:262`), while the pages read `res.data.total` (`AdminLegalAcceptancesPage.jsx:196`, `client/src/admin/pages/AdminLegalAuditLogPage.jsx:131`, `AdminCCPARequestsPage.jsx:310`). Acceptance rows also return camelCase user/document fields (`legalDocumentsController.js:282`) while visible components reference fields such as `document_version` (`AdminLegalAcceptancesPage.jsx:465`). Normalize the complete schema through a tested adapter; do not fix only a count and assume all pagination/detail fields align.

**T56-F03 — P1 [confirmed defect]: export filters and completeness do not match the reviewed screen.** Acceptance and audit clients send active filters, but the corresponding handlers run fixed unfiltered queries with `LIMIT 5000` (`legalDocuments.js:264`, `:304`). A user can receive evidence outside the intended case and lose everything beyond the cap. Existing `export_data` authorization is valuable, but it does not make an unfiltered file the same evidence set. The shared CSV helper at `legalDocuments.js:85` quotes values without the formula-prefix neutralization already implemented by User Action Log. Extend that established export safety rather than treating all CSV implementations as equivalent.

**T56-F04 — P1 [confirmed defect]: document publication is not durable or connected to public content.** Document mutations change a `Map` at `legalDocuments.js:148`, `:196`, `:208`, and `:222`. Restart or another API process sees different drafts. Approval only sets a status; it does not retain a scheduled instant, enforce an independent reviewer, or publish a database-backed immutable version. Public terms load bundled locale content at `:26`, so marking a draft “live” does not update that public source. The approval component’s fixture list is a separate code observation reinforcing that this authoring workflow is unfinished, not evidence that all Legal pages are fixtures. Existing document/version migrations should be evaluated for durable integration before creating parallel stores.

**T56-F05 — P2 [confirmed defect]: metrics can misstate assurance and publication coverage.** The denominator counts active nondeleted users, while acceptance numerators count distinct users without matching active/deleted eligibility or `deleted_at` on acceptances (`legalDocumentsController.js:309`). Revoked records are soft-deleted at `:512`; numerator/list queries still omit that exclusion. Document-status metrics are literal zeros at `:359`. The dashboard therefore needs an “unavailable/unconnected” state rather than suggesting a measured zero, and rates need a consistent eligible population and applicable version/jurisdiction. This is an engineering metric defect, not a legal conclusion about whether a specific agreement remains enforceable.

**T56-F06 — P1 [code observation]: privacy-request completion needs stronger workflow evidence.** CCPA request updates can set completed and fulfillment metadata in an independent UPDATE (`ccpaController.js:306`, `:349`) followed by auditing/notification. There is no expected-version comparison in that path, and optional `dataProvided` is not a verified fulfillment manifest. Existing deadlines and notification actions should be preserved, but expanded into configurable jurisdiction-reviewed routing, responsible owner, identity assurance, retention/legal-hold review, fulfillment evidence, and delivery retry. No statutory deadline or erasure obligation is asserted by this audit.

**Expansion scenario.** A multilingual agreement update targets one service in two countries while several hundred access/deletion requests and a revoked acceptance are under review. Legal reviewers need authoritative documents, language-specific approvals, exact acceptance versions, clear eligibility denominators, and exports limited to a case. Privacy staff must hand work across time zones without two people marking different fulfillment outcomes. A restart during approval cannot discard a negotiated text or cause a later publish operation to apply another draft. A restored production-like database must preserve earlier versions and all evidence links.

**Recommended controls and UX.** First repair current count binding and define a single tested client/API schema for every routed subpage. Apply screen filters and authorization scope to exports, show explicit row limits, and use durable asynchronous jobs for complete results. Reuse inert CSV encoding and no-store headers. Wire document components only when their actual durable workflow is ready; migrate drafts into the existing document/version model, preserve immutable content/language hashes, record proposer/reviewer/version, and publish exactly that approved snapshot through a durable scheduled worker. Require server-side version conflicts, independent approval where organizational policy demands it, visible diffs, rollback-as-new-version, and public-content confirmation. Update acceptance recording to verify the version actually presented, rather than merely accepting a caller-supplied positive number (`legalDocumentsController.js:26`). Keep provenance for administrative revocations. Extend request handling with assignment, configurable reviewed clocks, escalation, evidence manifests, retryable delivery, and explicit cross-tenant restrictions. Financial-policy text, provider-specific terms, and jurisdiction applicability require human legal/provider review.

**Dependencies / owner.** Legal Operations owns approved wording, locale/jurisdiction applicability, publication policy, and evidence standards; Privacy Operations owns request fulfillment; Identity owns authenticated acceptance; Platform/Data Engineering owns durable versions, adapters, and jobs; Finance/provider owners review financial-policy dependencies.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** Each of the four routed pages returns correct totals, fields, and page continuation in real isolated PostgreSQL tests with zero, one, and multiple filters. Acceptance/audit exports match filtered authorized counts for 50,000 synthetic rows and neutralize formula values. Restarting or load balancing preserves drafts and reviewed content. Concurrent edits return 409; self-approval is rejected where mandated; scheduled publication makes the exact approved locale/version publicly retrievable. Revoked/ineligible records do not inflate an applicable-version metric. Privacy requests cannot reach completed without the required reviewed fulfillment evidence and retain one outcome under concurrent updates. Run the full authenticated matrix without skip plus a production-like restored-data rehearsal; successful fresh-schema migrations alone are insufficient acceptance.

## 57. Visitors — `/admin/super/visitors`

**Mission / what exists.** Visitors is a real first-party traffic analytics surface, with date range, country, path, device, bot inclusion, and submitted search filters. It shows paginated visit history plus totals, country distribution, popular pages, and daily trends. Country names use locale-aware display names. Recording hashes IPs, strips URL query strings, respects Do Not Track, classifies devices/bots, and tolerates analytics insert failure without blocking the visitor. An existing pruning function supplies retention. The administrative grant is enforced on both the route manifest and analytics API. These privacy and availability measures should be retained while improving analytical accuracy and operating cost.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/VisitorHistoryPage.jsx:24` establishes controls, `:39` localizes country names, and `:70` retrieves both list and summary for every parameter change. `server/routes/siteVisits.js:14` requires `view_visitor_analytics`. `server/controllers/siteVisitController.js:31` parses date ranges, `:72` performs list/count, and `:115` computes four summary queries. `server/services/siteVisitService.js:77` handles DNT and hashed-IP recording; `:114` deletes old rows. `server/migrations/613_site_visits.sql:25` provides time/country/path/user indexes. `server/__tests__/siteVisits.test.js:68` checks sanitized paths, `:92` DNT, `:106` pruning, and `:141` filtered pagination. These mocked/helper tests do not establish target-scale analytics latency or consent adequacy in every jurisdiction.

**Findings.** **T57-F01 — P2 [code observation]: pagination repeats aggregate work unnecessarily.** `VisitorHistoryPage.jsx:75` fetches summary whenever page changes, though it omits page/limit from the summary request. The backend repeats count, distinct-IP, country, top-path, and daily groupings across the same time range. At millions of users and multiple views per visit, this can dominate a relatively cheap table page. No measured outage or saturated query is claimed; query plans and load need evidence.

**T57-F02 — P2 [code observation]: input bounds permit expensive historical queries and unstable continuation.** `parseRange` substitutes defaults for invalid dates but does not limit a valid wide range or reject reversed dates. The list uses timestamp-only ordering and offset paging (`siteVisitController.js:103`), while wildcard path/search predicates can broaden the requested match. Long ranges and insertion during browsing can produce costly, moving result sets despite a bounded page size. A browser’s 30-second timeout is not a database budget or cancellation strategy.

**T57-F03 — P1 [design requirement]: aggregate analytics and identifiable visit investigation require separate access.** The list joins registered-user name/email (`siteVisitController.js:97`). The current grant is global; a selected country filter does not limit the operator’s actual authorization. Country analysts usually need aggregates, while trust/support staff need a purpose-bound individual lookup. Hashed-IP distinct counts are estimates of network identifiers, not six million distinct people: shared networks, address rotation, DNT, and bot heuristics affect interpretation.

**T57-F04 — P2 [verification gap]: ingestion loss and retention execution need observable evidence.** Recording catches and suppresses insert failures (`siteVisitService.js:107`), an appropriate availability choice that can hide lost analytics unless a metric records them. The pruning helper exists, but this audit did not execute or verify its runtime schedule/retention outcome. Its one-statement DELETE also needs a volume/lock-budget rehearsal; the existence of pruning is not proof of cheap retention at worldwide traffic levels.

**Expansion scenario.** Marketing compares a restaurant campaign in Haiti with a logistics launch in Canada while support examines one customer’s failed journey. A single office address can correspond to hundreds of people, and a traffic spike can create tens of millions of pageviews. Country analysts must not browse unrelated user identities, and next-page clicks should not rerun every aggregate. Regional day boundaries and translation differences must not create apparently contradictory daily charts.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Separate list and summary dependencies, cache/roll up summaries by validated filter snapshot, expose freshness/sample/loss metadata, and label unique-network estimates honestly. Reject or queue expensive date windows, use stable cursors and documented time zones, and limit wildcard search cost. Provide aggregate-only grants with country/business scope; require audited case-based permission for identifiable histories. Instrument received/recorded/dropped counts, pruning lag, query duration, and bot classification coverage. Rehearse bounded batch retention or time partitions with the existing retention policy. Show partial summary failure independently from a usable list, and provide accessible chart alternatives and localized range descriptions.

**Dependencies / owner.** Data/Analytics Engineering owns definitions and rollups; Privacy/Security own identifiable access and retention policy; SRE owns ingestion and pruning health; regional Marketing owns interpretations and campaign context.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** Twenty page changes issue one summary fetch for unchanged filters. A 100-million-view synthetic dataset meets p95 page latency below one second and summary freshness within five minutes. Reversed/oversized ranges return a clear validation or job response. Aggregate-only regional users cannot obtain names/emails through direct API requests. Injected recording failures increment loss metrics without blocking page navigation. Retention rehearsals stay within agreed lock/latency budgets, and charts have keyboard/screen-reader summaries in all supported locales.

## 58. Profile Visibility — `/admin/super/profile-visibility`

**Mission / what exists.** Profile Visibility is an extensive moderation control surface, not a missing page. It searches several profile types, respects owner listing/activity in effective visibility, supports pagination, public preview, active-work inspection, reasoned hide/restore, history, undo, saved views, export, bulk dry run, idempotency keys, two-person approvals, scheduled changes with time zones, and owner-notification status/retry. The server gates single-profile hides with active work and requires approval for bulk requests above ten items. Existing scheduled-action and notification workers should remain the execution paths. Enterprise work concerns consistency among these paths, atomic approval execution, complete evidence, and safe distributed operation.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/ProfileVisibilityPage.jsx:131` defines the workspace, `:224` searches, `:317` applies a change, and `:348` begins active-work review. `server/routes/admin.js:103` protects search/export/scheduling/bulk/approval/history operations with `manage_profile_visibility`. `server/controllers/profileVisibilityController.js:364` gates single hides; `:545` implements bulk; `:683` undo; `:770` scheduling; `:922` export. `server/services/profileVisibilityApprovals.js:253` reviews requests. Migrations `617_profile_visibility_scheduled_actions.sql` and `618_profile_visibility_approvals.sql:10` persist schedules and approvals. `server/__tests__/profileVisibilityWorkstreamB.test.js:68`, `:121`, and `:158` cover self-review, payload fingerprints, and consumption with mocked queries, not simultaneous PostgreSQL execution.

**Findings.** **T58-F01 — P1 [confirmed defect]: approval review is not one database transaction.** `profileVisibilityApprovals.js:257` uses `db.query('SELECT ... FOR UPDATE')`, followed by a separate unconditional UPDATE at `:279`, without acquiring a client/BEGIN across those calls. The lock ends with the standalone statement. Two reviewers can both observe pending and overwrite one another; approval-triggered execution can also happen twice. Existing self-review and fingerprint checks are real, but they do not remove this race.

**T58-F02 — P1 [confirmed defect]: small bulk hides bypass the single-profile active-work gate.** Single hides call `getActiveWork` and require approval when work exists (`profileVisibilityController.js:375`). Bulk execution requires approval only when item count exceeds ten (`:598`), then calls `applyVisibilityChange` directly (`:620`). That helper performs the UPDATE without an active-work gate (`:310`). Dry run displays active work, but callers can skip preview and submit one to ten items. The same target can therefore require approval through one endpoint and succeed without it through another.

**T58-F03 — P1 [confirmed defect]: retry storage follows effects, and audit before-state is synthesized.** Bulk checks for an existing key, executes rows, and only afterward inserts the replay response (`:588`, `:613`, `:658`). Concurrent same-key requests can both perform changes before converging on a response. `applyVisibilityChange` records `before.is_hidden_by_admin` as the inverse of the desired state (`:337`), not the loaded state. A no-op repeated hide can produce misleading history, which undo subsequently relies on. These are current source defects, not proof of a past incident.

**T58-F04 — P2 [code observation]: scale exports and delegation remain bounded global operations.** Export sends only the first 5,000 rows (`:948`) without a continuation manifest, while scheduled list returns 500 (`:861`). The API’s global grant protects scope, but there is no region/tenant-derived authorization on these routes. A broader workforce must not receive a global moderation grant merely to manage local profiles.

**Expansion scenario.** A regional incident requires hiding 800 compromised profiles, including restaurants with open orders, while another team restores wrongly flagged businesses. The operator needs previewed impact, independent approval, per-item status, and notification recovery. Duplicate request delivery or a second reviewer must not create duplicate execution or fabricated before-states. Undo must not reopen a business whose later independent restriction remains valid. Existing bulk, approvals, and schedules are valuable but need one consistent state machine.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Claim approval and idempotency keys before effects inside a transaction or durable job; enforce status/version transitions, payload binding, requester/reviewer scope, and expiry. Apply active-work rules to every hide path, including small batches, scheduled execution, and undo that re-hides. Capture actual locked before-state and retain the originating change ID. Use conditional undo against the exact version. Extend existing workers for larger async batches, cancellation, per-item retries, and notification reconciliation. Add scoped moderation grants, meaningful totals, complete asynchronous exports, owner escalation, and accessible previews/diffs with localized time-zone explanations.

**Dependencies / owner.** Trust & Safety owns risk and exception policy; Platform owns atomic workflow; Business Operations owns active-work handoff; SRE owns jobs/notification health; Security owns delegated scope.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** Fifty concurrent reviewers produce one final decision and one execution. Same-key retries cause no repeated transitions or notifications. One-item bulk hides of active-work profiles require the same approval as single hides. Repeated no-op changes do not invent an opposite before-state. An 800-profile job resumes after worker loss, and a 25,000-row export reconciles fully. Cross-region HTTP requests fail, while keyboard users can review impact, approve, schedule, and inspect failures in all supported languages.

## 59. One Truth — `/admin/super/one-truth`

**Mission / what exists.** One Truth consolidates platform statistics, scoped user exploration, potential duplicate pairs, and a detailed user view. Country and configured region filters exist, with explicit warnings for geography fields that cannot fully represent a boundary. User and suspect panels have visible pagination; the server supplies scope metadata. The page is read-only and super-admin-only, which avoids turning a duplicate heuristic into automatic destructive identity management. Existing cross-domain summaries and enrichment are useful for investigation. Enterprise expansion requires a scalable read model, reliable scope behavior, currency-aware metrics, and a formal adjudication handoff rather than an additional “truth” database competing with business records.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/OneTruthPage.jsx:463` builds scope, `:479` loads statistics, `:493` loads suspect pages, and `:515` loads users. `server/routes/oneTruth.js:6` requires super-admin throughout. `server/controllers/oneTruthController.js:98` resolves region definitions, `:254` emits partial-geography warnings, `:606` lists users, `:674` generates suspects, and `:729` computes statistics. `:693` selects all scoped candidate users; `:702` enriches and generates pairs; `:711` slices after computation. `:916` aggregates marketplace cents; `:966` aggregates payments. Region definitions come from existing `admin_regions` migrations `115_super_admin_regions.sql` / `119_super_admin_regions.sql`, and grant-scope regression tests at `server/__tests__/adminPermissionScope.test.js:23` protect adjacent delegated endpoints, not One Truth’s query load or domain-metric semantics.

**Findings.** **T59-F01 — P1 [confirmed defect]: suspect pagination does not bound processing.** Every suspect request loads all users in scope, enriches them, constructs duplicate pairs, then slices the result in application memory (`oneTruthController.js:693`). Statistics separately selects the full scoped user population to count pairs (`:983`). Even requesting twenty visible rows can therefore process millions of records. This is an unbounded source path, not a measured memory crash; algorithmic pair complexity depends on the actual grouping logic and is not asserted as universally quadratic.

**T59-F02 — P1 [confirmed defect]: an invalid requested region falls back to unscoped results.** `getRegionScope` returns a warning and null definition when a region is not found (`:106`). That is not an authorization bypass for this super-admin-only route, but it is a dangerous analytical scope widening. An operator expecting a regional incident can receive global records. Partial geography mappings also warrant a deliberate coverage label, rather than a subtle warning alongside apparently definitive totals.

**T59-F03 — P1 [code observation]: financial volumes are aggregated without currency grouping.** Marketplace `total_cents` and payment `amount` are summed for the selected scope (`:918`, `:969`) with no currency group or conversion provenance. A global or multicountry scope can therefore produce a number whose unit is not established by this query. This is a metric-design limitation; it does not prove ledger corruption or that the current data necessarily contains mixed currencies. Currency-safe display and test fixtures are required before management relies on the total.

**T59-F04 — P2 [verification gap]: freshness, adjudication, and representative query plans lack acceptance evidence.** The inspected route contract exposes filters/stats/users/suspects/detail, not case ownership, dismissal disposition, or adjudicated identity links. No six-million-user or restored-data rehearsal was executed.
