The CI matrix skip also leaves interactive evidence, localized warnings, and stale-response handling unproven despite existing UI implementation.

**Expansion scenario.** A fraud team reviews suspected duplicates after expansion into markets with shared surnames, recycled phone numbers, family devices, and multiple legitimate businesses per person. A regional definition is retired while an analyst has it selected. Headquarters also requests a global paid-volume figure. The workspace must neither silently widen the investigator’s scope nor imply that an unmatched heuristic proves fraud, and its read cost must remain bounded during customer traffic.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Generate bounded candidate sets using indexed identifiers and incremental background processing, store versioned candidate evidence, and paginate persisted cases. Reuse existing user/business records and incident/case tooling for assignment, triage, false-positive disposition, and reversible adjudication; never merge or move funds solely from a score. Reject missing regions or require explicit global confirmation. Display boundary coverage and per-section snapshot/freshness. Group money by currency and precision, or show a separately labeled reporting conversion with rate/source/as-of metadata. Add purpose-bound analyst delegation only after server-enforced scope exists; keep sensitive detail separate from aggregate access. Present accessible localized evidence explanations and review checklists.

**Dependencies / owner.** Identity/Trust owns duplicate adjudication; Data Platform owns incremental candidates and metrics; Finance validates currency semantics; Security owns investigator disclosure and scoped access.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** A twenty-case page reads a bounded candidate batch and meets p95 under one second against six million synthetic users. Invalid region IDs return 400/404 and never global results. Mixed-currency fixtures produce separate totals and no unlabeled summed money value. Every candidate records evidence version, reviewer, disposition, and case owner. False-positive decisions preserve legitimate accounts and financial isolation. A production-like restored dataset and unskipped authenticated browser matrix verify warning visibility, pagination, and localized detail.

## 60. Franchise HQ — `/admin/super/franchises`

**Mission / what exists.** Franchise HQ implements an application workspace with status/country/search filters, selected-application detail, notes, owner assignment, checklist progress, agreement terms, training/launch status, activity history, and store activation. The server has bounded paginated application listing, transactions for several writes, activity records, and a row lock preventing duplicate business linkage during activation. Agreement fields include currency-related initial fee, royalty/marketing basis points, term, exclusivity, document reference, and signer metadata. This is a functioning franchise workflow foundation; enterprise enhancements should strengthen its gates, ownership, and evidence rather than claim that agreements or launch tools are absent.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/FranchiseHQPage.jsx:197` derives metrics from loaded applications; `:205` loads the list; `:256` initializes agreement fields; `:302` saves agreements. `client/src/admin/AdminApp.jsx:431` keeps the page super-admin-only. `server/routes/franchise.js:28` grants application, notes, checklist, agreement, and activation APIs to admins with global `manage_operations`. `server/controllers/franchiseController.js:363` defaults listing to twenty rows and `:459` returns pagination metadata; `:770` upserts agreements; `:876` activates a store. `server/migrations/247_franchise_programs.sql:122`, `:143`, and `:176` store checklist, agreement, and activity records. No dedicated franchise lifecycle/concurrency test surfaced in the inspected test inventory; that is a verification gap, not proof that all related behavior is untested.

**Findings.** **T60-F01 — P1 [confirmed defect]: the page ignores existing pagination and labels page-local counts as workspace metrics.** `FranchiseHQPage.jsx:213` requests without page/limit and reads only `data`; its metrics count that returned array (`:198`). The server returns twenty rows by default plus total/pages. No page continuation is consumed in this loader. A 21st application can therefore be inaccessible through the default visible list, and approval/launched totals are incomplete even though backend paging exists.

**T60-F02 — P1 [code observation]: UI and API authority differ materially.** Client entry is super-admin-only, but all franchise management endpoints accept the broad `manage_operations` grant (`franchise.js:28`). Hiding the page does not restrict direct agreement or activation requests. Current global-only grant semantics protect regional isolation by refusal; they do not create dedicated franchise/legal/launch separation. Decide whether broad operations authority is intentional, then align discoverability and server capabilities instead of granting super-admin to every regional franchise team.

**T60-F03 — P1 [confirmed defect]: activation does not enforce represented readiness prerequisites.** The controller locks the application and checks existing linkage and owner (`franchiseController.js:887`), then creates a business. It does not check approved application status, agreement completion, training, or checklist readiness before insertion. Those statuses exist elsewhere in the workspace. A manual direct call can activate a store without the administrative gates its record suggests. This is an engineering workflow defect; no assertion is made about contractual or jurisdiction-specific eligibility.

**T60-F04 — P2 [confirmed defect]: agreement initial-fee editing loses fractional units.** `FranchiseHQPage.jsx:258` converts stored cents to a fixed-zero-decimal string; saving uses `parseMoney` into cents (`franchiseController.js:774`). A 123.45 amount can become 123 on an otherwise routine save. More generally, agreement upsert lacks an expected-version contract and stores signer metadata without demonstrated provider-signature verification. Metadata should be labeled as recorded information until evidence is reviewed.

**Expansion scenario.** Teams launch 60 franchises across currencies and time zones. Legal reviewers amend a fee while operations marks training complete and another manager activates the same store. The default twenty-row view hides the remaining pipeline. Fractional fees must survive round trips, and a signed-document reference must point to the exact reviewed terms. Territorial exclusivity and provider acceptance require explicit human evaluation, not inference from a checkbox.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Consume backend pagination, show authoritative aggregate counts separately, and add queue ownership, SLA aging, localized status labels, and accessible detail forms. Introduce dedicated scoped franchise read/manage/agreement/launch capabilities using existing grants and record geography/business ownership. Enforce an adopted readiness state machine server-side, with reviewed exceptions, versioned agreement evidence, expected-version edits, and idempotent activation result replay. Preserve currency precision and validate rates/amount bounds. Integrate existing checklist/activity records with provider signature evidence and launch approvals; add reconciliation for fee collection and refunds through existing payment/ledger systems, without creating a parallel financial ledger.

**Dependencies / owner.** Franchise Operations owns pipeline/SLA and checklist; Legal owns agreements and jurisdiction review; Finance/provider teams own collection/royalty semantics; Platform owns state transitions and scope.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** A 10,000-application fixture is fully navigable with accurate filtered totals. Direct activation without required reviewed prerequisites fails before business creation. Concurrent activations return one business ID. A 123.45 fee survives load/save unchanged, with correct currency precision. Stale agreement edits return 409. Regional grants cannot alter another territory or agreement; every approved exception and signature reference identifies the reviewed version and accountable owner.

## 61. Careers — `/admin/super/careers`

**Mission / what exists.** Careers is implemented through the shared `HiringPipelineBoard` in platform-employer mode. The underlying system supports public openings, international country/language/compensation fields, authenticated employer management, applications, staged pipeline, interviews, offers, applicant decisions, and business-team membership on hire. The board can post openings, select a role, move applicants, book interviews, create offers, and close an opening when hiring. Server validation includes catalog-driven stages and transitions; business hiring uses existing business edit permission checks. These are existing applicant-tracking capabilities, not an empty careers page. Enterprise work should connect the platform hiring lifecycle to controlled workforce provisioning and improve security, queue handling, and recovery.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/CareersAdmin.jsx:6` mounts platform hiring. `client/src/components/careers/HiringPipelineBoard.jsx:43` stores openings/applications; `:131` moves stage; `:143` prompts for interview time; `:172` groups active stages. `server/routes/careers.js:17` exposes employer routes using ordinary authentication. `server/controllers/careerController.js:35` defines platform admins, `:109` authorizes openings, `:317` lists employer openings, `:490` lists applications, `:535` changes stage, and `:697` processes offer decisions. `server/migrations/531_career_hiring.sql:15` defines openings, `:71` applications, and `:138` offers. `server/__tests__/careerHiring.test.js:172` covers platform posting, `:201` rejects helper posting, and `:208` checks business-team conversion with mocked database calls.

**Findings.** **T61-F01 — P1 [confirmed defect]: platform hiring API access bypasses the admin control-plane gate.** The page requires super-admin, but employer routes use `authenticate` only and platform authorization accepts any `admin` or `super_admin` role (`careerController.js:35`, `:111`). No specific hiring grant or `requireAdmin` two-factor enforcement appears in this route chain. Ordinary authentication rejects provisional MFA tokens (`server/middleware/auth.js:65`), checks active users (`:134`), and checks applicable identity assurance (`:162`), but does not perform the configured-admin-2FA enrollment check in `server/middleware/adminAuth.js:29`. It is not a public-access defect: authentication still exists and business ownership checks remain. It is a direct-API least-privilege/assurance mismatch that matters for applicant personal data and salary offers.

**T61-F02 — P1 [code observation]: employer lists and nested application histories are unbounded.** Platform opening queries have no LIMIT (`careerController.js:338`), and each selected opening returns every matching application plus JSON aggregates of events/interviews/offers (`:502`). The board groups the entire returned array. A public cap of 100 openings does not solve employer-side access to a popular opening with tens of thousands of applications. Query/index coverage and response-size targets need measurement.

**T61-F03 — P1 [confirmed defect]: stage and accepted-offer effects are not transactional workflow transitions.** Stage validation reads an application, then unconditionally updates by ID (`:537`, `:547`) before independently recording an event and possibly adding membership/closing the opening. Concurrent edits can pass the same old-state validation. Offer decision conditionally claims pending, which is good, but later application/event/member writes occur separately (`:720`). A failure after acceptance leaves an accepted offer whose downstream hire cannot be retried through the pending-only path without explicit repair tooling.

**T61-F04 — P2 [design requirement]: global recruiting needs ownership and reliable scheduling/provisioning handoff.** A prompt for interview time is not an enterprise calendar workflow, and platform hires do not use the business-team conversion branch. The reviewed code does not demonstrate a controlled HR provisioning handoff, interview assignment, regional recruiter scope, or provider invitation delivery. These are expansion requirements, not a claim that applicant offers or interview records do not exist.

**Expansion scenario.** An international logistics opening attracts 40,000 applicants. Recruiters in three countries handle local queues, interviewing candidates across daylight-saving boundaries. Two managers move the same applicant toward rejection and offer, while an offer acceptance succeeds but membership/provisioning fails. The system needs an authoritative outcome, a recoverable handoff, and region-specific disclosure without turning every recruiter into a super-admin or exposing all compensation data.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Route platform hiring through existing admin assurance plus explicit scoped hiring/read/offer permissions; retain business ownership checks for business employers. Page openings/applicants, lazy-load histories, offer server-side stage/search/age filters, and use bounded asynchronous bulk actions with per-applicant results. Make stage/offer decisions conditional on version/status with transactional event recording and durable outbox tasks for notifications/provisioning. Add hiring manager/recruiter ownership, aging/SLA, interview time-zone confirmation, calendar-provider status, accommodations workflow, and role-approved employee invitation handoff. Review compensation, work authorization, applicant retention, and local hiring requirements with HR/legal; do not infer legal suitability from catalog fields.

**Dependencies / owner.** HR Talent Acquisition owns recruiting policy and candidate communications; Identity owns workforce handoff; Security owns scope/assurance; Platform owns transaction/outbox repair; calendar/email owners own provider delivery.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** An authenticated admin without hiring capability or required assurance receives 403 on direct platform endpoints. Cross-region recruiters cannot read foreign applicants/offers. A 40,000-applicant opening returns bounded pages below one-second p95 and histories only on demand. Concurrent decisions yield one valid stage/event sequence. Failure after offer acceptance resumes exactly once without duplicate membership or notifications. Time-zone/DST scheduling and keyboard-only applicant review pass in all supported UI languages, with provider failure visibly actionable.

## 62. Country Packs — `/admin/super/country-packs`

**Mission / what exists.** Country Packs is the implemented country registry and availability workspace. Operators can see active, coming-soon, and disabled packs, currencies, locales, persona counts, payment methods, expandable configuration, and status actions with confirmation. The public API supplies country selection and country configuration; admin reads and writes require super-admin. PostgreSQL stores the registry and JSON configuration, with status constraints and updated-at maintenance. The service caches registry/configuration for sixty seconds and invalidates its own cache after updates. This is a useful existing architecture for market configuration. Enterprise readiness concerns launch assurance, validation, propagation, and emergency withdrawal, not creating an additional registry or asserting that country support is absent.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/CountryPacks.jsx:29` loads packs, `:45` confirms and changes status, and `:145` displays currency/locales/payment methods. `server/routes/countryConfig.js:13` exposes public reads and `:15` protects administration. `server/controllers/countryPackController.js:68` patches via the service and logs the actor. `server/services/countryPackService.js:14` defines the local cache, `:87` reads a pack, `:122` serves fallback on errors, and `:160` accepts updates. `server/migrations/304_country_packs.sql:10` defines schema and `:26` maintains updated timestamps; `366_country_pack_launch_payment_methods.sql` records later payment-method alignment. The inspected test inventory did not establish a dedicated country activation/provider/replica-consistency suite. Fresh migration success is credited globally, but no production-like restore or provider acceptance was demonstrated.

**Findings.** **T62-F01 — P1 [code observation]: activation is a status update without an enforced readiness package.** The UI confirms a status change and sends only `{status}` (`CountryPacks.jsx:51`). The service validates the status enum but does not require approved jurisdiction review, provider capability, localized required documents, support ownership, or reconciliation readiness before making it active. This is an expansion governance gap, not a claim that every current active country is unapproved or that its providers are down.

**T62-F02 — P1 [confirmed defect]: arbitrary object configuration can override canonical public metadata.** Updates only require `config` to be an object (`countryPackService.js:188`), while public response construction spreads `...row.config` after canonical `code`, currency, locale, and updated time (`:111`). A supplied config containing these keys can replace the registry identity shown to clients. No hostile write is asserted; admin-only scope remains. Schema validation and reserved-key protection must also catch unknown payment methods, unsupported locales, and incompatible currency precision.

**T62-F03 — P1 [code observation]: failure and replication semantics can diverge from the intended registry state.** Any database-read error for US can return a built-in active fallback (`:122`), potentially hiding a deliberate disable when the database becomes unavailable. Cache invalidation is process-local (`:211`), leaving other API processes with prior status for up to the TTL. This does not establish that financial mutations trust country configuration alone; backend payment enforcement must be tested independently. It establishes that picker/config state is not an instantaneous globally consistent emergency control.

**T62-F04 — P2 [design requirement]: edits need versioned review and an operating owner.** The UPDATE has no expected version or durable before/after administrative audit in this path (`:204`); controller logging records code/status/actor only. Headquarters ownership, country launch delegate, on-call escalation, and service withdrawal procedures are not represented in this tab. A country filter elsewhere cannot substitute for scoped market-management authority.

**Expansion scenario.** Two launch managers prepare a new market while a payment provider withdraws one local method and an incident commander disables registrations. A second API replica still serves active state, and a transient registry error triggers fallback. Existing customers still need access to receipts, support, and lawful/approved recovery processes, while new transactions may need to stop. A single active/disabled switch cannot silently encode all those distinct customer outcomes.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Extend the existing registry with typed configuration, immutable approved versions, reserved-key rejection, expected-version writes, before/after auditing, rollout timestamps, and separate browse/onboard/transact/support capability states. Add a launch checklist linking provider test evidence, finance reconciliation, human jurisdiction review, translated documents, staffed SLA, and accountable owner. Publish cache invalidations or enforce version checks at sensitive consumer boundaries. Make fallback explicitly degraded and prevent it from authorizing launches or overriding emergency restrictions. Show effective version and replica propagation, dependency failures, rollback target, and localized accessible status actions. Provider approval and legal suitability remain human prerequisites distinct from engineering configuration.

**Dependencies / owner.** International Operations owns country launch/withdrawal; Platform owns registry validation/distribution; Finance/provider teams own payment readiness and recovery; Legal reviews jurisdiction applicability; SRE owns rollout health.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** Reserved keys and unknown capabilities return 400. A stale edit returns 409 with no mutation. Activation without required reviewed evidence fails server-side. A synthetic three-replica test observes an emergency restriction within ten seconds, including database failure, while preserving support/receipt access. UI capability status matches direct API enforcement for each country/payment method. Every active market has a named owner, escalation SLA, versioned evidence, and rollback exercise; no mixed-currency value is inferred from a locale setting.

## 63. Corporate Setup — `/admin/super/corporate/departments`

**Mission / what exists.** Corporate Setup manages departments, titles, default permissions, and staffing context, linking to existing assignments and org-chart functionality. The page includes department/title editors, permission selection, role/workspace playbooks, country-specific staffing views, and assignment counts. The corporate service already resolves department defaults plus assignment overrides, supports country-filtered assignments, and prevents some delegated assignment/override escalation. Department/title/default writes are restricted to super-admin inside the controller, while routes also check `manage_admin_accounts`. Existing country/region fields and assignment lifecycle are real. Enterprise requirements should strengthen these shared workforce capabilities rather than duplicate the corporate hierarchy in HR or regional tools.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/CorporateDepartments.jsx:38` edits departments; `:181` edits titles; `:369` saves allowed defaults; `:518` loads catalog/assignments; `:539` derives staffing counts. `server/routes/corporateRoles.js:21` protects catalog/read paths and `:25` department mutations. `server/controllers/corporateRolesController.js:115` restricts department edits; `:160` replaces defaults; `:332` lists assignments; `:388` creates them; `:519` updates under lock; `:700` guards override escalation. `server/services/corporateRolesService.js:62` resolves permissions, `:105` filters country, and `:155` handles overrides. `server/migrations/246_corporate_hierarchy.sql:474` persists geographic/reporting/lifecycle assignments and `:497` adds active-assignment indexes. `server/__tests__/adminPermissionScope.test.js:23` proves the separate admin grant boundary, not comprehensive corporate scope or workforce lifecycle behavior.

**Findings.** **T63-F01 — P1 [confirmed defect]: staffing counts are derived from a truncated assignment list.** The page loads active assignments without requesting an aggregate (`CorporateDepartments.jsx:524`) and counts that array by title/department/country (`:546`). The controller caps results at 500 without paging continuation (`corporateRolesController.js:355`). With 3,500 employees and potentially multiple assignments each, displayed staffing can materially undercount and omit countries. The underlying data is not necessarily lost; the workspace’s representation is incomplete.

**T63-F02 — P1 [code observation]: effective corporate permissions are not fully bound to target geography/lifecycle.** The resolver accepts country but no target region, unions active assignments, and does not check department/title activity or start/end effective times in the inspected assignment predicate (`corporateRolesService.js:107`). `requireCorporatePermission.js:53` derives country from caller query/header. These observations are not proof of a bypass in every downstream route; each consumer needs target-record authorization. The stored region field alone does not enforce regional least privilege, and turning a department inactive cannot be assumed to revoke access immediately.

**T63-F03 — P1 [confirmed defect]: unknown default permission codes are silently ignored while success echoes the submitted set.** `setDepartmentDefaults` maps known codes, deletes existing defaults, skips unknown codes (`corporateRolesController.js:193`), then returns the incoming permissions (`:204`). An operator can believe a permission was applied when it was not, or unintentionally replace a complete set with a malformed partial one. Full-set replacement also lacks expected-version comparison and dedicated before/after audit in this path, affecting every employee sharing those defaults.

**T63-F04 — P2 [verification gap]: workforce changes need impact simulation and distributed revocation evidence.** Permission and catalog caches are process-local, with invalidation at mutation time and TTL-based reads. No 3,500-worker access recertification, multi-replica revocation, restored-data hierarchy, or HR lifecycle rehearsal was performed. Existing seniority protections should be retained, but they do not by themselves document conflict-of-duty rules or country-aware manager delegation.

**Expansion scenario.** A global compliance department reorganizes 600 staff, retiring two titles and changing a default permission. Regional leads must review local assignments while Security removes access for departed employees. A 500-row sample makes some countries appear understaffed, and an obsolete permission code is silently skipped. A future-dated assignment should not open production access today. The process needs before/after impact and a reversible organizational plan without reviving terminated accounts.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Compute authoritative server-side counts distinct from assignment-row counts; paginate assignment drill-down and handle multiple roles explicitly. Resolve permissions against the target’s country/region/tenant, effective interval, active title/department, and approved conflict-of-duty rules. Validate every permission code before replacing any defaults. Version changes, lock the relevant department, capture audited before/after, preview affected users and resulting permissions, and route high-impact updates through existing approval concepts. Add HR joiner/mover/leaver batch jobs, dry run, named owner, recertification due dates, replica revocation acknowledgement, and accessible permission explanations. Keep organizational labels localizable without changing stable authorization codes.

**Dependencies / owner.** HR Operations owns workforce truth and reporting lines; Security/IAM owns effective privileges and recertification; Corporate Operations owns catalog/playbooks; Platform owns resolver, versioning, counts, and jobs.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** All 3,500 synthetic employees and multiple assignments reconcile by department/title/country. Unknown permission codes reject the entire update. Stale full-set edits return 409. Inactive departments/titles, future/ended assignments, and foreign target regions produce the adopted denied outcome through actual consumer APIs. Revocation reaches all replicas within ten seconds. A reorganization dry run lists every affected person and permission delta, and reviewed execution produces a complete auditable result manifest.

## 64. Mode Policies — `/admin/super/mode-policies`

**Mission / what exists.** Mode Policies already edits whether a mode requires platform approval, whether application requests auto-approve, which action codes are gated, and the application form schema. The UI displays updated time, dirty/save state, JSON parsing errors, accessible pressed-state toggles, and confirmation before turning approval off. Both admin policy list/update routes require super-admin. PostgreSQL persists policies with updated-at triggers; the service caches them for sixty seconds. This system deliberately separates choosing an account mode from performing gated activities. Consultant paid-booking checks are implemented in two controllers. Readiness work should align configurable promises with actual enforcement and manage changes safely, not duplicate the mode switch or approval service.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/ModePolicies.jsx:22` defines accessible toggles, `:42` stages editor values, `:60` parses/saves JSON, and `:86` confirms gate removal. `server/routes/modePlatformApprovals.js:27` protects policy APIs. `server/controllers/modePlatformApprovalController.js:119` maps updates. `server/services/modePlatformApprovalService.js:66` persists arbitrary provided fields, `:128` evaluates a gate, and `:153` validates submitted applications. `server/controllers/consultantNetworkController.js:331` checks paid referrals; `consultantSchedulingController.js:261` checks the consultant booking target. `server/migrations/272_mode_platform_approvals.sql:19` defines policy state and `:51` seeds known modes. `server/__tests__/adminPaginationContracts.test.js:13` references the adjacent approval list; it does not establish policy downgrade/schema/enforcement acceptance tests.

**Findings.** **T64-F01 — P1 [code observation]: editable action codes are not an enforcement inventory.** The service accepts arbitrary action names, and the policy editor offers text input. Repository call-site inspection found active gate calls for consultant `accept_paying_booking`, while routes such as tool listing creation (`server/routes/toolRentals.js:413`) and job assignment (`server/routes/jobs.js:48`) do not install the generic gate in their route chains. No claim is made that all those activities should currently be gated or lack other restrictions. The concrete gap is that configuring a new helper/tool action cannot itself insert an authorization check into its mutation path.

**T64-F02 — P1 [confirmed defect]: policy changes lack semantic validation and stale-edit rejection.** `updatePolicy` accepts schema JSON/action arrays directly and unconditionally updates by mode (`modePlatformApprovalService.js:80`, `:91`). A syntactically valid schema such as `{fields:42}` is accepted; submission validation treats a non-array `fields` as empty (`:155`). Unknown field types/action codes and incompatible auto-approval combinations are not validated here. Two editors can overwrite each other, while enabling auto-approve bypasses independent review through request submission without the gate-off confirmation specifically shown by the UI.

**T64-F03 — P1 [code observation]: assurance decisions can survive missing policy or stale policy caches.** `canPerformAction` allows when no policy row exists (`:130`) and approves based on an existing user/mode status without binding it to a policy version (`:135`). Cache invalidation affects only the writing process. The generic middleware also catches service errors and calls `next()` (`server/middleware/requirePlatformApproval.js:56`), but no route installation of that middleware was found in the inspected route inventory; this is dormant-code risk, not evidence that active consultant callers fail open on database exceptions. Error policy must be assessed per actual consumer.

**T64-F04 — P2 [design requirement]: country applicability, review ownership, and policy evidence need explicit modeling.** Current policies are global per mode and hold updated-by/time but no immutable reviewed revision, applicability scope, change reason, reviewer, or adoption date. This may suffice for today’s narrow consultant gate. Worldwide approval policy requires a documented connection to country packs, provider readiness, staff review capacity, and human jurisdiction review.

**Expansion scenario.** A country launch needs additional consultant evidence, while another market pauses one paid activity because a provider capability is unavailable. Headquarters edits schema/action text and assumes all user actions now enforce it. Existing approved users retain access, and another replica still holds the older policy. Operations must know which endpoints enforce the published revision and which applicants need re-review, without globally preventing harmless mode browsing.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Build an action registry from actual checked mutation paths and let the editor select only supported actions; show endpoint coverage and explicit unsupported configuration. Validate schema structure/types/options/bounds and locale labels before publication. Add expected-version updates, audited immutable revisions, change reasons, reviewed downgrades/auto-approval, staged effective time, impact preview, and policy-version binding for approvals. Define safe availability/assurance behavior per action; protected financial/provider actions should not silently become permitted due to missing configuration. Propagate invalidations and expose effective versions. Reuse country packs for applicability and existing approval jobs for re-review migration, with precise explanations and accessible localized forms.

**Dependencies / owner.** Trust/Compliance owns admission policy and review SLA; Platform owns action registry and atomic revisions; Finance/provider teams approve financial-capability dependencies; Legal reviews jurisdiction requirements; SRE owns propagation evidence.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** Unsupported actions/schema types return 400 without mutation. Stale edits return 409. Every published gated action has a direct API denial test for unapproved users and explicit reviewed admin exceptions. Downgrades and auto-approval changes require the adopted independent approval. Three replicas converge on a revision within ten seconds. A new evidence revision produces a measurable re-review queue and never silently treats old evidence as new-provider approval.

## 65. Mode Approvals — `/admin/super/mode-approvals`

**Mission / what exists.** Mode Approvals is the implemented super-admin review queue for submitted mode applications. It shows applicant name/email, mode, submission time, application fields, approve confirmation, and rejection reason entry. The API provides bounded pending-list parameters, approve/reject/revoke actions, applicant status endpoints, schema validation, and automatic approval when the policy permits it. PostgreSQL has one row per user/mode plus status/request-time indexes. Approval affects specific gated activities rather than granting the entire mode switch. The enterprise requirement is a governable case workflow with durable decisions, delegation, concurrency control, and applicant/provider evidence, extending these existing mechanisms.

**Evidence.** `client/src/admin/pages/super/ModeApprovals.jsx:19` renders a case, `:26` approves, `:36` requires a UI rejection reason, `:119` renders submitted fields, and `:148` loads pending applications using only a mode filter. `server/routes/modePlatformApprovals.js:31` requires super-admin and exposes revocation. `server/controllers/modePlatformApprovalController.js:139` clamps list limit/offset; `:154` maps decisions. `server/services/modePlatformApprovalService.js:195` upserts applications, `:243` decides, and `:278` lists requested records. `server/migrations/272_mode_platform_approvals.sql:76` persists state, `:93` enforces user/mode uniqueness, and `:96` indexes status/time. `server/__tests__/adminPaginationContracts.test.js:19` checks offset-clamping source text, not a complete queue or simultaneous reviewer test.

**Findings.** **T65-F01 — P1 [confirmed defect]: the queue cannot browse beyond the first API page.** The UI sends no limit/offset and reads only `approvals` (`ModeApprovals.jsx:149`). The controller defaults to 100 results (`modePlatformApprovalController.js:144`), and the response carries no total/continuation. The database indexes and server offset parameter exist, but neither makes later cases discoverable from this page. Processing the oldest cases one at a time may eventually expose newer ones; that is not a complete operational queue with accurate backlog visibility.

**T65-F02 — P1 [confirmed defect]: decisions overwrite current state without transition/version validation.** `decideApproval` updates any row by ID (`modePlatformApprovalService.js:250`) and does not require requested status for approve/reject or approved status for revoke. Two super-admins can overwrite decisions; a stale approval can restore an already revoked case. The API also permits rejection without a reason (`:264`), even though the UI requires one. Existing super-admin authorization prevents ordinary-user review but does not provide safe concurrent decision semantics.

**T65-F03 — P1 [confirmed defect]: resubmission destroys prior decision provenance.** The user request upsert overwrites status, application data, request/review timestamps, reviewer, notes, and rejection reason (`:218`) without restricting the prior state. Even an approved user can resubmit and become requested under a non-auto policy; a revoked user can enter automatic approval if policy now permits it. The single current row and logger messages do not preserve immutable application/decision versions. This is a current state-machine defect; it does not assert that any actual revoked user regained a paid activity.

**T65-F04 — P2 [design requirement]: a worldwide review service needs scoped ownership and renewal.** Every reviewer needs global super-admin; the screen has no case assignee, country/workspace jurisdiction, SLA age buckets, queue claim, delegation expiry, renewal deadline, or visible revocation/history controls despite a revoke API. Applicant-data rendering exposes arbitrary submitted fields and converts false/zero to an empty marker through `String(val || '—')` at `ModeApprovals.jsx:126`. Typed evidence and localized field labels need deliberate display rather than assuming every JSON value is an ordinary string.

**Expansion scenario.** A launch produces 12,000 consultant applications across five countries. Regional reviewers split evidence checks by language and jurisdiction, while headquarters handles exceptions. A reviewer approves a stale card after another reviewer revokes it for new evidence. The applicant resubmits corrected documentation, and an email-provider failure means they never learn the outcome. The queue needs a traceable sequence of submission, review, revocation, renewal, and delivery, not an overwritten row or a headquarters bottleneck.

**Recommended controls and UX.** Preserve the existing current-status lookup but add immutable submission/decision events tied to policy/evidence version. Require conditional transitions, expected version, reviewer assignment/claim, bounded nonblank reasons, and idempotent decision replay. Implement dedicated region/mode reviewer grants enforced from the applicant/case record; reserve global policy and emergency authority for appropriate roles. Page/search/sort queues with authoritative counts, SLA buckets, assignee/language filters, and async low-risk batch decisions under a reviewed policy. Add renewal/revocation views, evidence links, applicant-visible outcome, durable notification outbox/retry, and conflict recovery preserving drafts. Display typed false/zero values accurately. Human provider/jurisdiction approval remains distinct from the software status.

**Dependencies / owner.** Trust/Compliance Review owns case policy, staffing, and SLA; Security owns scoped reviewer authority; Platform owns transitions/events/idempotency; Identity owns applicant lifecycle; notification/provider teams own delivery evidence.

**Measurable acceptance criteria.** All 12,000 synthetic cases are reachable with accurate backlog and oldest-case age. A stale approve cannot overwrite revoke and returns 409. Reasonless rejection fails server-side. Resubmission creates a new version while retaining every prior reviewer/outcome, and revoked cases cannot regain access outside the adopted policy. Regional direct API tests deny foreign-case decisions. Worker/notification failure resumes without duplicate decisions; 95% of ordinary cases meet the adopted 24-hour SLA. Zero/false fields, conflict messages, queue navigation, and decision dialogs pass accessible five-language review in an unskipped browser matrix.

# Management capabilities required for the target company

These five reviews describe **missing or fragmented enterprise capabilities**, not five claims that OxSteed has no related code. Existing support staffing, Org Chart, Corporate Titles, Customer/Business 360, delivery dispatch/tracking, incident management, financial controls, legal/privacy, and credit sub-workspaces are credited below. Recommended route names are proposals, not live destinations. Build integrations and coherent workspaces over authoritative services; do not recreate payroll, accounting, payment ledgers, or legal systems without an explicit business reason.

## Management page 1. Workforce Identity, Delegation, and Access Lifecycle

**Proposed workspace:** `/admin/workforce-access`. **Accountable owners:** Chief People Officer and Security/IAM, with regional employment administrators. **Scope:** the controlled lifecycle of 3,500 employees and contractors, not a replacement for the customer Users tab.

### Existing foundation and precise gap

Admin Accounts, Permissions, Internal Access, Org Chart, Corporate Titles, Corporate Setup, support-agent onboarding, shifts, agents, and team hierarchy exist. The route inventory confirms those surfaces in `client/src/admin/adminRouteManifest.js:50-51,74-87,132-136,145`. Admin authentication checks active state and MFA, and permission middleware intentionally constrains existing delegated actions to global grants (`server/middleware/adminAuth.js:43-71,126-177`). Those controls are useful. The inspected admin authentication, permission, and account surfaces did not establish a complete enterprise SSO/SCIM employment lifecycle, synchronized HR status, regional object-level administration, or automatic cross-provider deprovisioning. This is a required capability to validate/build, not an assertion that no identity integration exists anywhere in the repository.

An organizational title is not an authorization policy. A person can simultaneously supervise a region, work a temporary support shift, and have narrowly approved financial-review rights. Customer identity, corporate identity, outsourced agent identity, driver identity, and service-account identity must be distinguishable. Staff moving teams or leaving the company must not retain rights merely because a browser has a cached menu or a provider account is still connected. E05 describes the global-grant limitation; solving it is a prerequisite for safe workforce delegation.

### Required workflows and data

**M01-F01, P1 [design requirement]: joiner/mover/leaver automation.** Integrate with the chosen HRIS and identity provider. Keep an employment identifier, employer/legal entity, region, manager, team, employment state, effective dates, device/session inventory, and approved entitlement bundles. A hire should enter a staged identity with no production rights until training and required reviews are complete. A team transfer recalculates access; a departure disables authentication and revokes operational tokens and active sessions without deleting business audit evidence. Provide rehire and mistaken-termination recovery with independent approval.

**M01-F02, P1 [design requirement]: least privilege and separation of duties.** Define read, investigate, initiate, approve, export, and override capabilities separately, with object/region/entity scope and monetary limits. Finance reviewers should not be able to both create and approve their own payout; support supervisors should not gain credit reserve policy rights. Emergency access requires a reason, incident reference, expiry, and independent after-action review. Self-grants, circular approvals, expired delegations, and manager changes must have explicit rules. Backend authorization must run again when a queued action executes, not only when its draft was created.

**M01-F03, P1 [design requirement]: access inventory and attestation.** Show source-of-truth employment state versus application rights, last use, upcoming expiry, dormant privileged accounts, active sessions, and provider access. Schedule manager and security reviews with evidence of removal or accepted exceptions. Keep service identities separate, with owners and rotation/expiry policy. Federation outages need an independently secured recovery path, not a permanent shared super-admin password.

### Operator design and expansion scenario

Use task-based access packages and scope chips, not a flat list of dozens of cryptic grants. An employee drawer should show current/effective/pending rights, why each exists, who approved it, when it expires, and a before/after preview. The account lifecycle queue should expose blocked onboarding, overdue approvals, failed provider revocation, and temporary duties ending during a shift. Bulk onboarding must preview eligibility and record per-person outcomes. Notifications must contain safe summaries rather than sensitive personnel files.

Consider 200 seasonal staff hired in three countries, ten supervisors changing regions, and an outsourced support vendor losing its contract on the same day. The system must onboard only approved duties, prevent managers from granting global privileges to solve a queue problem, and revoke the vendor across HTTP, sockets, exports, and connected tools. A removed operator cannot approve a previously opened financial dialog. A country director can delegate coverage for a holiday without exposing another country's conversations. Payroll and employment decisions remain in the approved HR system; OxSteed records the operational entitlement result and its evidence.

### Acceptance and boundaries

Test all lifecycle actions for active sessions and delayed jobs. Proposed revocation objective: application HTTP and websocket rights cease within 60 seconds; external provider revocation failures remain in an owned exception queue until resolved. Load 3,500 employee identities and realistic entitlement histories. A region A operator must be denied region B list/detail/export/socket/actions. A maker, their temporary delegate, and a second account controlled by the same identity must not bypass declared conflict rules. Complete a quarterly access review in the tool with no spreadsheet-only exceptions. Identity outages, duplicate HR events, rehires, missing managers, and unknown region mappings must fail safely. These are acceptance gates, not current passing results.

## Management page 2. Treasury, Entity Accounting, Tax, and Financial Close

**Proposed workspace:** `/admin/finance-close`. **Accountable owner:** CFO/Controller. **Supporting owners:** payments engineering, treasury, regional tax/accounting, risk, and external accounting-system administrators.

### Existing foundation and precise gap

Money, Payments, Orders, Revenue, Financials, Payouts, reconciliation exceptions, fees/pricing, restaurant wallets, and the OxSteed Credit ledger/settlement/reserve/compliance/reconciliation pages exist. The manifest records the related financial routes at `client/src/admin/adminRouteManifest.js:42-43,108-130`. The tab reviews examine their actual totals, reporting bases, currencies, history, and recovery controls. This management workspace must connect them to entity books and close procedures rather than introduce another transaction ledger or call every gross movement revenue.

The enterprise gap is a finance-owned control process proving that balances, obligations, provider statements, bank cash, tax documents, and reports agree at a defined cutoff. Existing operational balance screens do not by themselves establish a multi-entity financial close. Worldwide participation also does not mean every credit or payout product is authorized everywhere; market approvals belong in Management page 5.

### Required workflows and controls

**M02-F01, P1 [design requirement]: entity and currency reporting contract.** Every applicable financial movement needs its authoritative legal entity, native currency, product/channel, provider reference, posting/effective time, accounting treatment, and reversible linkage. Keep the original units and precision. A reporting-currency conversion must use an identified FX snapshot and rounding policy; it cannot sum CAD, USD, and JPY cents as one USD figure. Document whether Money, Revenue, Financials, and provider reports reflect recognized income, collected cash, customer liability, GMV, pipeline, or net margin. Reconcile differences instead of forcing all dashboards to show the same number.

**M02-F02, P1 [design requirement]: close workflow and reconciliation ownership.** Define daily settlement checks and monthly/quarterly close tasks by entity: provider-to-ledger, ledger-to-bank, outstanding authorizations, reserves/customer liabilities, refunds, disputes, failed payouts, commissions, taxes, and intercompany activity where applicable. Track reconciled/unmatched/ambiguous states, evidence, owner, aging, and materiality. A manual adjustment must be a reasoned compensating entry through existing authorized services, never SQL editing a posted row. Lock approved periods against casual restatement; reopen only under a controlled policy that records prior versions and changes.

**M02-F03, P1 [design requirement]: treasury and payment authority.** Show liquidity forecast, upcoming merchant/helper obligations, protected/customer funds according to the approved legal model, reserves, settlement delays, currency exposure, and provider limits. Separate initiator, approver, and executor; require independently verified bank-account changes and limits. Ambiguous timeouts must reconcile with the provider before retrying. Payout failure, return, dispute loss, refund after payout, and negative balance must have owned recovery procedures. Employee payroll and vendor accounting should integrate with the selected accounting/payroll platforms, not use customer payout controls as a substitute.

**M02-F04, P1 [verification requirement]: tax and document lifecycle.** Establish invoice/receipt numbering, entity identity, tax evidence, retention, correction/credit-note linkage, and required contractor/merchant reporting as determined by qualified regional advisors. Do not infer tax obligations from a Country Pack or a single tax-rate field. Test exports to the accounting system for duplicate delivery, mapping errors, missing references, changed chart-of-account versions, and reconciled totals. Track document issuance failures without pretending a PDF generated locally means the legal document was successfully delivered.

### Operator design and expansion scenario

Organize the workspace by entity and close period, with a progress checklist, unreconciled balance, oldest exception, responsible reviewer, evidence completeness, and “cannot close because…” explanation. Provide a drill-through bridge from each total to authoritative entries and provider artifacts. Large exports should be asynchronous, resumable, access-controlled, and watermarked with scope/time/version. Show per-currency totals by default; the consolidated view must visibly disclose FX and eliminations. Separate payment-operation permissions from finance read-only analytics.

A regional controller closes the month while a provider webhook is delayed and a payout is returned after cutoff. A restaurant's restricted credit balance is a liability, not automatically revenue; a helper's receivable is not cash already settled. The close must record the timing difference and reopen/adjust only under policy. If two teams process the same exception, one authoritative outcome should result. A weekend treasury forecast should account for provider operating windows rather than promising instant transfers because an API call succeeded.

### Acceptance and boundaries

Finance must sign off exact report definitions and a reproducible bridge between tabs, ledger, provider, bank, and accounting platform. In isolated fixtures, demonstrate complete lifecycle and duplicate/out-of-order cases for subscription, order, job, delivery extra, credit load/transfer/refund, payout return, and dispute. Reconcile every smallest currency unit or explicitly account for approved rounding entries. A closer cannot approve their own material adjustment or modify a locked period silently. Show native-currency totals and original FX evidence. An entity A reviewer cannot access B's bank details or financial exports. Accounting/tax/provider approvals are documented business gates, not claims that code alone satisfies them.

## Management page 3. Global Service Operations, Capacity, and Safety

**Proposed workspace:** expand `/admin/operations` with scoped task workspaces and an enterprise service-control view. **Accountable owner:** COO. **Supporting owners:** delivery, restaurant operations, Direct, marketplace, rentals, consulting, support, trust/safety, and regional management.

### Existing foundation and precise gap

This is explicitly **not a proposal to create a second delivery dispatch or customer CRM system**. `client/src/pages/DeliveryDispatchPage.jsx`, `DeliveryTrackingOpsPage.jsx`, `client/src/admin/pages/Customer360.jsx`, and `Business360.jsx` already exist. The route manifest includes incident handling, extensive support operations, logistics sub-workspaces, jobs/orders/quotes, and corporate organization (`client/src/admin/adminRouteManifest.js:23-105`). Operations Overview already combines domain signals; its findings identify query amplification, scope, freshness, ownership, and actionability gaps.

The target company needs coherent command, resource planning, safe escalation, and accountable handoffs across these domains. A restaurant order can be delayed by kitchen preparation, an unavailable runner, a payment ambiguity, or a customer-address problem; a generic “pending” count does not identify who can resolve it. A delivery may use OxSteed staff or an explicitly released marketplace helper while the customer sees one authorized lifecycle. The control plane must coordinate the existing owners, not blur fulfillment source, move payments outside approvals, or overload future `/logistics` with customer-delivery semantics.

### Required workflows and controls

**M03-F01, P1 [design requirement]: accountable exception queues.** Each blocked or overdue engagement needs reason, customer-impact severity, scope, owner/team, due time, last action, allowed next actions, and links to evidence. Use explicit work-context references. Operators must claim work atomically, hand it over, and escalate under SLA. Two agents resolving the same issue must receive a state/version conflict, not silently overwrite one another. Escalation should reach the duty owner rather than always requiring the founder.

**M03-F02, P1 [design requirement]: capacity and commitment control.** Connect approved operating hours, available staff, driver/vehicle equipment, shared resource intervals, geographic coverage, capability matching, business calendars, and current demand. Do not advertise a deliverable time because a vehicle profile exists or because a marketplace helper might accept. Plans and tentative times remain distinct from commitments. Include approved fallback, backlog limits, service pause/resume, and overload rejection rules. Forecast staffing from actual workload and skills, with human review; headcount alone does not establish available qualified capacity.

**M03-F03, P1 [design requirement]: safety and incident handling.** Create controlled safety escalation for injury, threats, damaged/missing cargo, unsafe delivery access, fraud, food-quality concerns, and provider/merchant incidents. Record evidence, immediate protective actions, dispatch/customer communication, required qualified review, and restricted access. Jurisdiction-specific notification and insurance requirements must be determined by approved owners. Ordinary support staff cannot silently settle a safety incident by refunding money and deleting its history. Location tracking must have justified access, consent/retention policy, stale-location cues, and missing-data states; exact coordinates cannot become an unrestricted admin feed.

**M03-F04, P2 [design requirement]: global handover and partner quality.** Provide follow-the-sun shift handover, language/skill routing, unassigned cases, service quality trend, partner readiness, business coverage, equipment maintenance, and overdue compliance documents. Extend existing support shifts/team tools rather than duplicate rosters. Maintain operating playbooks for no-shows, late fulfillment, inaccessible addresses, outages, failed proof, returns, compensation, and customer disputes. Pause affected products/regions explicitly while keeping unrelated work operating.

### Operator design and expansion scenario

Offer role-specific workspaces with an attention queue, map only where needed, timeline, owned next actions, and scope/timezone chips. A dispatcher sees tasks they can route; a support agent sees verified customer communication and recovery paths; regional leadership sees capacity/quality without unrestricted private messages or wallet controls. Display source timestamp, predicted versus confirmed ETA, uncertainty, and “cannot verify availability” rather than fabricated precision. Related records must retain their own record-level authorization.

During a severe weather event, internal drivers become unavailable in one region and hundreds of restaurant handoffs are delayed. Operators must pause new unsupported commitments, safely release eligible deliveries to the approved pool, notify customers consistently, triage perishables, preserve base-price snapshots, and route extra-charge requests for customer approval. Cases inherited at the next shift must retain ownership and evidence. A marketplace helper cannot see or claim unreleased internal work. A regional outage must not cause a global shutdown simply because all dashboards share a counter.

### Acceptance and boundaries

Demonstrate complete journeys for jobs, Direct, rental, consulting, food/credit, and delivery, including abandonment and every permitted recovery. Under 1,500 active staff, shared assignment/claim races produce one owner and actionable conflicts; permissions remain regional and field-specific. Test overload, shift gaps, DST, unverified capacity, missing tracking, rejected proof, safe cancellation/return, and provider ambiguity. A blocked engagement always lands in an owned queue with a deadline. Customer-facing fulfillment remains source-neutral except required disclosures, and no routing action silently changes quote price, payment approval, or authoritative stock. Regional duty staff can operate for 24 hours without direct database edits or founder intervention.

## Management page 4. Reliability, Releases, Integration Recovery, and Cost

**Proposed workspace:** `/admin/platform-control`, with separate observation, operational, and emergency privileges. **Accountable owner:** CTO/SRE. **Supporting owners:** security, database, integrations, finance operations, and product engineering.

### Existing foundation and precise gap

Readiness, release metadata, audit health, operation summaries, incidents, reconciliation exceptions, credit health, durable workflow/outbox mechanisms, migration CI, backups, and recovery scripts exist. `server/services/readinessService.js:45-127` establishes initialization/schema/dependency checks. `client/src/admin/adminRouteManifest.js:23,114,118,130` records incidents and recovery-related routes. `scripts/backup-coolify-production.sh` and `verify-production-restore.sh` provide operational artifacts. The gap is **a verified, independent, governed control surface and operating procedure**, not an absence of every retry or backup capability.

The same Mac/Colima deployment hosts important production dependencies, and browser acceptance skips without configuration. Those facts make E01, E06, E07, and E08 prerequisites. An attractive health page on the failed application host is not an independently available recovery console. A successful dump, a green HTTP probe, and a connected provider record are three different kinds of evidence.

### Required workflows and controls

**M04-F01, P1 [design requirement]: service and dependency inventory.** Enumerate app/worker/database/storage/socket/queue/scanner dependencies and Stripe, maps, messaging, AI, calendar, tracker, and other providers, with owner, environment, allowed products/countries, authentication setup, capacity/quota, expiry, last successful probe, and relevant SLO. Show connected, unconfigured, unsupported, degraded, and revoked states separately. Suppress secret values; privileged credential maintenance should use the approved secret workflow, not a general admin export.

**M04-F02, P1 [design requirement]: governed recovery and replay.** Expose existing failed events, webhook results, outbox deliveries, and reconciliation runs with safe correlation references, retry history, queue age, owner, and reason. Classify replayable read-model/notification failures separately from irreversible financial, stock, or state transitions. Retry must retain original idempotency identity and reauthorize the action. Ambiguous provider outcomes require reconciliation before resending. Large replays need preview, scope/volume limits, approval, throttling, progress, cancellation, and evidence of each result. Poison events must not starve unrelated tenants.

**M04-F03, P1 [design requirement]: tested change/release control.** Record immutable artifact, commit, schema compatibility, configuration version, approvals, environment, canary outcomes, and rollback criteria. Native deployment must not outrun required CI. Rehearse migrations on redacted restored data with target-scale cardinalities, resource budgets, and representative index/lock effects. Old and new application versions must coexist safely during rollout. Configuration changes need staged validation, before/after effect estimates, and rollback rules; restoring an old app cannot erase committed financial transactions.

**M04-F04, P1 [verification requirement]: independent disaster recovery and resource budgets.** Record backup scope, encryption/access separation, retained recovery points, copy location, restore test evidence, recovery objectives, and operator readiness. Restore database, media, mappings, and configuration on a separate environment; reconcile external provider state before resuming jobs. Track database connection/lock/query load, disk/WAL growth, object retention, websocket pressure, worker lag, vendor quota/spend, and AI budget. A daily spend anomaly must have a kill/degrade option that preserves customer commitments and evidence.

### Operator design and expansion scenario

Use a service map and severity-sorted attention queue, not a wall of green icons. Link every symptom to affected business journeys and data timestamps. On-call operators need a precise runbook, safe command preview, emergency contact, and change record. High-risk restart/freeze/replay operations must identify scope, expected customer effect, prerequisites, independent approval, and post-checks. Read-only staff can observe without obtaining production secrets or write permissions. Export incident evidence with redaction and immutable release identifiers.

Suppose a host fails while Stripe has captured money but the app has not recorded its webhook, and the customer has uploaded proof to local storage. A restore that brings back only SQL can leave missing proof; replaying every payment request can charge twice. The recovery plan must reconstruct safe durable state, reconcile provider outcomes, recover media, verify authorization, and selectively resume workers. If an administrator loses the application itself, the independent incident channel and infrastructure access must still work. Mass customer reconnect must be capacity-tested rather than treated as normal steady traffic.

### Acceptance and boundaries

Perform a two-day minimum steady/peak soak plus controlled host/node/provider/queue/disk/credential-expiry tests before target-scale launch; extend duration according to traffic and periodic job cycles. Recover from a fresh independent host within the approved RTO/RPO, with audited money/stock/message/proof checks. Record one safe replay of each consumer class, one refused unsafe replay, and one provider ambiguity resolved without duplication. A deliberately failing release cannot promote. Mandatory browser tests cannot silently skip. Finance/security approve action thresholds; SRE records verified capacity and runbooks. Continuing synthetic checks and restore exercises remain necessary after launch.

## Management page 5. Market Governance, Privacy, Contracts, and Policy Assurance

**Proposed workspace:** `/admin/governance`, combining controlled views over existing domain approvals. **Accountable owners:** Legal/Compliance, Privacy, Security, and regional business leadership. **Supporting owners:** finance, trust/safety, HR, logistics, food operations, and product.

### Existing foundation and precise gap

Legal acceptances, legal audit/metrics/privacy-request routes, country packs, regions, internal approvals, business modes, content-removal/moderation, credit compliance/partners, and logistics compliance review exist. See `client/src/admin/adminRouteManifest.js:70-73,98-99,111-123,134-148` and `server/services/complianceCaseService.js`. Those are actual starting points, not missing modules. The enterprise gap is a cohesive evidence and policy lifecycle governing which legal entity can offer which product in which market, who approved it, and how privacy/contract obligations are fulfilled across all authoritative stores and providers.

The mere presence of a country configuration, merchant agreement, payment currency, or untranslated page cannot be treated as a worldwide launch approval. Stored-value products, cards, restaurant wallets, delivery coverage, contractor arrangements, marketing consent, and data processing can each have different restrictions. This report does not determine jurisdiction-specific legal obligations; qualified owners must document them and engineering must enforce their decisions.

### Required workflows and controls

**M05-F01, P1 [design requirement]: market/product readiness register.** Record entity, jurisdiction/service area, supported products, provider approvals, required agreements, approved policy version, insurance/operational coverage, settlement capability, languages, escalation contacts, responsible approvers, expiry, and evidence. Model disabled, preparation, approved, limited pilot, suspended, and retired states. Approval is scoped; enabling restaurant ordering must not automatically enable unrestricted credit, freight, external cards, or cross-border delivery. Domain APIs enforce the approved capability set, not just hide a navigation link.

**M05-F02, P1 [design requirement]: privacy request and retention orchestration.** Extend existing privacy workflows with identity verification, explicit data inventory, scoped retrieval, time-bound export, deletion/anonymization, retention exceptions, legal holds, provider propagation, and completion evidence. Financial/audit obligations may require retaining minimal evidence while removing ordinary profile data; approved policy decides how. Include media, chats, logs, search histories, trackers, marketing tools, caches, analytical stores, and backups according to realistic deletion/recovery rules. A deletion request is not complete merely because the Users table changed.

**M05-F03, P1 [design requirement]: agreement and policy lifecycle.** Track document type, contracting parties, version, effective/expiry dates, country/language, signature/acceptance evidence, covered product, renewals, approved variations, and fulfillment obligations. Policy changes need impact analysis and a communication/reacceptance plan where required. Historical engagements retain their applicable version. Provider, franchise, business, consultant, employee, and customer agreements are different controls. Use an approved contract/document platform where practical, with explicit links rather than a parallel file store hidden in an admin note.

**M05-F04, P1 [verification requirement]: control assurance and sensitive investigations.** Link compliance cases, fraud/safety/security incidents, payment disputes, content appeals, and regulatory inquiries through authorized references. Assign owners and deadlines, retain evidence under access restrictions, and protect against a user reviewing their own case. Require review of sensitive exports and bulk decisions. Keep autonomous AI away from final legal, employment, financial, or access decisions unless separately approved and bounded. Audit not only changed records but sensitive reads, attempted prohibited actions, evidence exports, and emergency access.

### Operator design and expansion scenario

Provide market/product readiness matrices, expiry queues, privacy case timelines, contract obligations, outstanding policy approvals, control-test evidence, and exceptions with accountable owners. Use plain-language blockers: “delivery paused: required coverage expired” or “credit unavailable: provider approval pending,” not a global boolean that implies every feature is ready. Sensitive information should be masked until a verified task needs it. Display applicable country/entity/language and the historic policy basis on decisions. Bulk renewal or policy migration needs previews and resumable per-record outcomes.

A country launch is approved for discovery and domestic delivery but not the credit rail. A merchant's coverage expires, a user requests erasure while a dispute is open, and a regional regulator asks for selected records. Operators must pause only affected capabilities, retain required minimal dispute evidence under a documented hold, safely export authorized records, and avoid leaking private conversations to unrelated reviewers. When the coverage issue is resolved, resumption needs a new approved record, not merely editing the visible badge. The founder should not be the only person who knows which promises are permitted.

### Acceptance and boundaries

Demonstrate server rejection for an unapproved market/product/provider combination, including direct API and delayed-job attempts. Run a verified privacy case across representative DB/media/log/provider stores and prove completion or explicitly justified retention exceptions. Test policy changes, expiry, suspension, appeal, duplicate request, identity mismatch, provider failure, and recovery from backup without resurrecting prohibited active data. A contract/history view must preserve the originally applicable version. Governance review must be signed by qualified business/legal/security owners with expiration and evidence; code tests alone cannot certify legal compliance.

# Additional functionality backlog and build-versus-integrate decisions

Prioritize capabilities by business commitments and risk, not how many tabs can be added. Each item below is a **requirement to assess/extend**, not a verified claim that every supporting function is absent.

- **Enterprise customer/partner management:** extend Customer 360/Business 360 with account ownership, commitments, contract scope, case history, health, renewal, escalation, and field-level access. Integrate a CRM if sales account management exceeds the existing operational record model.
- **Fraud/risk investigation:** linked identity/device/payment/order/credit/delivery signals, evidence provenance, rule version, reviewed outcomes, appeals, and false-positive monitoring. Rules need accountable owners and safe rollback; do not use opaque risk scores as sole authority for irreversible action.
- **Merchant/helper/provider readiness:** onboarding checklist, restricted versus ready capabilities, payout prerequisites, expiring verification/coverage, remediation, and independently verified provider state. Extend Stripe setup and partner controls; a connected account is not automatically payout-ready.
- **Inventory/assets/maintenance:** driver equipment, Direct-owned tools, peer rentals, spare stock, custodians, inspections, maintenance, damage/loss, and retirement. Preserve ownership distinctions and authoritative inventory movements.
- **Commercial procurement/vendor lifecycle:** sourcing, supplier assessment, approvals, purchase commitments, receiving, invoices, disputes, and renewals. Integrate an ERP/procurement platform rather than stretching simple customer `/delivery` into unsupported freight/procurement.
- **Subscription/entitlement operations:** invoice failure, retry/grace rules, plan changes, proration, refund, cancellation, provider outage, duplicate webhook, and access revocation. Effective entitlements must derive from verified domain/provider state, not displayed catalog prices alone.
- **Business continuity/customer communication:** approved outage templates, service-area notices, consent-aware channels, message version, delivery outcome, localization, and incident follow-ups. Extend existing templates/messaging and prevent duplicate campaigns after worker retries.
- **Data/metrics catalog:** approved definitions, source lineage, freshness, access, retention, quality checks, entity/currency semantics, and consistent export totals. Keep analytical warehouses out of synchronous financial authorization.
- **Experiment and configuration governance:** audience/scope validation, safe defaults, staged rollout, monitoring, immutable versions, expiry, owner, and controlled rollback. A feature flag count is not evidence that exposure is safe.
- **AI oversight and cost:** provider/model version, evaluated use case, data minimization, human review, abuse limits, spend quotas, fallback, prompt-injection tests, and incident controls. Free-model availability is not an enterprise capacity guarantee.
- **Workforce scheduling and training:** qualifications, region/language, shift capacity, handover, required training, performance review, and QA. Integrate payroll/HR rather than calculate employee salaries with marketplace payout logic.
- **Document/evidence operations:** malware validation, storage policy, object ownership, expiry, redacted exports, signature/receipt provenance, independent restoration, and legal holds. A successful upload or scanned file is not proof of delivery validity by itself.
- **Mobile/offline operational reliability:** durable pending commands, idempotency, conflict resolution, stale GPS, battery/network loss, lost devices, permission withdrawal, and accessible status. Staff must distinguish queued changes from committed milestones.
- **Integration/partner API governance:** scoped credentials, rate limits, quotas, owner, version/deprecation policy, consent, replay/revocation, observability, and sandbox certification. A working connector key alone is not complete governance.
- **Franchise/enterprise partner assurance:** territory contracts, role delegation, brand standards, settlement obligations, controlled reporting, inspection exceptions, suspension/appeal, and renewals. Extend Franchise HQ with enforceable domain boundaries rather than trusting its map alone.

# Verification contract: how the report becomes an operable company

## Every-tab acceptance template

For **each of the 65 numbered tab reviews**, maintain an executable test record containing its manifest key, list/detail routes, data sources, scopes, sensitive fields, every visible action, allowed state transitions, and denial conditions. Cover authorized read; unauthorized HTTP/direct navigation/export/socket; valid action; invalid input; stale version; simultaneous operator conflict; duplicate request; provider timeout; unavailable dependency; recover/retry; audit evidence; and post-action authoritative refresh. Dependent detail pages and hidden workspaces are included, not just sidebar URLs. A missing test environment is a failed release prerequisite, not a pass.

Exercise roles: super-admin, global specialist admin, country/regional admin, entity/restaurant-scoped staff, support agent, dispatcher, internal driver, marketplace helper, business owner/manager/member, customer, guest, suspended/revoked/expired-grant user, and service account. Test all five UI languages with actual application language selection, Arabic RTL, light/dark themes, mobile and desktop, keyboard and screen readers, reduced motion and animation fallback. Browser locale alone may not change a saved application preference. Use Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit for critical workflows, and document any consciously unsupported environment.

Minimum isolated end-to-end scenarios: customer request to quote to approval to scheduled work to completion to receipt/payment to support; food discovery to customization to stock reservation to order/payment to kitchen/runner handoff to receipt/refund; restaurant credit request/load/QR/transfer/history and failure recovery; broader credit authorization/hold/settlement/refund/dispute/reconciliation with separate balances; delivery internal assignment or marketplace release/claim to pickup/transit/proof/delivery/extra approval/receipt/return; rental pickup/return/damage; consulting intake/session/invoice/recovery; subscription change/cancel/payment failure/entitlement; account recovery/suspension/deletion/appeal; access joiner/mover/leaver; regional launch pause/resume; incident/outage/restore/reconciliation. The relevant authoritative service decides the outcome in each case.

## Data, concurrency, and recovery testing

Generate six million synthetic users and realistic relationships/history without copying raw production personal data. Model hot tenants, popular restaurants/helpers, old disputes, large wallet histories, multi-currency/entity records, high audit volumes, rare unsupported statuses, incomplete legacy data, and unusual timezones. Confirm fresh migrations already passing, then add redacted restored-data rehearsal, migration interruption/restart, mixed app versions, forward/rollback compatibility, long-lock budgets, and independent restore. Verify index/query plans and cost at representative scales rather than extrapolating from four production users.

Run concurrent operator/customer races for every scarce or financial resource: delivery ownership, helper claims, driver/vehicle windows, booking slots, stock, quote acceptance, credit holds/limits, payouts, approval checker identity, and case assignment. Crash between provider success and local commit; deliver webhooks twice and out of order; expire a worker lease; reconnect clients to another node; revoke an operator while an export/action is queued. Assert no duplicate financial/stock effect, no stale-state overwrite, no cross-scope leakage, no fabricated proof, and a recoverable owned exception.

Benchmark the declared capacity model with workload distribution, hardware/topology, latency percentiles, error rates, queue growth, DB waits, provider quotas, memory, CPU, socket reconnect, and storage growth published. Include at least 48 hours of soak and periodic jobs; longer validation may be needed to exercise weekly/monthly cycles. A short peak test does not certify the future company. Resilience tests should lose an independent compute node and provider, not destructively kill the sole production database with customers active.
