## Release and operating gates

**Gate A - Integrity/security:** all P0 and applicable P1 ownership, payment, proof, authorization, privacy, and idempotency issues closed with tests. **Gate B - Scope/operability:** every current tab and supporting workflow has approved specialist ownership, server scope, failure handling, and a trained recovery path. **Gate C - Capacity/continuity:** measured workload targets, independent failure domains, restored-data migration proof, and independent disaster restore meet agreed objectives. **Gate D - Market/business:** finance/legal/provider/operations approvals are complete for the actual products and countries being launched. **Gate E - Release evidence:** mandatory CI/browser scenarios run without silent skips; exact tested artifact/configuration/schema is promoted; canary and rollback are exercised.

Roll out in bounded markets and traffic tiers. Validate at current demand first, then agreed expansion thresholds (for example 10%, 25%, 50%, 100% of the design peak), with stop conditions for integrity failures, sustained lag, scope leaks, or recovery-objective violations. Pilot limits are business exposure limits, not permission to bypass correctness. Publish queue/service freshness and periodic access reviews, restore exercises, dependency-expiry checks, metric review, and capacity reassessment. These ongoing controls are what prevent the same class of issue from returning unnoticed.

# Ordered delivery program and closure evidence

**Stage 1: establish truth and safe gates.** Confirm source/deployed identity, mandatory staging/browser execution, domain route/action coverage, defect reproductions, shared permission boundaries, and complete evidence inventory. Owners: engineering lead, QA, Security, SRE. Outputs: verified backlog with finding IDs, scoped test accounts, release manifest, and reproducible failures. Do not expand permissions just to make tests pass.

**Stage 2: correct irreversible and sensitive workflows.** Close money, credit, assignment, proof, state/version, privacy, and access lifecycle issues before visual polish. Preserve authoritative ledgers and recovery histories. Owners: domain engineering with finance/risk/security reviewers. Outputs: negative/concurrency/provider-ambiguity evidence, approval rules, and runbooks. A business owner signs that failure cases can be resolved without SQL or founder intervention.

**Stage 3: build resilient shared infrastructure and read models.** Separate workloads, add tested shared realtime/presence, worker ownership/fencing, DB budgets/query isolation, independent backup/restore, promotion controls, scoped aggregates, and operational SLOs. Owners: SRE/database/platform/data teams. Outputs: capacity results, independent recovery evidence, restore-point records, and safe release drills. Avoid a premature architecture rewrite of every domain.

**Stage 4: complete enterprise management and operator design.** Integrate HR/identity, accounting/treasury, global service control, reliability, and governance workspaces described above. Apply consistent accessible grids, task-based navigation, status clarity, translation QA, and owned exceptions to all tabs. Owners: business domain heads and admin UX. Outputs: usability evidence, role-specific training, handover procedures, finance/legal approvals, and closed per-tab acceptance records.

**Stage 5: certify the defined deployment envelope.** Independently review security and finance controls; run complete journeys, restores, regional scope tests, long-running load/resilience checks, and limited-market pilots. Owners: accountable executives, QA, Security, SRE, Finance, Legal/Compliance. Outputs: an explicit approved capacity/market/product envelope, remaining accepted P2/P3 risks, expiration/review dates, and signed launch decision. Do not describe unresolved P1 blockers as completed enhancements.

Each finding's closure record must include source revision, exact behavior changed, test/trace evidence, migration/configuration dependency, rollout state, rollback/recovery path, business owner approval, and any residual exposure. A merge or passing unit test alone does not close an operational risk. An accepted deferral needs a bounded customer/employee/market exposure, compensating controls, accountable approver, and review date. No report can honestly promise the business will never encounter a new scenario; this program provides the controls to identify and handle new scenarios without unsafe improvisation.

## Final certification decision

**Healthy observed deployment: yes. Current CI success: yes. Fresh migration runner repeatability: yes. All admin business actions executed and verified: no. Six-million-user capacity demonstrated: no. Worldwide workforce/financial/market readiness certified: no.**

The business can expand only within a verified operating envelope. The path to the requested full-company capability is the per-tab remediation, shared enterprise controls, five management workspaces, and measurable gates above. Until those gates are satisfied, neither this report nor the present green deployment should be represented to employees, customers, partners, or investors as proof that every operational, financial, legal, or failure scenario is covered.
